| Document Type | scope_srs |

| Last Updated | 2026-05-19 |

## 1. Scope Overview

이 문서는 SpecKiwi를 사용하는 개발자와 AI coding agent의 구현 전후 workflow, release readiness, CI validation, baseline 운영을 정의한다.

## 2. Scope Boundaries

### In Scope

- 구현 전 SRS 탐색
- 구현 후 evidence 갱신
- release readiness summary
- CI validation 예시
- AGENTS.md 및 CLAUDE.md instruction
- baseline tag 운영

### Out of Scope

- 프로젝트 관리 도구 연동 자동화
- issue 자동 생성
- PR 자동 생성
- 배포 자동화

## 3. Assumptions and Constraints

- SRS는 Git에 저장된다.
- release 판단은 target/status/evidence summary를 기준으로 한다.
- agent는 CLI 또는 MCP를 통해 SRS를 사용할 수 있다.

## 4. Requirements

### FR-FLOW-001 — 구현 전 요구사항 탐색 workflow

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v1.0.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | workflow, agent, implementation |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | stable |
| Verification Method | inspection |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi는 구현자가 `00.index.md`에서 scope를 찾고 Requirement Block을 읽은 뒤 구현하도록 workflow를 정의해야 한다.

#### Rationale

요구사항과 무관한 구현을 줄이려면 작업 시작점이 명확해야 한다.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: AGENTS.md가 구현 전 SRS 탐색 순서를 포함한다.
- [x] AC-2: README가 CLI로 요구사항을 찾는 예를 포함한다.
- [x] AC-3: MCP tool 목록이 agent 사용 흐름을 포함한다.
- [x] AC-4: workflow는 `00.index.md`의 `Active Target`을 현재 작업 목표 버전으로 사용하도록 설명한다.

### FR-FLOW-005 — Agent instruction 생성

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v1.0.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | agents-md, claude-md, instructions |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | stable |
| Verification Method | inspection |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi는 AGENTS.md와 CLAUDE.md에 넣을 수 있는 versioned SRS-enforcement agent instruction을 제공해야 한다.

#### Rationale

AI coding agent는 SRS-MD 구조와 CLI/MCP 사용 규칙을 명확히 알아야 한다.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: 문서에 `# SpecKiwi SRS 워크플로 v1.3` heading과 `<!-- /SpecKiwi SRS 워크플로 -->` suffix marker가 포함된 AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md 추가 문장이 포함된다.
- [x] AC-2: `speckiwi init`이 해당 문장을 `AGENTS.md`와 `CLAUDE.md`에 생성, append, 또는 update한다.
- [x] AC-3: agent instruction은 `docs/spec/`을 필수 source of truth로 선언하고, 작업 전 `docs/spec/00.index.md` 조회, Requirement ID 확인, 작업 요약의 Requirement ID 언급을 요구한다.
- [x] AC-4: 일치하는 Requirement ID가 없으면 agent가 구현을 중단하고 SRS 요구사항 생성 또는 수정을 사용자에게 물어야 한다고 명시한다.
- [x] AC-5: updater는 `# SpecKiwi SRS 워크플로 v{version}` heading과 suffix marker로 managed block 범위를 찾고, 현재 버전과 다르면 전체 block을 교체한다.
- [x] AC-6: legacy unversioned `# SpecKiwi SRS workflow` block은 outdated managed block으로 보고 현재 versioned block으로 교체한다.
- [x] AC-7: versioned heading이 suffix marker 없이 존재하면 안전한 managed block으로 보지 않고 기존 text를 보존한 뒤 현재 block을 append한다. suffix marker는 다음 top-level Markdown heading 전에 나오는 경우에만 해당 heading의 marker로 인정한다.
- [x] AC-8: agent instruction은 Active Target, in-progress/blocked/implemented requirements, missing evidence, Completed Work Log를 함께 읽는 current work status workflow를 포함한다.
- [x] AC-9: agent instruction은 behavior change에 대해 실패 자동 테스트를 먼저 작성하는 TDD 원칙과 자동 테스트 예외 처리 절차를 포함한다.

### FR-FLOW-006 — current work status agent workflow

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v1.1.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | agents, workflow, active-target, completed-work |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | stable |
| Verification Method | test, inspection |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi agent instructions must define how coding agents answer current work status requests using Active Target, requirement status, missing evidence, and Completed Work Log.

#### Rationale

사용자가 "현재 작업 진행중인 목록과 상태"를 물으면 agent는 임의 추측 대신 SRS index와 Requirement Blocks에서 상태를 조합해야 한다.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: workflow는 MCP `get_active_target` 또는 CLI `speckiwi active-target --json`으로 Active Target을 먼저 읽도록 요구한다.
- [x] AC-2: Active Target이 비어 있으면 no active target 상태를 보고하고 target-scoped 변경 전 사용자 확인을 요구한다.
- [x] AC-3: workflow는 `in_progress`, `blocked`, `implemented` 요구사항을 조회하도록 요구한다.
- [x] AC-4: workflow는 summary 또는 `summarize_target`의 `missingEvidence`를 확인하도록 요구한다.
- [x] AC-5: workflow는 MCP `list_completed_work` 또는 CLI `speckiwi completed-work --json`으로 Completed Work Log를 읽도록 요구한다.
- [x] AC-6: Completed Work Log가 source of truth가 아니며 Requirement Block status/evidence가 우선임을 명시한다.

- The workflow keeps Completed Work Log as a summary and uses Requirement Blocks for final completion decisions.

#### Implementation Notes

### FR-FLOW-007 — release readiness core contract

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v1.2.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | release, readiness, governance |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test, inspection |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/06.hardening-governance-research.md](../research/06.hardening-governance-research.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi release readiness must be a core workflow contract that resolves target safely, reports blocking findings, and serves as the single source of truth for release scripts and CI.

#### Rationale

Release readiness cannot be only a script wrapper; it must prove whether the selected target is ready and explain why it is blocked.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Release readiness uses an explicit target when provided; otherwise it uses the `Active Target` metadata value.
- [x] AC-2: If no explicit target exists and `Active Target` is empty, release readiness returns `ready=false` with a blocking reason.
- [x] AC-3: The summary includes `target`, `targetSource`, `ready`, `diagnosticsSummary`, `blocked`, `plannedOrInProgress`, `implementedNotVerified`, `criticalHighUnverified`, `missingEvidence`, `acCoverageGaps`, `missingEvidenceReferences`, `brokenTraceLinks`, `warnings`, and `baselineCommand` fields.
- [x] AC-4: Release check scripts and CI call the core release-readiness workflow rather than duplicating target readiness logic.
- [x] AC-5: Release readiness treats target status and validation diagnostics consistently with SRS target status rules.
- [x] AC-6: Release readiness exposes `commandEvidencePolicyViolations` in the result and populates it from the FR-FLOW-008 command evidence policy gate.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | command | npm run build; npm run typecheck; npm run lint; npm test; npm run test:integration; npm run release:acceptance; npm run perf:srs | all | P9 bash verification passed on 2026-05-10 |
| VE-2 | command | npm run release:check | all | P9 release readiness passed on 2026-05-10 |

#### Trace Links

| --- | --- | --- | --- |

| Requirement | OPS-NODE-002 | related_to | CI must execute release readiness gates. |

### FR-FLOW-008 — evidence and AC coverage release gate

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v1.2.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | release, evidence, traceability |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test, inspection |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/06.hardening-governance-research.md](../research/06.hardening-governance-research.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi release readiness must verify evidence existence and Acceptance Criteria coverage for every verified requirement across `docs/spec`.

#### Rationale

The `verified` status should mean the tool can prove AC-level coverage and evidence reference validity, not merely that a requirement has at least one evidence row.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Every `verified` requirement has all Acceptance Criteria checked.
- [x] AC-2: Every checked AC is covered by evidence with `Covers = all` or an explicit matching AC identifier.
- [x] AC-3: Local evidence references resolve under the project root or are reported as missing evidence references.
- [x] AC-4: URL evidence references are syntactically valid or reported as invalid evidence references.
- [x] AC-5: Command evidence references are classified as command evidence and checked against the release policy before a requirement can contribute to release readiness.
- [x] AC-6: Requirement trace links resolve to existing requirement IDs or are reported as broken trace links.
- [x] AC-7: Release readiness reports AC coverage gaps, missing evidence references, broken trace links, and command evidence policy violations separately from generic validation diagnostics.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | command | npm run build; npm run typecheck; npm run lint; npm test; npm run test:integration; npm run release:acceptance; npm run perf:srs | all | P9 bash verification passed on 2026-05-10 |
| VE-2 | command | npm run release:check | all | P9 release readiness passed on 2026-05-10 |

#### Trace Links

| Requirement | FR-FLOW-007 | depends_on | Release readiness result must expose the detailed gate outputs. |
| Requirement | FR-PARSE-006 | depends_on | Evidence parser provides `Covers` values. |
| Requirement | FR-PARSE-007 | depends_on | Trace link parser provides requirement references. |

#### Research / Analysis

- [SpecKiwi Hardening and Governance Research](../research/06.hardening-governance-research.md) sections 5.2 and 7.2.

#### Implementation Notes

- Command evidence classification and policy violations are part of the v1.2.0 P0 release gate. A command evidence row must either satisfy the documented policy or appear in `commandEvidencePolicyViolations`.
- The release gate fixture set should include stale evidence, broken local path evidence, invalid URL evidence, command evidence policy violation, AC coverage gap, and broken trace link cases.

### FR-FLOW-009 — canonical root agent instruction files

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v1.2.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | agents, init, governance |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test, inspection |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/06.hardening-governance-research.md](../research/06.hardening-governance-research.md), [docs/rule/SRS-MD-Rules-v2.5.0.md](../rule/SRS-MD-Rules-v2.5.0.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi repositories must provide canonical root `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` managed instruction blocks, and init must upsert the current managed block deterministically.

#### Rationale

Many coding agents discover uppercase root instruction files; relying only on lowercase or stale agent files weakens SRS workflow enforcement.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Repository hardening ensures root `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` exist with the current SpecKiwi managed block.
- [x] AC-2: `speckiwi init` and MCP startup init upsert the managed block by versioned heading and suffix marker, not by skipping when a rules link exists.
- [x] AC-3: Existing legacy unversioned or older managed blocks are replaced with the current managed block without removing unrelated user content.
- [x] AC-4: Lowercase `agents.md` is either mirrored intentionally or documented as non-canonical.
- [x] AC-5: Tests or inspection verify managed block presence and replacement behavior.

### FR-FLOW-010 — stability-aware agent and release workflow

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v1.3.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | workflow, agent, release, stability |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test, inspection |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/07.requirement-stability-lifecycle-research.md](../research/07.requirement-stability-lifecycle-research.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi must expose requirement `Stability` in agent current-work workflows, target summaries, and release readiness so humans and agents can distinguish draft requirements from implementation-ready, frozen, and deprecated requirements.

#### Rationale

Coding agents need more than implementation `Status` to decide whether work can proceed. A `planned` requirement can still be too unstable to implement, and a `verified` requirement should not remain `draft`.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Current work status workflow reports active target plus `Status` and `Stability` counts for target-scoped requirements.
- [x] AC-2: Agents must stop before implementing non-discarded requirements with `Stability=draft` or `Stability=deprecated` unless the user explicitly overrides the workflow.
- [x] AC-3: Target summary and release readiness expose stability blockers or warnings separately from status blockers.
- [x] AC-4: Release readiness blocks release of a target that still contains non-discarded `draft` requirements.
- [x] AC-5: Managed `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` instructions explain `Status` versus `Stability` and the required behavior for `draft` requirements.
- [x] AC-6: CLI and MCP JSON outputs for active target, target summary, and current work status include enough stability data for agents to answer status questions without scanning raw Markdown.
- [x] AC-7: Completed Work Log remains a summary only; `Stability` does not replace Requirement Block `Status`, Acceptance Criteria, Verification Evidence, or Change Notes as the source of truth.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

| VE-2 | command | npm run release:acceptance | AC-3, AC-4, AC-7 | 2026-05-11 release acceptance suite passed: release readiness stability behavior covered. |

| Requirement | FR-FLOW-007 | depends_on | Release readiness must expose stability blockers. |
| Requirement | FR-PARSE-015 | depends_on | Parser and validator define the stability taxonomy. |

#### Research / Analysis

- [Requirement Stability Lifecycle Research](../research/07.requirement-stability-lifecycle-research.md)

#### Implementation Notes

- The managed agent instruction block version must be bumped when stability guidance is added.
- JSON payloads should preserve backward compatibility by adding fields rather than replacing existing status fields.

### FR-FLOW-011 — next target authoring workflow

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.1.0 |
| Status | implemented |

| Verification Method | test, inspection |
| GitHub Issue | - |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi agent instructions must define how agents handle a user request such as "set the next target to 2.1.0" and ensure subsequent SRS authoring uses that Active Target by default.

#### Rationale

Active Target is useful only if agents consistently consult it before target-scoped SRS changes. The workflow must prevent agents from writing new requirements to stale targets after the user selects a new goal version.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Agent workflow reads Active Target before creating or updating target-scoped requirements.
- [x] AC-2: When the user asks to set a new target that is not registered, the workflow uses MCP `set_active_target` with creation enabled or CLI `speckiwi set-active-target <target> --create`.
- [x] AC-3: After target assignment, agents confirm the resolved Active Target through MCP `get_active_target` or CLI `speckiwi active-target --json`.
- [x] AC-4: Subsequent SRS creation uses the resolved Active Target unless the user explicitly supplies a different target.
- [x] AC-5: If Active Target is empty and no explicit target exists, agents stop and ask for the target before making target-scoped SRS changes.
- [x] AC-6: Managed `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` instructions document the next-target workflow and the difference between explicit target and Active Target defaulting.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

| Requirement | FR-MCP-015 | supports | MCP-first workflow implements target creation and Active Target defaulting. |
| Requirement | FR-NODE-014 | depends_on | Core services provide deterministic target lifecycle behavior. |
| Requirement | FR-FLOW-006 | depends_on | Current work status workflow already starts from Active Target. |

#### Research / Analysis

- Current managed instructions require reading Active Target, but they do not yet define target creation or defaulted authoring after a "next target" request.

#### Implementation Notes

- The managed agent instruction block version must be bumped when this workflow text is added.
- The workflow should prefer MCP and use CLI only when MCP is unavailable.
- The phrase "next target" should map to Active Target, not to Product Version.

### MIG-FLOW-001 — Claude Kiwi skills to Codex skills migration

#### Requirement

The skills workspace must migrate every Claude Kiwi skill and shared Kiwi resource under `skills/claude/` into Codex-compatible skill assets under `skills/codex/`, while preserving the Claude source tree and producing a migration plan before rewriting copied skill instructions.

#### Rationale

The repository now contains Claude-oriented Kiwi skills, but Codex skill discovery requires Codex-compatible `SKILL.md` anatomy, metadata, path conventions, and tool terminology. A governed migration requirement prevents ad hoc copying and keeps the Claude source assets intact as the migration baseline.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The migration inventory enumerates every source skill folder and shared Kiwi resource under `skills/claude/` before copying.
- [x] AC-2: The `skills/codex/` tree contains copied counterparts for all source skill folders and shared Kiwi resources, and the original `skills/claude/` files remain unchanged unless separately requested.
- [x] AC-3: Each migrated Codex skill folder contains a required `SKILL.md` whose YAML frontmatter contains only `name` and `description`, and the folder name matches the skill name.
- [x] AC-4: Claude-specific commands, absolute Claude skill paths, tool names, model-role references, and invocation syntax are rewritten or isolated so the Codex-facing instructions use Codex-compatible terminology and available tools.
- [x] AC-5: Shared references, scripts, and relative links used by migrated skills resolve within `skills/codex/` after migration.
- [x] AC-6: A migration plan records the per-skill transformation approach, validation commands, risks, and deferred manual decisions before bulk rewriting copied skill bodies.
- [x] AC-7: Verification includes an inventory diff, UTF-8 read/write handling, Codex skill validation, and sub-agent reviewer validation before the migration is declared complete.

### FR-FLOW-012 — Kiwi skills require SpecKiwi MCP for normal operation

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.2.3 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | skills, kiwi, mcp, workflow, local-llm |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | inspection, validation |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi must require every repository-distributed Kiwi skill variant, including Codex skills and OpenCode/Hermes local-LLM `skills/etc` variants, to treat a working `speckiwi mcp` connection as the required normal-operation interface for SRS reads, mutations, validation, status and stability changes, acceptance-criteria updates, evidence, trace links, and completed-work logging.

#### Rationale

Kiwi skills depend on SpecKiwi SRS governance. Bypassing MCP for raw Markdown edits would weaken ID generation, validation, target summaries, status and stability lifecycle checks, evidence linkage, and completed-work consistency. Local LLM variants are especially likely to drift without a structured mutation interface, so MCP must be a hard operational dependency rather than an optional convenience.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Every Kiwi skill variant documents that normal target-scoped SRS or implementation workflows require available SpecKiwi MCP tools before proceeding.
- [x] AC-2: If SpecKiwi MCP is unavailable, a Kiwi skill must not perform SRS mutations and must halt with remediation guidance or restrict itself to MCP setup diagnostics.
- [x] AC-3: CLI fallback is allowed only for preflight diagnostics, bootstrap assistance, or MCP remediation guidance, not as the normal SRS mutation path for Kiwi skills.
- [x] AC-4: Kiwi skill instructions must not direct agents to manually edit `docs/spec/**` as the normal path for requirement, acceptance-criteria, evidence, trace, status, stability, or completed-work changes.
- [x] AC-5: OpenCode/Hermes local-LLM `skills/etc` variants inherit the same MCP requirement even when their execution profile defaults to `--max` and single-subagent operation.
- [x] AC-6: Migration or install validation must include inspection or scanner evidence that migrated Kiwi skills carry the MCP dependency rule and do not contain normal-operation raw Markdown mutation guidance.

| VE-5 | subagent-review | Locke subagent review 019e40a3-c101-7b12-a10e-3739af9385e7 on 2026-05-20 | FR-FLOW-012 AC-1 AC-2 AC-3 AC-4 AC-5 AC-6 | - |

- The existing Codex migration requirement `MIG-FLOW-001` validated skill anatomy and terminology, but it did not elevate `speckiwi mcp` from preferred interface to mandatory runtime dependency. The v2.2.3 target goal and subsequent user clarification make MCP availability a product-level skill workflow constraint.

#### Implementation Notes

- Skill bodies and shared Kiwi references should centralize MCP preflight wording. Installer or migration validation should scan every generated `SKILL.md` and relevant shared reference for the MCP requirement. CLI-only fallback may help users start or diagnose `speckiwi mcp`, but should not be documented as a normal replacement for MCP mutations.

### MIG-FLOW-002 — Kiwi etc skills migration for OpenCode and Hermes local LLMs

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | migration |
| Target | v2.2.3 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | skills, migration, etc, opencode, hermes, local-llm, kiwi |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | inspection, validation |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The skills workspace must migrate the Kiwi skill set and shared Kiwi resources into a repository-local `skills/etc` tree for OpenCode and Hermes local-LLM usage, preserving existing Codex and Claude source trees while producing a migration plan before rewriting copied skill instructions.

#### Rationale

OpenCode and Hermes can consume Agent Skills, but their runtime assumptions differ from Codex. Local LLM usage also changes the cost and quality tradeoff: default Max-style evaluation remains useful, but multi-subagent fanout is inappropriate. A dedicated `skills/etc` migration keeps Codex and Claude assets stable while creating a local-LLM-oriented source tree with explicit MCP dependency and validation gates.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The migration inventory enumerates every Kiwi source skill folder and shared Kiwi resource considered for `skills/etc`, and records whether each item is copied from `skills/claude`, `skills/codex`, or another documented baseline.
- [x] AC-2: The `skills/etc` tree contains migrated counterparts for every selected Kiwi skill folder and shared Kiwi resource, and existing `skills/claude` and `skills/codex` files remain unchanged unless a separate requirement explicitly owns that change.
- [x] AC-3: Each migrated etc skill folder contains a required `SKILL.md` whose frontmatter is compatible with the open Agent Skills format, includes at least `name` and `description`, and whose `name` matches the containing folder.
- [x] AC-4: OpenCode/Hermes/local-LLM variants remove or isolate Codex-only and Claude-only tool syntax, absolute paths, provider names, and invocation wording so the instructions are suitable for OpenCode or Hermes execution contexts.
- [x] AC-5: Every migrated etc skill documents that normal operation requires `speckiwi mcp`; if MCP is unavailable, the skill must halt or provide remediation guidance rather than performing normal SRS mutations.
- [x] AC-6: Every migrated etc skill uses the local-LLM execution profile: `--max` is the default, multi-subagent fanout is disabled, and only one subagent or one evaluator worker is used at a time when delegation is available.
- [x] AC-7: The evaluation and improvement loop for etc skills terminates only after the applicable evaluator set reports no improvements for three consecutive rounds, with the single-worker local-LLM profile treating that as three consecutive clean evaluations from the same evaluator role.
- [x] AC-8: Validation includes inventory comparison, UTF-8 checks, `SKILL.md` frontmatter checks, path-reference resolution, OpenCode/Hermes compatibility scans, MCP dependency scans, and confirmation that no `skills/llm` source tree is created.
- [x] AC-9: A migration plan records per-skill transformation approach, source baseline, install-target assumptions, validation commands, risks, and deferred manual decisions before bulk rewriting copied skill bodies.
- [x] AC-10: The migration result distinguishes repository-local `skills/etc` as the canonical source tree from agent runtime destinations such as OpenCode global or project skill directories and Hermes user skill directories.

#### Research / Analysis

- OpenCode supports `SKILL.md` discovery through `.opencode/skills`, `~/.config/opencode/skills`, `.claude/skills`, and `.agents/skills`. Hermes uses `~/.hermes/skills` as its primary skill directory and supports external directories for shared skills. These facts support maintaining `skills/etc` as a repository-local canonical source tree while leaving installation into runtime discovery paths to the CLI installer requirement.

#### Implementation Notes

- Use `skills/etc` as the target tree name, not `skills/llm`. The migration may use the already-cleaned Codex tree as a practical transformation baseline, but the inventory must state the chosen baseline per item. Shared Kiwi references should centralize local-LLM profile rules when possible to avoid divergent copies across skills.

### OPS-FLOW-001 — 10-agent MCP and CLI tool abandonment research

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | operational |
| Target | v2.3.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | v2.3.0, research, mcp, cli, tools |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | stable |
| Verification Method | inspection, validation |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/09.mcp-cli-tool-abandonment-research.md](../research/09.mcp-cli-tool-abandonment-research.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi v2.3.0 must include a docs/research investigation that uses ten independent sub-agent research axes to explain why coding agents abandon MCP/CLI tools for SRS read, change, and add workflows, and to recommend concrete tool improvements.

#### Rationale

The active v2.3.0 goal is tool improvement, and real project use already showed agents falling back to raw Markdown because documented MCP/CLI workflows were incomplete or misleading. The research deliverable needs its own traceable SRS anchor before changing docs/research.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The research uses ten distinct investigation axes covering CLI reads, CLI mutations, MCP reads, MCP mutations, parser/validation diagnostics, agent workflow docs/templates, tests/fixtures, core architecture/API reliability, dogfood workflow simulation, and packaging/install/MCP registration environment.

### REL-FLOW-002 — Tool signature parity across runtime and docs

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | reliability |
| Target | v2.3.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | v2.3.0, workflow, docs, tools, signature |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test, inspection |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/09.mcp-cli-tool-abandonment-research.md](../research/09.mcp-cli-tool-abandonment-research.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi must keep CLI help, MCP schemas, appendix command examples, managed agent instructions, and Kiwi skill snippets aligned with the runtime tool registry through generated output or automated signature parity tests.

#### Rationale

The research found that docs and skills mention unsupported options or stale parameter shapes. Signature drift causes agents to call tools that do not exist or pass unsupported arguments, then abandon official tools.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A signature parity check compares Commander CLI commands and options against documented appendix examples for supported tool surfaces.
- [x] AC-2: A signature parity check compares MCP tool schemas against documented MCP examples and managed agent snippets for supported parameters.
- [x] AC-3: Kiwi skill snippets and managed instruction templates fail tests when they reference unsupported SpecKiwi CLI flags, MCP fields, or response shapes.
- [x] AC-4: Runtime help, MCP schema metadata, appendix examples, and skill snippets are generated from a shared registry or validated against a shared extracted registry.
- [x] AC-5: Tests include known drift cases from the v2.3.0 research, including unsupported target creation flags, optional target defaulting claims, include-content style drift, and mutation parameter aliases.

### MIG-FLOW-003 — Kiwi skills migration to official workflow tools

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | migration |
| Target | v2.3.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | v2.3.0, kiwi, skills, workflow, migration |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test, inspection |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/10.kiwi-planner-jsonl-tooling-research.md](../research/10.kiwi-planner-jsonl-tooling-research.md), [docs/research/09.mcp-cli-tool-abandonment-research.md](../research/09.mcp-cli-tool-abandonment-research.md) |

#### Requirement

Kiwi skill instructions must migrate from bespoke workflow artifact discovery, raw JSONL parsing, and shell append snippets to official SpecKiwi workflow resolvers, readers, validators, work-order tools, and guarded mutations when those tools are available.

#### Rationale

The Kiwi planner research found that skills currently encode path and JSONL conventions directly. Once official tools exist, skill text should prefer them to reduce token use, prevent field hallucination, and avoid unsafe manual writes.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: kiwi-planner, kiwi-pm, kiwi-coder, kiwi-pipeline, kiwi-srs, and related Kiwi skills instruct agents to use official workflow artifact readers and work-order tools before raw file reads for covered flows.
- [x] AC-2: Skill text uses official guarded workflow mutation tools before shell JSONL append snippets or direct plan checkbox edits for covered flows.
- [x] AC-3: Fallback to raw files is documented as degraded mode and requires captured tool diagnostics, affected artifact paths, active target, and follow-up requirement or candidate ID.
- [x] AC-4: Skill snapshot or scanner tests fail when covered flows reference unsupported SpecKiwi CLI flags, MCP fields, legacy path assumptions, or bespoke JSONL append snippets after official tooling exists.
- [x] AC-5: Migration notes preserve the existing MCP-required SRS workflow rule and do not reintroduce CLI as a normal replacement for MCP SRS mutations.

### OPS-FLOW-002 — Merge-time SRS duplicate ID repair workflow

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | operational |
| Target | v2.3.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | v2.3.0, workflow, merge, requirement-id, collision-repair |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test, inspection |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/230.md](../research/230.md), [AGENTS.md](../../AGENTS.md), [CLAUDE.md](../../CLAUDE.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi agent and release workflows must define a merge-time SRS duplicate Requirement ID repair runbook that uses validation and guarded repair tools after branch, rebase, or worktree merges, and forbids manual or aesthetic renumbering when official repair tooling is available.

#### Rationale

Doc230 identified the practical user workflow: parallel branches or worktrees add requirements, merge later, and trigger SRS-E002. Agent instructions must prevent unsafe manual renumbering and teach the supported diagnose, plan, apply, validate sequence.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Agent instructions and workflow documentation require running validate before repair and using the repair workflow only when SRS-E002 duplicate Requirement ID diagnostics exist or a named duplicate ID is confirmed in parsed diagnostics.
- [x] AC-2: The runbook requires normal Git conflict markers to be resolved first, then diagnose and dry-run repair, explicit keep and rename occurrence selection, apply from a plan or explicit mapping, and final validate, target summary, and link-check evidence.
- [x] AC-3: The workflow explicitly distinguishes merge-time collision repair from general renumbering, gap filling, ID beautification, bulk archive, bulk finalize, or status and stability transition workflows.
- [x] AC-4: The workflow prohibits hand-editing Requirement IDs when an implemented and validated runtime-callable CLI or MCP repair surface from IR-CLI-044 or FR-MCP-039 exists in the active checkout; planned requirements or core-only APIs do not block a degraded minimal SRS-MD patch path, but that path requires explicit user authorization, must be limited to the selected duplicate occurrence and explicitly mapped references, and must be followed by validate, target summary, and links checks.
- [x] AC-5: The runbook states that duplicate logical requirements must first receive unique IDs, and any discard, logical deletion, supersedes, status, stability, AC, or evidence changes must happen through separate guarded SRS mutations.
- [x] AC-6: Release or workflow verification for this feature requires evidence that duplicate IDs are zero after repair, ambiguous references were reported or explicitly mapped, and validation, target summary, and links checks pass.

### MIG-FLOW-004 — Runtime-installed Kiwi skill mirror coverage

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | migration |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi v2.3.0 skill migration work must cover both canonical repository skill sources and runtime-installed Kiwi skill mirrors discovered in the active checkout, or explicitly classify those mirrors as generated install outputs with a validated refresh path.

#### Rationale

The 10-axis SRS usefulness review found that current runtime skills are loaded from .agents/skills, while MIG-FLOW-003 and the implementation plan focused on canonical skills/codex sources and left installed mirrors out of scope.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Migration inventory identifies canonical skill source trees and runtime-installed mirrors such as .agents/skills when they exist in the active checkout.
- [x] AC-2: Each runtime mirror is either included in migration validation or classified as generated output with a documented refresh command, source manifest, and no-manual-edit rule.
- [x] AC-3: Shared resource references from runtime mirrors, including ../_shared/kiwi paths, are validated or explicitly blocked before claiming migration readiness.
- [x] AC-4: Scanner tests or inspection compare canonical source files, install metadata, runtime mirror paths, and shared-resource availability so stale or broken installed skills cannot pass unnoticed.
- [x] AC-5: Skill migration remains gated on official workflow tools being implemented and validated; until then, runtime mirrors may document degraded raw-file fallback only with captured diagnostics and follow-up requirement IDs.

| Task | T-PH018-02 | implements | - |

#### Research / Analysis

- Current Codex skill discovery in this checkout loads Kiwi skills from `.agents/skills/**`, not only from the canonical `skills/codex/**` source tree.
- Earlier runtime mirrors contained `.speckiwi-skill-install.json` files with only `name`, `agent`, and `installedAt`, which was not enough by itself to prove source path, source revision, checksum, refresh command, or no-manual-edit policy. Current mirror manifests and the installer contract must preserve those provenance fields so a documented refresh path can reproduce the same metadata.
- Some runtime skill instructions reference shared resources through `../_shared/kiwi/...`; migration readiness therefore requires validating whether the installed mirror includes those shared resources, whether they resolve back to the canonical source tree, or whether the mirror must be refreshed.
- The implementation plan must treat runtime mirrors as first-class migration inputs or explicitly classify them as generated install outputs before declaring skill migration complete.

#### Implementation Notes

- Do not migrate runtime mirrors to proposed workflow tool calls before those tools exist. First make mirror/source identity and shared-resource resolution visible and testable.

#### Requirement

The kiwi-spec-merge skill SHALL orchestrate an SRS merge as a sequence of per-REQ, single-REQ, req-scoped operations — dry-run, explicit user approval, a single one-REQ mutation call, journal append, and resume — and SHALL NOT invoke any N-REQ batch finalize tool. For each requirement it SHALL route the diff_steps classification to the matching guarded mutation: NEW to promote_step_requirement, UPDATE to an in-place body update, and CONFLICT to supersede_requirement. On completion it SHALL migrate loser-side verification evidence and emit a post-merge verification-decrease report.

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: The skill issues exactly one requirement per mutation call and never invokes an N-REQ batch finalize tool.

- [ ] AC-3: The skill routes a diff_steps NEW class to promote_step_requirement, an UPDATE class to an in-place body update, and a CONFLICT class to supersede_requirement.
- [ ] AC-4: The skill resumes an interrupted merge from the merge-journal without duplicating already-applied per-REQ calls.
- [ ] AC-5: The skill produces a post-merge verification-decrease report and migrates loser-side (superseded) evidence.

### FR-FLOW-016 — Versioned agent-instruction governance snippet with idempotent AGENT_INSTRUCTION_VERSION upsert

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL render a single managed SpecKiwi SRS-workflow governance snippet into both CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md during project init, bounded by a versioned top-level heading (`# SpecKiwi SRS 워크플로 v<AGENT_INSTRUCTION_VERSION>`) and a fixed end marker (`<!-- /SpecKiwi SRS 워크플로 -->`). The snippet body SHALL carry the SRS MUST rules (spec source of truth, Requirement-ID lookup, Status/Stability lifecycle, TDD, current-work and next-target workflows). When AGENT_INSTRUCTION_VERSION is bumped, the versioned-marker upsert SHALL idempotently replace the prior managed block in place so that re-running init leaves exactly one up-to-date block per file, while unmanaged local sections and mere prose mentions of the heading are preserved.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Running init on a repository writes the governance snippet into both CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md, each containing the current versioned heading (`# SpecKiwi SRS 워크플로 v1.4`), the SRS MUST-rule / Status-Stability / TDD / workflow prose, and the closing end marker `<!-- /SpecKiwi SRS 워크플로 -->`.
- [ ] AC-2: The rendered heading version is driven by the AGENT_INSTRUCTION_VERSION constant; a block whose version equals the current AGENT_INSTRUCTION_VERSION and which has the end marker is treated as up to date and skipped, whereas an older versioned block is superseded — so bumping AGENT_INSTRUCTION_VERSION causes the next init to replace the prior block.
- [ ] AC-3: Re-running init is idempotent: each of CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md ends with exactly one versioned heading and exactly one end marker (no duplicated snippet).
- [ ] AC-4: A stale managed block — either an older versioned heading paired with the end marker, or a legacy unversioned `# SpecKiwi SRS workflow` heading — is replaced in place by the current block, while unmanaged sibling sections (e.g. a local heading) are preserved.
- [ ] AC-5: A versioned heading that lacks the end marker, a bare prose mention of the heading, or a malformed heading that would otherwise pair with a later unrelated block's marker is NOT treated as a managed block; such content and any intervening local sections are preserved and the current block is added without corrupting surrounding content.

### FR-FLOW-020 — Code comment trace-tag convention for sdd, vibe, and tdd modes

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL define a code-comment trace-tag convention so that synthesis and plain grep can attribute a code region to its originating work item. In sdd mode, a change to a newly defined class, method, or function SHALL carry the governing SRS Requirement ID in a single-line comment tag of the form `@req <REQ-ID>` (e.g. `// @req FR-NODE-039`, `# @req FR-X-001`), one Requirement ID per line, attached immediately before the definition or in its docstring. In vibe mode, where no SRS requirement exists yet, the change SHALL instead carry the active step task-name as the trace tag. In tdd mode, where the SRS requirement is promoted post-hoc, the change SHALL likewise carry the active step task-name during green implementation and SHALL be reconciled to the promoted Requirement ID during synthesis/promote. The convention SHALL be inserted and applied by the coding skills and documented as its source of truth, while remaining a purely informational breadcrumb that gates nothing (a missing or stale tag never blocks a build, test, review, or commit).

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: An sdd-mode change to a newly defined named class/method/function carries the governing SRS Requirement ID in a `@req <REQ-ID>` code comment (single Requirement ID per line, placed on the line before the definition or in its docstring, using the language's comment style: `//` for TS/JS/Java/Go/Rust/C-family, `#` for Python/Ruby/Shell, `* ` inside JSDoc/JavaDoc blocks).
- [ ] AC-2: A vibe-mode change, made while no SRS requirement exists for the work, carries the active step task-name as its trace tag in place of a REQ-ID, so the region can later be attributed to that task during synthesis.
- [ ] AC-3: The trace-tag convention is inserted and applied by the coding skill during its implementation phase and is documented as the convention's source of truth (scope of the attach obligation, closed-list exemptions, single-line tag format, and dedupe/placement rules), while being explicitly exempt from every verification, review, plan-code-match, and gate check so it can never block work.
- [ ] AC-4: A tdd-mode change, made while the SRS requirement is promoted post-hoc, carries the active step task-name during green implementation and is reconciled to the promoted Requirement ID during synthesis/promote, so the code region attributes to the final requirement.

#### Requirement

The system SHALL, on project init, generate and idempotently maintain a versioned, canonical managed agent-instruction block that declares a tool-first policy for SRS work. The block SHALL state that when SpecKiwi MCP tools are available agents MUST use them for requirement lookup and safe SRS updates, and MUST fall back to the `speckiwi` CLI when MCP is unavailable, and it SHALL forbid creating an alternate requirements source outside `docs/spec/`. The same block SHALL be injected into both `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` and be embedded in the bundled SRS-MD-Rules document, and it SHALL be versioned by `AGENT_INSTRUCTION_VERSION` so that re-running init upserts exactly one current block without duplicating or clobbering unrelated content.

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

### FR-FLOW-023 — kiwi-srs research-document-driven SRS verify/improve (A/B) loop

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

kiwi-srs SHALL accept research document(s) as an argument or via a user prompt reference. After SRS authoring it SHALL run an A/B loop. Process A spawns verification subagent(s) that read the research document(s) and compare them against the newly authored SRS to detect missing requirements, incorrectly authored requirements, and implementability in the current architecture — reusing the existing kiwi-srs-feasibility implementability evaluation and additionally checking that existing product features are not broken — and write an improvements document to a run-scoped temp directory; if there are no improvements, Process A terminates. Process B spawns a separate subagent that reads the improvements document and improves the SRS, after which control returns to Process A. The loop SHALL be bounded by a divergence guard (maximum iterations).

#### Rationale

v2.3.0.1 tool-improvement: close the loop between research documents and authored SRS so missing/incorrect requirements and architectural infeasibility are caught and improved before work proceeds. User-directed 2026-07-09.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: kiwi-srs accepts research document(s) as an explicit argument or resolves them from a user prompt reference.
- [x] AC-2: Process A: a verification subagent compares the research document(s) against the newly added SRS and reports (i) missing requirements, (ii) incorrectly authored requirements, and (iii) implementability in the current architecture — reusing the existing feasibility evaluation AND flagging any break to existing product features — writing an improvements document to a run-scoped temp directory; when no improvements are found, Process A terminates.
- [x] AC-3: Process B: a separate subagent reads the improvements document and applies improvements to the SRS, then returns control to Process A, bounded by a maximum-iteration divergence guard.
- [x] AC-4: In `--max` mode, Process A uses 3 verification subagents.
- [x] AC-5: With multiple research documents, non-max mode spawns verification subagents sequentially per document; `--max` mode spawns (document count × 3) verification subagents.

### FR-FLOW-024 — kiwi-srs no-research-document ambiguity resolution, qna loop, and requirement-gathering research

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

When no research document is provided, kiwi-srs SHALL investigate the requirements for ambiguity and question the user only about genuinely non-standard ambiguities that have no reasonable default; a user-supplied decision SHALL itself be re-examined for ambiguity and re-questioned when needed. A sufficiently vague request SHALL auto-activate an unbounded qna loop (also forced by `--qna-force`), replacing the prior bounded QnA — except under `--auto`, where the interactive qna loop and `--qna-force` are suppressed and remaining ambiguities are resolved directly by the FR-FLOW-025 committee. The `--qna-force` flag SHALL be parsed fail-closed: it matches the exact token `--qna-force` only (never `--qna`, whose prefix it contains), and any unrecognized or single-dash flag variant SHALL raise a hard error rather than a silent no-op or an inverted action. When `--auto` is absent the loop SHALL run until the user signals end, at which point kiwi-srs SHALL enter auto-decision mode for the remaining ambiguities. It SHALL then spawn 3 subagents that perform requirement gathering and research into how to cleanly implement the requirements in the current architecture and project structure (architecture, algorithms, and a brief implementation plan), saving the result under `docs/research/`, and SHALL then proceed to the research-document verify/improve loop (FR-FLOW-023).

#### Rationale

v2.3.0.1 tool-improvement: give kiwi-srs a principled path for under-specified requests — ask only genuinely non-standard questions, otherwise research and decide — feeding the research output into the verify/improve loop. User-directed 2026-07-09.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: With no research document, kiwi-srs detects ambiguity in the requirements and asks the user only about genuinely non-standard ambiguities that have no reasonable default; a user-supplied decision is re-checked for ambiguity and re-questioned when needed.
- [x] AC-2: A sufficiently vague request (e.g. "make an SRS for a game") auto-activates the unbounded qna loop, and `--qna-force` forces it — except under `--auto`, where the interactive qna loop and `--qna-force` are suppressed and remaining ambiguities are resolved by the FR-FLOW-025 committee (no interactive qna). `--qna-force` is parsed fail-closed as an exact token (never the `--qna` alias it prefixes; unrecognized or single-dash variants hard-error). This replaces the prior bounded QnA. When `--auto` is absent, the qna loop runs until the user signals end.
- [x] AC-3: On end, kiwi-srs enters auto-decision mode for the remaining ambiguities.
- [x] AC-4: kiwi-srs spawns 3 subagents that gather requirements and research a clean implementation approach in the current architecture/project structure (architecture, algorithms, a brief implementation plan), save it under `docs/research/`, then proceed to the research-document verify/improve loop (FR-FLOW-023).

#### Verification Evidence

### FR-FLOW-025 — --auto decision committee sizing and simple-majority decision

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The `--auto` auto-decision mode SHALL convene a decision committee of research-performing subagents that decides the most reasonable option, replacing the prior `--auto` "block instead of ask" behavior. The committee SHALL have 3 members under `--auto`, or 5 members under `--max`, and SHALL decide by simple majority — the option holding strictly more than half of the votes cast — adopting that option immediately. The unanimity requirement and the 3-to-5 and 5-to-7 escalation ladders SHALL be removed: a non-unanimous committee SHALL NOT be enlarged at either size, and no committee SHALL be re-convened at a larger size for any reason. A committee that produces no majority SHALL be governed by the separately authored no-majority escalation requirement in `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` and SHALL NOT be resolved by enlarging the committee.

#### Rationale

v2.3.0.1 tool-improvement: make --auto auto-decide via a research-performing committee with a defined unanimity-escalation ladder (3->5 majority; --max 5->7 majority), superseding the prior --auto "block" semantic. User-directed 2026-07-09.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `--auto` convenes a 3-member decision committee whose members perform research investigation and select the most reasonable option.
- [x] AC-2: `--max` raises the decision committee to 5 members.
- [x] AC-3: Under `--auto` without `--max`, the 3-member committee decides by simple majority — the option holding strictly more than half of the votes cast — and adopts it immediately; a non-unanimous result never enlarges the committee to 5 members, and no 3-to-5 escalation rung remains in any of the four `auto-option.md` copies.
- [x] AC-4: Under `--auto --max`, the 5-member committee decides by simple majority without requiring unanimity; a non-unanimous result never enlarges the committee to 7 members, and neither a 5-to-7 rung nor any other committee-enlargement rung remains in any of the four `auto-option.md` copies.
- [x] AC-5: Verification evidence for this requirement carries an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed copy at `~/.claude/skills/_shared/kiwi/auto-option.md` and recording that landing the four bundled copies changes no `--auto` behaviour on the machine until a reinstall, since the charter forbids running `speckiwi init` with this repository as its target root.

### FR-FLOW-026 — kiwi-pipeline end-to-end research-to-implementation cycle orchestration

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The kiwi-pipeline skill SHALL orchestrate the full research -> planning -> implementation cycle by chaining the kiwi-* sub-skills, rather than only recommending a single next step. Given a work input it SHALL run `/kiwi-srs`, then judge whether `/kiwi-srs-feasibility` is needed and run it only when required, then run `/kiwi-planner`, `/kiwi-pm`, and `/kiwi-review-fix-loop` in sequence. When invoked without a work input it SHALL advance the cycle from the last pipeline.jsonl event via Table T1. Between stages it SHALL apply a user gate unless `--auto` is set; under `--auto` it SHALL decide every gate autonomously via the FR-FLOW-025 decision committee and run to the end, while still halting on a sub-skill NEEDS_USER/FAILED and the existing critical gates. Under `--max` it SHALL propagate `--max` to every spawned sub-skill. If the user supplies a research document it SHALL pass that document through to `/kiwi-srs`.

#### Rationale

v2.3.0.1 tool-improvement: elevate kiwi-pipeline from a single-step next-action advisor to an end-to-end research->plan->implement cycle orchestrator that composes the kiwi-* skills. Designed to compose with goal mode for large autonomous runs. User-directed 2026-07-09.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: With a work input, kiwi-pipeline runs the sequence /kiwi-srs -> (conditional /kiwi-srs-feasibility) -> /kiwi-planner -> /kiwi-pm -> /kiwi-review-fix-loop, chaining stages rather than stopping after a single next-step recommendation.
- [x] AC-2: kiwi-pipeline runs /kiwi-srs-feasibility only when it judges it is needed (e.g. the just-authored requirements carry draft stability or unverified implementability); otherwise it skips feasibility.
- [x] AC-3: Between stages a user gate is applied unless `--auto` is set or the user asks to run automatically; under `--auto` every gate is auto-decided via the FR-FLOW-025 committee and the cycle runs to the end, while a sub-skill NEEDS_USER/FAILED or an existing critical gate still halts.
- [x] AC-4: `--max` is propagated to every spawned sub-skill.
- [x] AC-5: A user-supplied research document is passed through to `/kiwi-srs` (per FR-FLOW-023).

### FR-FLOW-027 — kiwi-pipeline worktree isolation with merge-or-PR completion gate

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

When the user requests worktree isolation or passes a `--wt` argument, the kiwi-pipeline skill SHALL create a dedicated git worktree and run the cycle inside it. On successful completion it SHALL resolve the worktree: in interactive (non-auto) mode it SHALL ask the user whether to merge the branch or open a PR; under `--auto` it SHALL open a PR (via kiwi-commit-auto-pr) and SHALL NOT direct-merge the base branch. This extends kiwi-pipeline beyond its prior read-only nature (whose only side effect was appending to pipeline.jsonl) to a defined git worktree mutation.

#### Rationale

v2.3.0.1 tool-improvement: let a full pipeline cycle run in an isolated worktree and resolve cleanly (ask merge-or-PR interactively; auto-PR under --auto), keeping the base branch safe. User-directed 2026-07-09.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A `--wt` argument, or a user request for worktree isolation, makes kiwi-pipeline create a dedicated git worktree and run the cycle inside it.
- [x] AC-2: On successful completion in interactive (non-auto) mode, kiwi-pipeline asks the user whether to merge the worktree branch or open a PR.
- [x] AC-3: Under `--auto`, kiwi-pipeline opens a PR (kiwi-commit-auto-pr) on completion and does not direct-merge the base branch.
- [x] AC-4: Each shipped rendering of the skill states the worktree creation of §2.6 as an explicit exception to its §0.2 self-mutation ban, rather than declaring without qualification that it mutates git under no circumstances.

### FR-FLOW-028 — kiwi-pipeline GitHub issue entry mode with research-first flow

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

When the user supplies a GitHub issue number, the kiwi-pipeline skill SHALL first run `/kiwi-srs-research` to research how to resolve the issue and perform additional research on the implementation approach, then start `/kiwi-srs`. When the research alone does not sufficiently resolve the requirement's ambiguities it SHALL invoke `/kiwi-srs` with the `--qna-force` option — except under `--auto`, where it SHALL suppress `--qna-force` and resolve remaining ambiguities via the FR-FLOW-025 decision committee. The cycle SHALL then continue through the standard planning and implementation stages (/kiwi-planner, /kiwi-pm, /kiwi-review-fix-loop).

#### Rationale

v2.3.0.1 tool-improvement: give kiwi-pipeline a GitHub-issue entry point that researches the resolution and implementation approach first, then authors SRS (escalating to --qna-force only when research is insufficient and not under --auto). User-directed 2026-07-09.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A supplied GitHub issue number makes kiwi-pipeline run `/kiwi-srs-research` to research the resolution plus additional implementation-approach research before starting `/kiwi-srs`.
- [x] AC-2: When research alone leaves the requirement ambiguous, kiwi-pipeline starts `/kiwi-srs` with `--qna-force`; under `--auto` it instead suppresses `--qna-force` and resolves ambiguities via the FR-FLOW-025 committee.
- [x] AC-3: After the issue-driven research and authoring, the cycle continues through /kiwi-planner, /kiwi-pm, and /kiwi-review-fix-loop.

### FR-FLOW-029 — kiwi-wave-master multi-wave orchestrator with per-wave targets and resumable progress

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide a `kiwi-wave-master` skill that orchestrates a large research/plan effort as an ordered sequence of waves above kiwi-pipeline. Given the user's existing research and plan documents, it SHALL decompose them into ordered waves — using explicit wave structure in the document when present, otherwise a subagent SHALL analyze the document's overall flow to split the waves. For each wave it SHALL register a dedicated target (e.g. `wave-{n}`) through `/kiwi-srs` with an explicitly specified work scope, so that downstream feasibility, planning, and review are bounded to that wave and never look beyond it. It SHALL persist wave progress to a JSONL file (e.g. `./kiwi/waves.jsonl`) and invoke `/kiwi-pipeline` per wave in registration order to implement each wave, marking a wave complete in the JSONL only after its kiwi-pipeline run finishes successfully, so that a cleared or restarted session resumes from the first incomplete wave. The up-front `/kiwi-srs` pass SHALL author each wave's requirements; the per-wave `/kiwi-pipeline` SHALL skip re-authoring and run from feasibility/planning through implementation. Under `--auto` it SHALL run all waves autonomously to the end (per-wave kiwi-pipeline safety gates still applying); under `--max` it SHALL propagate `--max` to every wave's kiwi-pipeline and sub-skills.

#### Rationale

v2.3.0.1 tool-improvement: add a wave-level orchestrator above kiwi-pipeline so a large research/plan effort is decomposed into scoped, independently-targeted waves executed in order with JSONL-backed resumability across cleared sessions. Composes strongly with goal mode. Name and division auto-decided 2026-07-09 in the user's absence (provisional). User-directed 2026-07-09.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: kiwi-wave-master decomposes the user's research/plan documents into ordered waves — using explicit wave structure in the document when present, otherwise a subagent analyzes the document's overall flow to split waves.
- [x] AC-2: For each wave, kiwi-wave-master registers a dedicated target (e.g. `wave-{n}`) via `/kiwi-srs` with an explicitly specified work scope, so downstream feasibility/planning/review are bounded to that wave and do not look beyond it.
- [x] AC-3: kiwi-wave-master persists wave progress to a JSONL file (e.g. `./kiwi/waves.jsonl`); a wave is marked complete only after its execution finishes, so a cleared/restarted session resumes from the first incomplete wave.
- [x] AC-4: kiwi-wave-master invokes `/kiwi-pipeline` per wave in order to implement each wave; the up-front `/kiwi-srs` authors each wave's requirements and the per-wave `/kiwi-pipeline` skips re-authoring, running from feasibility/planning through implementation.
- [x] AC-5: Under `--auto`, kiwi-wave-master runs all waves autonomously to the end (per-wave kiwi-pipeline safety gates still apply); under `--max`, `--max` is propagated to every wave's kiwi-pipeline and its sub-skills.

### FR-FLOW-031 — kiwi-planner planner-document management and validation via v2.3.0 workflow MCP tools

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The kiwi-planner skill (all packaged variants) SHALL use the v2.3.0 SpecKiwi workflow_* MCP tool family as the primary path for reading, validating, and mutating its planner document (plan.md + sidecar), superseding bespoke file handling and the bundled validator.mjs. Reads SHALL go through workflow_plan_status / workflow_plan_task / workflow_next_plan_task; validation and diagnostics through workflow_doctor / workflow_schema_check / workflow_diff; checkbox mutations through workflow_task_check / workflow_task_uncheck / workflow_checklist_set; and pipeline/worklog events through the guarded workflow emit tools. Validation coverage SHALL NOT regress: any plan-contract check formerly done by validator.mjs (C01-C25) that the workflow MCP family does not yet provide SHALL be preserved during implementation. Raw docs/plans file access SHALL remain only a documented degraded fallback per MIG-FLOW-003 AC-3.

#### Rationale

v2.3.0.1 tool-improvement: kiwi-planner uses the v2.3.0 workflow_* MCP tool family as the primary path to read, validate, and mutate its planner document, superseding bespoke file handling and the bundled validator.mjs, while ensuring plan-contract validation coverage does not regress. Extends MIG-FLOW-003 (depends_on) to concrete planner-document management and closes the unmigrated claude variant. User-directed 2026-07-09.

- [x] AC-1: kiwi-planner uses the workflow_* MCP tool family as the primary path for reading (workflow_plan_status / workflow_plan_task / workflow_next_plan_task), validating (workflow_doctor / workflow_schema_check / workflow_diff), and mutating (workflow_task_check / workflow_task_uncheck / workflow_checklist_set) its planner document, superseding raw file handling and the bundled validator.mjs.
- [x] AC-2: All packaged kiwi-planner variants (claude, codex, etc) route planner-document operations through the workflow_* MCP tools; the claude variant, which currently invokes none, is brought into compliance (also satisfying MIG-FLOW-003).
- [x] AC-3: Validation coverage does not regress: any plan-contract check formerly in validator.mjs (C01-C25) not yet provided by the workflow MCP family is preserved during implementation, and raw docs/plans file access remains only a documented degraded fallback.

### FR-FLOW-032 — kiwi-planner plan-vs-SRS coverage verification loop

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

After kiwi-planner produces a plan, it SHALL run a coverage verification loop that thoroughly checks the plan against the SRS and the intended implementation scope. The loop SHALL start by reconciling the count of target SRS requirements against the plan coverage entries, then cross-check each requirement ID one-by-one against the plan to confirm every requirement and acceptance criterion is satisfied. The plan coverage for a requirement SHALL be verified by 2 subagents applied in sequence (running on the current session model per FR-FLOW-022): the first subagent confirms it, and only after that does a second subagent independently re-confirm it; the requirement SHALL be marked verification-complete (implementation-ready) only after both subagents confirm, and that marking SHALL be persistent so a marked requirement is not re-analyzed or changed in later iterations. Under --max the verification SHALL be strengthened: coverage SHALL be checked at acceptance-criterion granularity (each AC, and where applicable its test/TDD task, mapped to a plan task); after the two sequential confirmations an independent third subagent SHALL attempt to refute full coverage and the requirement is marked verification-complete only if that refutation fails; and the loop SHALL terminate only after two consecutive rounds with zero new omissions. When an omission is found — a requirement or acceptance criterion not covered by any plan task, or a plan task outside the SRS scope — kiwi-planner SHALL improve the plan and re-verify only the not-yet-marked items, iterating until every requirement is marked verification-complete, bounded by a divergence guard.

#### Rationale

v2.3.0.1 tool-improvement: require kiwi-planner to formally verify, in a loop, that its plan covers every target requirement and AC before completing, catching plan/SRS omissions early. Formalizes kiwi-planner's internal SRS-satisfaction evaluation as an explicit requirement; uses 2 subagents by default under FR-FLOW-022's current-model policy. User-directed 2026-07-10.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The verification loop starts by reconciling the count of target SRS requirements against the plan coverage entries, then cross-checks each requirement ID one-by-one against the plan.
- [x] AC-2: Verification uses 2 subagents applied in sequence (on the current session model per FR-FLOW-022): the first subagent confirms a requirement plan coverage, and only after it passes does the second subagent independently re-confirm; the requirement is marked verification-complete (implementation-ready) only after both confirm, and that marking is persistent so a marked requirement is not re-analyzed or changed in later iterations. Under --max, coverage is checked at acceptance-criterion granularity and an independent third subagent must fail to refute full coverage before the requirement is marked.
- [x] AC-3: When an omission is found (a requirement or AC uncovered by any plan task, or a plan task outside the SRS scope), kiwi-planner improves the plan and re-verifies only the not-yet-marked items, iterating until every requirement is marked verification-complete, bounded by a divergence guard; under --max, the loop terminates only after two consecutive rounds with zero new omissions.

### FR-FLOW-033 — kiwi-* --auto safe propagation preserves child safety gates uniformly across runtime variants

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | planned |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

When a kiwi-* skill propagates `--auto` to a child kiwi-* skill, it SHALL NOT auto-synthesize any option that bypasses the child skill's dry-run precedence or user-approval safety gate (such as `--auto-apply` or `--yes-all`); it SHALL propagate only `--auto` (plus flags the user explicitly supplied or inherited `--max`/`--model`). This safe-propagation contract SHALL be identical across all runtime variants (claude, codex, etc). Specifically, `kiwi-hot-fix --auto` delegating to `kiwi-srs-sync` SHALL propagate `--auto` alone — never `--auto-apply` or `--yes-all` unless the user explicitly supplied those flags — and `kiwi-srs-sync` under `--auto` alone SHALL retain its dry-run precedence and its critical_gates HALT (e.g. irreversible apply-all MCP mutation), so `--auto` propagation cannot silently bypass user approval.

#### Rationale

v2.3.0.1 tool-improvement safety alignment (committee decision D4). The claude kiwi-hot-fix defined `--auto` as synthesizing `--auto-apply --yes-all` and propagating it to the child kiwi-srs-sync, which bypasses kiwi-srs-sync's dry-run precedence + user-approval gate. codex/etc forbid that synthesis (propagate `--auto` alone). This asymmetry means the same command has different safety gates per runtime. kiwi-srs-sync under `--auto` alone already retains critical_gates HALT and is safely autonomous, so the synthesis was redundant and only removed review discretion. Align claude to the safe (codex/etc) side and standardize the contract across all variants. User-directed committee decision 2026-07-12.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: AC-1 (no gate-bypass synthesis): The `kiwi-hot-fix --auto` delegation to `kiwi-srs-sync` propagates only `--auto` and does not auto-synthesize `--auto-apply` or `--yes-all`; those direct-apply flags propagate only when the user explicitly supplied them.
- [ ] AC-2: AC-2 (cross-variant symmetry): The `kiwi-hot-fix --auto` sync-propagation contract is identical across the claude, codex, and etc runtime variants in both the kiwi-hot-fix SKILL body and the `_shared/kiwi/auto-option.md` special-propagation table and migration/compatibility note.
- [ ] AC-3: AC-3 (critical_gates retained): `kiwi-srs-sync` under `--auto` alone retains its dry-run precedence and critical_gates HALT (apply-all irreversible MCP mutation is not auto-skipped), so `--auto` propagation cannot bypass user approval of irreversible SRS mutations.

### FR-FLOW-034 — Loop round-cap flags (--mini preset, --loops counter) across kiwi skills, superseding the --mini removal

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

All kiwi-* skills that run a verification/improvement (or evaluation) loop SHALL accept two loop-round-cap options: `--mini` and `--loops N`. `--mini` is a quick-mode preset equivalent to a verify/improve round cap of 3. `--loops N` is an explicit loop-round counter that sets the cap to N, where N is a positive integer. The round cap is an upper bound on verify->improve rounds; the existing severity gate (Normal CRITICAL=0+HIGH=0; Max 2-consecutive-MEDIUM=0) MAY terminate the loop earlier. When both `--mini` and `--loops N` are supplied, `--loops N` SHALL take precedence order-independently, and the skill SHALL emit a non-fatal warning that the preset's 3-round count was overridden. When the round cap is reached without a clean gate, the skill SHALL stop looping and report residual findings without silent truncation; residual CRITICAL/HIGH findings SHALL still surface, i.e. the round cap SHALL NOT bypass the safety gate. `--mini` and `--loops` SHALL be orthogonal to `--max` (which controls verifier strength and gate strictness) and SHALL compose; an explicit `--loops N` SHALL also override any `--max`-derived default cap while `--max` still governs gate strictness. A shared SSOT `_shared/kiwi/loop-option.md` SHALL define these semantics. This re-introduces the `--mini` token with round-cap semantics ONLY and SUPERSEDES the blanket `--mini` token prohibition in FR-FLOW-022 AC-1; the removed model-swap `--mini` semantics and `_shared/kiwi/mini-option.md` SHALL remain removed, and model selection SHALL remain governed solely by `--model` (FR-FLOW-022 AC-2 and AC-3 remain in force). Skills without a round-capped loop (kiwi-step, kiwi-srs-research) SHALL accept the flags as a documented no-op for orchestration uniformity.

#### Rationale

User-directed 2026-07-12: add a quick-mode `--mini` (bounded verify/improve rounds) and an explicit loop counter to all kiwi skills, with orchestrators propagating to sub-skills. Distinct from the model-swap `--mini` removed by FR-FLOW-022; here `--mini` caps loop rounds only and model selection stays on `--model`. Precedence (explicit `--loops` wins over `--mini`, with a warning) decided by unanimous 3-subagent research (CLI prior-art, user-intent/safety, kiwi-internal consistency).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `--mini` and `--loops N` are accepted and documented in the option table of every kiwi-* skill; skills without a round-capped loop (kiwi-step, kiwi-srs-research) document them as a no-op accepted for orchestration uniformity.
- [x] AC-2: `--mini` sets the verify/improve round cap to 3; `--loops N` sets the cap to N where N is a positive integer, and N less than 1 is rejected with an error.
- [x] AC-3: When both `--mini` and `--loops N` are supplied, `--loops N` wins order-independently and a non-fatal warning records that `--mini`'s 3-round count was overridden.
- [x] AC-4: Reaching the round cap without a clean severity gate stops the loop and reports residual findings without silent truncation; residual CRITICAL/HIGH findings still surface and the cap does not bypass the safety gate.
- [x] AC-5: `_shared/kiwi/loop-option.md` exists (claude and codex variants) as the SSOT for `--mini`/`--loops`; `_shared/kiwi/mini-option.md` and model-swap `--mini` semantics remain absent repo-wide, and `--model` remains the sole model-selection control.
- [x] AC-6: `--mini`/`--loops` are orthogonal to `--max` and compose (e.g. `--max --loops 5`); an explicit `--loops N` overrides any `--max`-derived default loop cap while `--max` still governs gate strictness.

Auditable artifact: docs/analysis/kiwi-loop-option-2026-07-12.speckiwi.v2301/priority-research.md. ### FR-FLOW-035 — Orchestrator propagation of loop round-cap flags to delegated kiwi sub-skills

#### Requirement

Kiwi orchestration skills SHALL propagate `--mini` and `--loops N` to every kiwi sub-skill they spawn or delegate to, consistent with the existing child-propagation SSOT (`_shared/kiwi/auto-option.md` section 7 / 7.1, mirrored for loop options). Propagation SHALL cover at least: kiwi-pm to kiwi-coder; kiwi-pipeline to every kiwi sub-skill it spawns; kiwi-wave-master to its per-wave `/kiwi-srs` and `/kiwi-pipeline`; kiwi-hot-fix to kiwi-srs-sync; and kiwi-coder to its kiwi-review-fix-loop follow-up. The stale `MINI=` child-spawn line in kiwi-pm (a leftover reference to the removed model-swap flag) SHALL be repurposed to forward the new `--mini`/`--loops` values so no dead reference to the removed flag remains. Propagation SHALL apply uniformly across the claude, codex, and etc runtime variants.

#### Rationale

User-directed 2026-07-12: orchestration skills must apply --mini/--loops to all sub-skills. Mirrors the established --auto/--max/--model child-propagation pattern in _shared/kiwi/auto-option.md so a quick-mode or loop-count run is consistent end-to-end through pm/pipeline/wave-master/hot-fix delegations. Also removes the stale kiwi-pm MINI= dead reference surfaced during structure mapping.

- [x] AC-2: kiwi-pipeline forwards `--mini`/`--loops N` to every kiwi sub-skill it spawns.
- [x] AC-3: kiwi-wave-master forwards `--mini`/`--loops N` to its per-wave `/kiwi-srs` and `/kiwi-pipeline` invocations.
- [x] AC-4: kiwi-hot-fix forwards `--mini`/`--loops N` to its kiwi-srs-sync delegation.
- [x] AC-5: kiwi-coder forwards `--mini`/`--loops N` to its kiwi-review-fix-loop follow-up.
- [x] AC-6: The former stale `MINI=` line in kiwi-pm's child-spawn prompt is repurposed to forward `--mini`/`--loops`, leaving no dead reference to the removed model-swap flag.
- [x] AC-7: A child-propagation table for `--mini`/`--loops` is documented in the shared SSOT (`_shared/kiwi/auto-option.md` section 7 or `_shared/kiwi/loop-option.md`) and applies across claude, codex, and etc variants.
