### FR-FLOW-059 — Unattended wave-run continuity across undeclared stops and evidence windows

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.4 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/analysis/wave-fit-eval/round2-findings.md |

#### Requirement

When kiwi-wave-master runs unattended, every point at which the run can stop, lose its evidence window, or lose its denominator SHALL be resolved by the run's own declared options or declared as a gate row, and no such point SHALL depend on a value the user must type.

#### Rationale

Round-2 evaluation findings H1, H3, M4, M5, M7, M8, L1, L2, L3. The implementation skill keeps two consent gates that unattended mode alone cannot resolve — integration-test consent and the large-task cost warning — because both are declared resolvable only on explicit input, and the pass-through chain that would carry them exists but has no source: the natural-language option table has no row for either, so their values exist only if the user types them. Since long tasks and integration tests are near-default in a coding wave, the run stops in its first wave, at a stop that no gate table predicts. The same undeclared class covers the requirement-authoring child, which the orchestrator calls directly rather than through the pipeline, so its own critical gates return through a path the orchestrator's pipeline-scoped gate row does not name; the gate section's own prose count and positional references no longer match its table. The document-selection question at the start of decomposition is likewise an undeclared gate, which the shared unattended contract resolves by committee default. Beyond stopping, the run loses information it needs: the reinforced mode does not reach the per-wave authoring call because propagation is described only for what the pipeline cycle spawns, the evidence window is a single pipeline run identifier while every re-entry mints a new one, so verification reads either pre-fix evidence or stale clean evidence, and no point freezes the verification denominators, so authoring and fixes inside the loop keep enlarging them and invalidated rounds consume the cap. A crash leaves the task-runner lock in place with no propagation path to clear it on resume, and the terminal hop of the cycle chain is undefined against the transition table, leaving a possible unannounced external side effect.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The integration-test consent gate and the large-task cost-warning gate SHALL be reachable through the orchestrator's declared pass-through option chain, the orchestrator SHALL state that its unattended option alone does not pass them and that each is bypassed only when its own option is explicitly supplied, and both gate identifiers SHALL appear in the orchestrator's gate table.
- [x] AC-2: A directly invoked requirement-authoring child that returns a needs-user or failed outcome SHALL be a declared gate row covering both the per-wave authoring step and the verification-driven re-authoring step, and the surrounding prose SHALL name that child alongside the pipeline child.
- [x] AC-3: Every count and positional reference in the gate section's explanatory prose SHALL agree with the rows of the gate table it describes.
- [x] AC-4: The question asking which document to decompose SHALL be a declared gate rather than an undeclared decision resolved by the shared unattended contract's default.
- [x] AC-5: The reinforced mode SHALL propagate to the per-wave requirement-authoring call that the orchestrator invokes directly, in the same way the round-cap options already do.
- [x] AC-6: A stale task-runner lock left by a crashed run SHALL be resolvable on resume through a declared propagation path, so that pipeline resume is not blocked by it.
- [x] AC-7: The verification evidence window SHALL resolve every pipeline run belonging to that wave, including runs created by re-entry, so a round never judges against pre-fix evidence or against a clean result predating the latest fix.
- [x] AC-8: The verification denominators SHALL have a declared freeze point per round, so authoring or fixes applied during the loop do not retroactively invalidate rounds and consume the round cap.
- [x] AC-9: The terminal hop of the cycle chain SHALL be unambiguous against the transition table, and any external side effect at that hop SHALL be a declared gate.

### FR-FLOW-060 — Executable remediation routing for wave verification findings

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.4 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/analysis/wave-fit-eval/round2-findings.md |

#### Requirement

When a wave or final verification round produces a finding that requires a fix, the run SHALL have an executable route from that finding to a scoped re-entry that actually changes code, and every input that route depends on SHALL be resolvable from the journal alone.

#### Rationale

Round-2 evaluation findings H2, H4, H7, M1, M13. The verification-driven re-entry is required to pass an unaddressed-requirement filter and a decision about reusing the plan run identity, but the child it re-enters accepts neither: its option table has no requirement filter and no plan run argument, and the handover to its own children carries only the unattended, model, and requirement-closing flags. The plan run identity is derived from date, project, and target, so a re-entry on the same day produces the identical identifier, the emit step skips it as already recorded, and the chain never observes the completion event it waits on; meanwhile the plan and the session directory are reused with every task already marked done, so the re-entry can report completion without executing a single task. Carry-forward has the mirror problem: it is declared the primary response and is recorded as a residual naming the receiving wave, but neither the per-wave authoring step nor the pipeline step is told to read it, so the primary response terminates in a record with no consumer and the carried defect is absent from the receiving wave's denominator and is never re-detected. A wave appended solely to receive carried findings corresponds to no section of the input document, so it cannot be given the source coordinates the design baseline requires and its verification denominators become undefined. The final pass compounds this: it declares reuse of four things and does not include the remediation routing, its remediation rows are all phrased in terms of the wave the finding belongs to although a run-scope finding belongs to none, and its two statements about halting contradict each other — one makes a requirement-level finding an unconditional halt, the other reserves halting for when neither carry-forward path exists. The journal also omits each wave's diff base and head, which the preservation layer, the cross-wave decision, and the fixer scope all presuppose, and the recorded existing-module list has no reader at all.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Pipeline re-entry SHALL accept an explicit unaddressed-requirement scope and an explicit plan run identity, so a verification-driven re-entry does not re-run the whole plan.
- [x] AC-2: A re-entry SHALL be distinguishable from the run it re-enters at the event-emission layer, so the chain observes a fresh completion event instead of an idempotent skip, and a re-entry that executes no task SHALL NOT report completion.
- [x] AC-3: The all-wave final verification SHALL have its own remediation routing for run-scope findings, stating how such a finding reaches an executing wave and that the pass is re-run afterwards.
- [x] AC-4: The rule governing when a cross-wave finding halts the run SHALL be stated consistently in one form — halting reserved for when no carry-forward path exists — and a requirement-level finding SHALL have a stated route rather than only an unconditional halt.
- [x] AC-5: Wave entry SHALL collect every residual carried into that wave and SHALL include it in both the requirement-authoring input and the re-entry scope.
- [x] AC-6: A wave appended solely to receive carried findings SHALL have a defined design-layer denominator derived from the carried findings, so its verification denominators remain computable without a source-document range.
- [x] AC-7: Each wave's diff base and head commit SHALL be recorded on the journal, so the preservation layer, the cross-wave decision, and the fixer scope are reconstructible after a resume without conversation state.
- [x] AC-8: The recorded existing-module list SHALL have a declared consumer at a stated point in the flow rather than being written and never read.

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/skills/kiwi-wave-continuity-r2-content.test.ts | AC-1, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-8 | describes R2-H2, R2-H4, R2-H7 and R2-M13 assert over all three variants: R2-H2 pins --req-filter and --plan-run-id as their own kiwi-pipeline §1.2 option rows, pins the §7 Phase 4 hand-off line forwarding both together to kiwi-planner and kiwi-pm, and pins kiwi-planner accepting --plan-run-id so the forwarded option has a consumer (AC-1); R2-H4 pins the §5.6 run-scope routing sentence that appends wave-N+1 under the §0.5 exception instead of halting, the follow-up requiring §4~§5.5 execution and a §5.6 re-run, and the §5.5.7 rewrite that tries carry-forward first and reserves HALT for when both paths are impossible while keeping the HALT token unhedged (AC-3, AC-4); R2-H7 pins §4 collecting every residual whose carried_into is this wave into the authoring input and §5 including it in the pipeline re-entry scope, plus the §3.1 rule giving a carry-forward wave its design-layer denominator from the carried finding list (AC-5, AC-6); R2-M13 pins §5.5.2 making verifier 2 consume existing_modules for cross-wave regression risk and its §5.5.1 bundle row (AC-8). |

### FR-FLOW-061 — Cross-variant critical gate identifier parity across the wave chain

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.4 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/analysis/wave-fit-eval/round2-findings.md |

#### Requirement

When the same wave runs under a different shipped skill variant, the declared critical gate identifiers and their membership SHALL be identical across variants, and a gate a child skill declares critical SHALL be declared critical by every ancestor that could otherwise resolve it automatically.

#### Rationale

Round-2 evaluation findings H6 and M9. The shared unattended contract uses gate identifiers as interface keys, so a gate declared under one identifier by a child and absent from its parent's critical list falls through to the parent's default classification, where a sufficiently confident automated decision adopts it. That is precisely what happens to the gate forbidding an existing test from being weakened or deleted: the implementation skill declares it critical, the task runner's gate table and its unconditional-halt list omit it while listing the sibling contract-change gate, and no ancestor recovers the stop because the orchestrator halts only when a child returns needs-user — which the task runner never does once it has decided the matter itself. The one change that removes the regression safety net is therefore the one change an unattended run can approve on its own, and it is the least reversible. Independently, the shipped variants disagree on the identifiers themselves: the planner's declared gate identifiers differ entirely between two variants, and the implementation skill has rows present in one variant and absent from the other in both directions, so the same wave stops under one variant and continues under another.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The gate forbidding an existing test from being weakened or deleted SHALL appear in the task runner's critical gate table and in its unconditional-halt list, in every shipped variant.
- [x] AC-2: No gate declared critical by the implementation skill SHALL fall through to the task runner's automated business-decision classification.
- [x] AC-3: The planner's declared critical gate identifiers SHALL be identical across all shipped variants.
- [x] AC-4: The implementation skill's declared critical gate identifiers SHALL be identical across all shipped variants, with no rows present in one variant and absent from another.
- [x] AC-5: Every gate identifier the shared unattended contract consumes as an interface key SHALL resolve in every shipped variant.
- [x] AC-6: Variant-to-variant divergence in the declared gate identifier sets SHALL be detectable by an automated check rather than by manual reading.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/skills/kiwi-wave-continuity-r2-content.test.ts | AC-1, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | describe R2-H6 pins, in all three kiwi-pm variants, the §0.G7 existing-test-weakened-or-deleted row whose reason cell names kiwi-coder, and the always-HALT bullet carrying both 약화 and 삭제 unhedged, so the gate cannot fall to severity classification (AC-1). describe R2-M9 is the automated parity check: for each of the seven chain skills (kiwi-wave-master, kiwi-coder, kiwi-pm, kiwi-pipeline, kiwi-srs, kiwi-planner, kiwi-review-fix-loop) it compares the gate-id SET parsed from each variant's critical_gates table — set equality across claude/codex/etc, equality against the CANONICAL_GATE_IDS map pinned in the test, exactly-once declaration per gate, and absence of every RETIRED_GATE_IDS spelling. Set comparison rather than presence is what makes a variant carrying extra or missing halts fail, which is how divergence is detected mechanically instead of by reading (AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6). |
| VE-2 | test | test/skills/kiwi-wave-continuity-r3-content.test.ts | AC-2 | describe R3-H11 covers AC-2 over all three variants by asserting the general inheritance rule rather than per-gate transcription — the transcription approach is what left eight kiwi-coder critical ids without a kiwi-pm counterpart in the first place. The kiwi-pm §0.G7 section now carries a catch-all paragraph stating that a gate_id a child raises in its NEEDS_USER payload, when that id is in the child's own critical_gates list, is treated as an unconditional HALT even with no same-named row in kiwi-pm's table, and is not re-classified by severity (claude:101, codex:112, etc:105). The channel that carries the id exists on both ends: kiwi-coder's emit section adds a `gate_id` field on the NEEDS_USER payload (claude SKILL.md:860, codex references/extended-workflow.md:417, etc:377), and kiwi-pm §3.4 reads it and maps it to the same-named gate without severity re-classification (claude:515, codex:521, etc:511). The escalation payload's severity enum gains a fourth value `critical` (§3.2 return-format block, claude:472/codex:478/etc:468) with a matching §3.5 row whose action cell is an --auto-independent always-HALT naming existing-public-contract-change, existing-test-weakened-or-deleted and existing-file-deleted-or-moved (claude:525, codex:531, etc:521), so an escalated critical gate can no longer arrive as a category the parent may decide automatically. |

### FR-FLOW-062 — Producer wiring for wave verification windows and denominators

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.4 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/analysis/wave-fit-eval/round3-findings.md |

#### Requirement

When wave verification consumes a journal field, an evidence window, or a denominator, the run SHALL have a declared point at which that value is produced and recorded, so every consumed value is resolvable from the journal alone in a session that never saw the decomposition conversation.

#### Rationale

Round-3 evaluation findings H3, H9, M1, M4, M11 and L1. The verification layer names four things it reads from the journal and the run declares a producer for none of them. The wave diff window and the list of pipeline runs belonging to a wave appear only on the consuming side — the evidence bundle rows, the window-boundary prose, and the preservation denominator — while the recording section obliges only a single latest pipeline run identifier and the event contract carries both as optional fields whose only production account is an emit example, which is not normative. The orchestrator has itself ruled that a resumed session holds no decomposition conversation, so on resume neither value exists, the preservation denominator cannot be derived, and since zero unapproved damage is a pass condition, the verification becomes impossible to execute rather than merely weaker. The all-wave final pass inherits the same hole from the other side: it requires the same zero-damage condition and reuses a denominator defined as that wave's diff, but a run-scope pass has no wave and the event contract explicitly omits the diff window from run-scope events, so picking any wave's window turns a whole-run damage judgement into a re-check of one wave. Its design-layer denominator is likewise undefined for the run-scope event, because the fixing rule is written in terms of one wave's design items and the cross-wave integration items belong to no wave at all. Underneath both, the orchestrator pins an event-contract version older than the one that introduced every field it now consumes, and its own enumeration of verification keys predates the layers it requires, so a compliant producer reading the pinned version would emit none of them. Finally, a run that fails, is resumed, and then completes leaves its failure as the permanent terminal record, because the terminal emit is idempotent on a run identifier the resume deliberately reuses; and the unreachable-pass predicate compares remaining rounds against a streak requirement that is stated to not exist in the normal mode, so the predicate has no value to compare against.
- [2026-07-29] Additionally finding M20: the regression pass condition's primary clause is a subset test of failing tests against the pinned baseline, while an exit code of zero appears only as the degraded condition applying when baseline capture failed; an assertion matching either token indifferently no longer pins the primary clause, so the pass condition can be evidenced by the fallback while the delta rule goes unchecked.
- [2026-07-29] Finding M20 is specified by FR-FLOW-066 AC-7; the note above is retained as context only and adds no acceptance criterion here.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The wave diff window's base and head commits SHALL have declared capture points and a declared recording obligation, so the preservation-layer denominator is derivable in a session that holds no decomposition conversation.
- [x] AC-2: Every pipeline run spawned for a wave SHALL be appended to that wave's recorded run list at the time it is spawned, so the evidence window covers re-entry runs rather than only the most recent run.
- [x] AC-3: The journal fields that the wave-verification and completion events depend on SHALL be declared required on those events by normative text rather than being optional fields shown only in an emit example.
- [x] AC-4: The all-wave final verification SHALL have a declared preservation denominator whose diff window spans the whole run rather than one wave, and the event contract SHALL carry a run-scope field able to express it.
- [x] AC-5: The all-wave final verification's design-layer denominator SHALL have a declared fixing subject and item unit that covers the run-scope event, including the cross-wave integration items that belong to no single wave.
- [x] AC-6: The event-contract version the orchestrator pins SHALL be the version that defines the fields it consumes, and the orchestrator's enumeration of verification keys SHALL list every key it requires.
- [x] AC-7: A resumed orchestration that runs to completion SHALL record its terminal chain event, so a failure recorded by the interrupted attempt is not the run's permanent last word.
- [x] AC-8: The consecutive-clean streak requirement of each mode SHALL be stated as a number, so the unreachable-pass predicate that compares remaining rounds against it is evaluable in every mode.

### FR-FLOW-063 — Judgement symmetry between the execution layer and the verification layer

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.4 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/analysis/wave-fit-eval/round3-findings.md |

#### Requirement

When the execution layer and the verification layer judge the same change to existing structure or the same regression result, they SHALL apply the same resolution rule against the same baseline, and any behaviour one layer presupposes of the other SHALL be defined in that other layer.

#### Rationale

Round-3 evaluation findings H1, H2, M12, M13, M14 and M15. The two layers judge the same act by different rules, and the asymmetry runs in the direction that makes planned work impossible. Wave verification admits an evidence-based resolution — a move, deletion, or signature change backed by a requirement or a plan task is recorded as an intended improvement — but the implementation skill declares the same three acts unconditional critical gates that no automated decision may bypass and that state outright that listing the file in the plan does not resolve them. The result is that a refactor approved by the design, the requirements, and the plan still halts the run, so unattended completion holds only for designs that add files and never touch what exists. Inside the implementation skill the same event carries two mutually exclusive handlings: one section prescribes self-healing re-invocation, the other prescribes a halting gate, and whichever reading wins kills the other path — if the first, the gate never fires and the damage surfaces only as loop divergence; if the second, the repair route is dead. The task runner meanwhile defines its own protection in terms of a bubble-up from the child, and the child's document never uses that word, defines no such escalation, and has no field in its status mapping that could carry the gate identifier upward. On the verification side, the preservation judgement names no criteria of its own and inherits them only through a clause that governs the fixer, and assertion weakening is absent from the wave denominator altogether, so weakening performed inside an approved task passes the wave gate that was written precisely to not be satisfiable by a sub-loop's own pass. The claim that all layers share one regression baseline is likewise unsupported: the implementation skill and the review-fix skill each capture their own baseline at their own moment and neither has a field to receive the parent's, so a failure the current wave has just introduced is classified as pre-existing by the delegated loop and as new by the wave gate. The recorded existing-module list flows only into verification and never into authoring or planning, so the layer that creates the change never sees the structure the judging layer will hold it to. Finally two shipped variants of the review-fix skill declare the preservation scan in their clause list and gate table but wire it into no workflow step and no exit criterion, so the declaration has no execution point.
- [2026-07-29] Additionally findings H11 and H12: nine gate identifiers the implementation skill declares critical have no counterpart in the task runner, whose escalation severity vocabulary cannot express a critical gate at all, so each falls to the default automatically-decidable class — per-gate transcription already missed one sibling preservation gate. And a re-entry whose tasks are all already done reports completion having executed nothing.
- [2026-07-29] Findings H11 and H12 are specified by FR-FLOW-066 AC-1 through AC-4; the note above is retained as context only and adds no acceptance criterion here.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The implementation skill's existing-structure preservation gates SHALL admit the same evidence-based resolution the wave verification layer admits, so a move, deletion, or signature change required by a recorded requirement or plan task is recorded as an intended improvement rather than halting the run.
- [x] AC-2: A single detection of an existing-structure violation SHALL have exactly one declared handling, so a self-healing re-invocation path and a halting gate are not both prescribed for the same event.
- [x] AC-3: The escalation that the task runner presupposes of its child implementation skill SHALL be defined in that child, including the channel that carries the gate identifier to the parent.
- [x] AC-4: The wave-layer preservation judgement SHALL name the criteria it judges by, rather than reaching them only through a clause that governs the fixer.
- [x] AC-5: Weakening of an existing test assertion SHALL be inside the wave-layer preservation denominator, so a weakening performed inside an approved plan task is not admitted by the wave gate.
- [x] AC-6: The regression baseline SHALL be produced once and carried to every layer that judges against it, so a failure introduced by the current wave is not classified as pre-existing by a delegated loop that captured its own baseline.
- [x] AC-7: The recorded existing-module list SHALL be an input to requirement authoring and planning as well as to verification, so the layer that creates a change sees the structure the judging layer will hold it to.
- [x] AC-8: Every shipped variant of the review-fix skill SHALL wire its declared preservation scan into an executable workflow step and into its exit criteria, not only into its clause list and gate table.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/skills/kiwi-wave-continuity-r3-content.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7, AC-8 | All assertions run over all three shipped variants, following each variant's own emit location (kiwi-coder/kiwi-planner/kiwi-pm keep their emit sections in SKILL.md for claude and in references/extended-workflow.md at the same section number for codex/etc). R3-H1 requires a new kiwi-coder §0.20.4 row admitting evidence-based resolution — a REQ-ID or Task-ID in the sidecar whose action explicitly states the move, deletion or signature change — for existing-file-deleted-or-moved and existing-public-contract-change, recording the outcome as intended-improvement, while excluding §0.20.3 weakening and existing-test deletion from that resolution, and requires each of those two §0.G6 rows to point at §0.20.4 as its resolution path (AC-1). R3-H2 requires the §5.1(d) plan-code gate to bound self-healing: a second detection of the same item does not re-invoke (c) but bubbles up under the matching §0.G6 gate_id, so one detection has one handling rather than two mutually exclusive ones (AC-2); it also requires the kiwi-coder emit section to declare a gate_id field on the NEEDS_USER payload and kiwi-pm §3.4 to map that id to the same-named gate without severity re-classification, which is the channel the parent presupposed and the child did not define (AC-3). R3-M12 requires the §5.5.2 preservation denominator to name its four classes itself — deleted/moved existing files, deleted/changed existing public symbols, deleted/modified existing test files, and weakened existing-test assertions judged by kiwi-coder §0.20.3's closed list — rather than reaching them through the fixer clause (AC-4), and requires the rule that weakening is always unapproved-damage even with REQ or Task evidence (AC-5); it also requires the §5.5.2 line stating the judgement shares one rule with kiwi-coder §0.20.4. R3-M13 requires --regression-baseline to exist as a receiving option on kiwi-coder §1.2 and kiwi-review-fix-loop's input table with a parent-wins rule in each capture section, and as a pass-through row at every intermediate hop (kiwi-wave-master §7.4, kiwi-pipeline §7.4, kiwi-pm §1.3 and its child-args reproduction) (AC-6). R3-M14 requires kiwi-wave-master §4 to forward the wave's existing_modules as an authoring input to the requirement-authoring child, and a new kiwi-planner §0.22 convention row making that list an input to planning with move/delete/signature-change tasks obliged to state it in the task action (AC-7). R3-M15 requires each variant of kiwi-review-fix-loop to carry a dedicated preservation-scan section, to scan the fixer diff, to state unhedged that it runs before the prickly re-review, and to escalate a detection to CRITICAL through the three named gate ids (AC-8). |

### FR-FLOW-064 — Idempotency and scope of wave resume and stage re-entry

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.4 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/analysis/wave-fit-eval/round3-findings.md |

#### Requirement

When a wave run is resumed or a wave stage is re-entered, the run SHALL re-enter at the granularity at which it stopped, SHALL be distinguishable from the run it re-enters at every layer that keys on run identity, and SHALL reach every input it needs without replaying the stages it skips.

#### Rationale

Round-3 evaluation findings H4, H5, M6, M7, M8, M9 and M10. Re-entry is declared by one skill on behalf of others and contradicted everywhere else. The pipeline skill states that a re-entry uses a round-suffixed emit key and that its children apply the same convention, but both children still carry the plain rule that an event with an identical run identifier is skipped, the shared event contract knows nothing of the suffix, and the suffix character is outside the identifier syntax that one child validates and halts on. A re-entry therefore either emits nothing the chain can see, or emits something the child rejects; and when the child emits nothing, the journal shows two consecutive orchestrator events, which the chain's own self-recursion check turns into an error at the next wave entry. Resume has the mirror defect: the run's resume granularity stops at the stage, while the stage itself is always entered from feasibility, and the only place that requires a narrowing scope is the verification-driven re-entry, not the resume path. A run interrupted mid-stage therefore re-applies per-requirement stability mutations, re-authors a plan that is frozen on the same day and freshly identified on any other, and re-runs tasks already implemented — and the values that would let it re-enter narrowly are never written to the journal at the time the stage runs. The wave's target reaches the pipeline only as a side effect of the authoring stage, which the resume path is explicitly told to skip, so a resumed wave enters the pipeline with no established target. One declared pass-through option is accepted by the orchestrator and by the final consumer but is absent from the intermediate skill's pass-through table, so the chain is broken in the middle. The instruction to re-run feasibility for only the affected requirement has no corresponding argument, and feasibility performs per-requirement stability mutations, so the only executable reading is a full re-evaluation with side effects on requirements that were not in question. The phase description presents wave registration as a single batch while the execution and resume sections require it interleaved and consuming carried-forward input. And the loop that appends waves in response to findings has no upper bound at all — the round cap governs one verification loop, and whether its counter accumulates across final-verification re-runs is unstated — so unattended termination is not guaranteed.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The re-entry event key convention SHALL live in the shared event contract that every participating skill reads, so no child retains a plain-identifier skip rule that the convention contradicts.
- [x] AC-2: The re-entry event key SHALL be distinct from the run identifier that children validate syntactically, so a re-entry key cannot fail the identifier gate that would halt the child.
- [x] AC-3: A re-entry SHALL leave an emitted event sequence that the chain's self-recursion check accepts, so re-entry does not make the next wave entry an error.
- [x] AC-4: Resume into a pipeline stage SHALL carry the scope and the plan identity of the interrupted run, so resume does not re-apply per-requirement stability mutations, re-author the plan, or re-run completed tasks.
- [x] AC-5: The values that a stage-level resume needs SHALL be recorded on the journal while the stage runs, so a resume that skips earlier phases can still resolve them.
- [x] AC-6: The wave's pipeline target SHALL be established by an explicit hand-off rather than by a side effect of a stage that the resume path skips.
- [x] AC-7: Every option declared to propagate from the orchestrator to a descendant SHALL be accepted and forwarded by each intermediate skill on the declared path.
- [x] AC-8: Re-running the feasibility stage for a selected subset of requirements SHALL be expressible as an argument, so the declared narrow re-run does not require a whole-target re-evaluation with its per-requirement side effects.
- [x] AC-9: The phase description of the run SHALL agree with the interleaved order that the execution and resume sections require, and the loop that appends waves in response to findings SHALL have a declared upper bound whose accumulation across final-verification re-runs is stated.

### FR-FLOW-065 — Closed denominator-shrinking paths and declared stopping points

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.4 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/analysis/wave-fit-eval/round3-findings.md |

#### Requirement

When a wave run excludes an item from a verification denominator or reaches a point at which it cannot continue, that exclusion SHALL be re-examined by a declared check and that stopping point SHALL be a declared gate, so no item leaves every denominator on the run's own discretion and no stop is resolved by default classification.

#### Rationale

Round-3 evaluation findings H6, H7, H8, H10, M2, M3, M5, M16, M17 and L2. The run fixes three of its four verification denominators externally and then leaves open the cheapest ways to shrink all of them. A section recorded as out of scope satisfies the coverage gate, enters no wave's design items, enters no final denominator, and is covered by no re-examination clause, so a decomposition subagent writing one line of free-text reason removes that section from the entire run's verification with no user gate — the exact discretion the external fixing rule was written to remove. The constraint layer has the reverse hole: every consumer is present, but nothing says what is read to populate the constraint artifact or at what granularity items are extracted, and the unattended path closes the child's clarification loop, so in practice the artifact is always empty, the expected count is zero, and the layer can fail nothing. The same layer is also the only one absent from the per-round frozen denominator, so the row-count invalidation rule that protects the other three does not reach it, and the artifact the orchestrator is instructed to hand to the authoring child has no receiving argument on that child, which the same section elsewhere states is exactly what makes a hand-off ineffective. On the stopping side, ending a wave with unresolved findings before the cap is defined as a terminal verdict and calls for a user decision, but it is the one such ending absent from the critical gate list — against the run's own stated rule that partially declaring gates drops the rest to committee classification. That wave also cannot record a completion event, so the committee has no journal-legal option and the run returns to the same wave regardless of what it decides, leaving an interval with no defined stopping condition; and the treatment of residual medium findings is readable both as normal-mode early termination and as a user decision. The final pass reuses four things and not the remediation routing, so a wave-attributable finding at that pass halts although the routing that answers it already exists; a wave created only to receive carried findings has a design-layer interface stated three different ways across the orchestrator and the event contract; a declared pass-through option has no row in the natural-language option table, so it is propagatable but not specifiable; and rejection of an invalid loop count is a halt that no gate table declares.
- [2026-07-29] Additionally findings M18 and M19: the two consent gates are deliberately not passable by unattended mode alone and require their own explicit options, so what must be guaranteed is that they are reachable and that the cost is stated rather than silently granted. And the shared contract's gate catalogue still lists an identifier that every canonical set deliberately excludes, so a new skill can adopt a gate that resolves nowhere.
- [2026-07-29] Findings M18 and M19 are specified by FR-FLOW-066 AC-5 and AC-6; the note above is retained as context only and adds no acceptance criterion here. AC-9's option-table clause continues to cover M16, and its loop-count clause covers L2.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A section recorded as out of scope SHALL be re-examined by a declared check rather than leaving every denominator on a recorded reason alone, and that reason SHALL be drawn from a closed set of values.
- [x] AC-2: The declared user constraints SHALL have a declared collection step naming what is read and at what granularity items are extracted, so the constraint layer's denominator is not structurally always empty.
- [x] AC-3: The constraint layer's expected count SHALL be fixed externally from the constraint artifact and SHALL be part of the per-round frozen denominator, so the row-count invalidation rule reaches it as it reaches the other layers.
- [x] AC-4: The constraint artifact SHALL have a receiving argument on the requirement-authoring child, so the declared hand-off of that artifact has a consumer.
- [x] AC-5: Ending a wave with unresolved findings before the round cap SHALL be a declared gate, so it does not fall to the shared unattended contract's default classification at a point where the journal admits no completion record.
- [x] AC-6: The treatment of residual medium findings SHALL be stated as one branch, so normal-mode early termination and the user-decision route are not both readable for the same residual.
- [x] AC-7: The all-wave final verification SHALL reuse the remediation routing and the cross-wave rule for findings attributable to a wave, so such a finding has a route other than an unconditional halt.
- [x] AC-8: The design-layer interface of a wave created solely to receive carried findings SHALL be stated consistently across the orchestrator and the event contract, including which fields carry its denominator and its authoring input.
- [x] AC-9: Every option the pass-through chain declares SHALL have a row in the orchestrator's natural-language option table so it is specifiable and not only propagatable, and rejection of an invalid loop-count value SHALL either be a declared gate or fall outside a stated scope of the gate table.

R3-H6 requires three separate closures for an out-of-scope exclusion: a §0.G gate out-of-scope-user-consent that stops even under --auto when out_of_scope is non-empty, a §3.1 rule giving each item the closed exclusion_class enum (already-implemented / superseded / external-ownership / user-excluded / non-normative) so the exclusion is not a free-text reason, and a §5.6 line carrying out_of_scope in full into the final pass as its own layer where verifier 2 confirms each item's non-implementation was intended — the re-examination point the exclusion previously lacked (AC-1). ### FR-FLOW-066 — Parent inheritance of child-declared critical gates and refusal of no-op completion

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.4 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/analysis/wave-fit-eval/round3-findings.md |

#### Requirement

When a child skill escalates a gate that the child declares critical, the parent SHALL halt on that gate by a general inheritance rule rather than by per-gate transcription, a re-entry that changes no task state SHALL NOT report completion, and every gate identifier and pass condition the chain declares SHALL resolve to something a shipped skill actually enforces.

#### Rationale

Round-3 evaluation findings H11, H12, M18, M19 and M20. Two of these disable the protections that unattended execution rests on. Of the fifteen gates the implementation skill declares critical, nine have no counterpart in the task runner — including the file-deletion-or-move preservation gate that was itself added in the previous round alongside two siblings, one of which was transcribed and one of which was not. The shared unattended contract classifies any gate absent from the parent's critical list as an ordinary business decision, and the task runner adopts such a decision automatically above a confidence threshold, so each of those nine is approvable by an automated decision in an unattended run. The gate cannot even be expressed on the way up: the child's escalation payload types its severity as one of three values, none of which is critical, so the parent has no vocabulary in which to receive a critical gate. The previous round closed exactly one of these by hand, which is the evidence that per-gate transcription does not hold — the rule has to be general, keyed on the child's own declared set, or the next gate added will be missed the same way. Separately, the task runner reports completion when every task is done and the closing mutation succeeds, and a re-entry whose tasks are already all done satisfies that condition without executing anything; nothing anywhere in the skill tree forbids it. The improvement loop therefore re-enters, appears to succeed, changes nothing, and the next verification round raises the same finding, repeating until the round cap is spent. Alongside these, the shared contract's standard gate catalogue still lists an identifier that every canonical set deliberately excludes, so a new skill can adopt a gate that resolves nowhere; the two consent gates are deliberately not passable by unattended mode alone, which means what must be guaranteed is that they are reachable and that the cost of that choice is stated rather than silently granted; and the regression pass condition, whose primary clause is a subset test against the pinned baseline, can be evidenced by the exit-code token that belongs only to the degraded path taken when baseline capture failed, so the delta rule the condition is named for goes unchecked.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The parent SHALL halt on any escalated gate identifier that belongs to the child's own declared critical set, by a general rule that compares the escalated identifier against that set, so honouring a gate does not depend on it having been transcribed into the parent.
- [x] AC-2: The severity vocabulary of the child's escalation payload SHALL be able to express a critical gate, so an escalated critical gate is not received as a category the parent may decide automatically.
- [x] AC-3: No gate that the implementation skill declares critical SHALL be resolvable through the parent's automated decision path, including the existing-file-deleted-or-moved preservation gate and its siblings.
- [x] AC-4: A re-entry in which no task changes state SHALL NOT return the completion outcome, and SHALL instead return an outcome carrying the fact that no work was performed.
- [x] AC-5: The integration-test consent gate and the large-task cost gate SHALL be reachable through the orchestrator's pass-through option chain, and the orchestrator SHALL state that unattended mode alone does not pass them and that each requires its own explicit option.
- [x] AC-6: Every gate identifier listed in the shared unattended contract's standard catalogue SHALL be one that a shipped skill declares, and a withdrawn identifier SHALL be removed or annotated as withdrawn together with what replaced it.
- [x] AC-7: The regression pass condition SHALL pin its primary condition — no new failure relative to the pinned baseline — and its degraded condition — exit code alone when baseline capture failed — in distinct roles, so the pass condition cannot be evidenced by the degraded token while the primary condition goes unchecked.

R3-H11 asserts the general inheritance rule instead of nine transcribed rows: kiwi-pm's §0.G7 section must carry a paragraph stating that a gate_id in a child's NEEDS_USER payload, when that id belongs to the child's own critical_gates list, is an unconditional HALT even with no same-named row in kiwi-pm's table, and is not re-classified by severity (AC-1, AC-3). The same describe covers the payload vocabulary: the §3.2 return-format block's severity enum gains a fourth value critical alongside the existing three, and §3.5 gains a matching row whose action cell is an --auto-independent always-HALT naming existing-public-contract-change, existing-test-weakened-or-deleted and existing-file-deleted-or-moved by id, so the preservation gate added in round 2 and its siblings cannot reach the automated decision path (AC-2, AC-3). R3-H12 requires the kiwi-pm emit section to carry one line keyed on "상태가 바뀐 Task" = 0 건 that returns NEEDS_USER rather than TASK_DONE and states the no-op fact in the summary (AC-4). ### FR-FLOW-067 — recommended-marked gate option adopted with no committee under --auto

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [--auto contract decisions](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/07.auto-contract-decisions.md) |

#### Requirement

When `--auto` is active and a user-decision gate offers an option carrying the structured marker `recommended: true`, the shared `--auto` contract SHALL adopt that option immediately without convening a decision committee and without spawning any committee member, and SHALL rank the two committee bypasses as `recommended` over `default_if_auto` over the committee. The marker SHALL be a structured boolean field declared on `kiwi-pm`'s `NEEDS_USER` option schema alongside the existing `key`, `label` and `consequence` fields, opt-in and absent by default, and an unparsed prose `(권장)` label in skill text SHALL confer nothing. `auto-option.md` §3's prohibition on adopting a decision that is neither unanimous nor a plurality SHALL be reworded in the same change so that it admits a zero-vote fast-pathed adoption while continuing to forbid every other arbitrary adoption, and the reworded prohibition SHALL admit no lead-member tie-break.

#### Rationale

Charter §2 makes the recommended fast path the first half of the `--auto` change, and `07` §6 requires it to be authored as a new requirement rather than folded into `FR-FLOW-025`, so the zero-deliberation path carries its own acceptance criteria and its own evidence. The three constituent edits are `05` §12 rows 12.2 (§3's `:159` prohibition, which as written forbids the feature being added, so a compliant agent must HALT on every fast-pathed gate), 12.3 (precedence, since two bypasses with different scopes and no ranking is the likeliest next contradiction) and 12.4 (a structured field rather than a prose scan, because two of the three existing `(권장)` labels in `kiwi-pm` annotate a HALT option and a prose scan would auto-adopt a recommended HALT). The charter excludes the gate that judges why an option carries a recommendation, so the schema declares no field describing the motive.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All four `auto-option.md` copies — `skills/claude/_shared/kiwi/`, `skills/codex/_shared/kiwi/`, `skills/etc/_shared/kiwi/` and `.agents/skills/_shared/kiwi/` — state that under `--auto` a gate option carrying `recommended: true` is adopted immediately, with no committee convened and no committee member spawned.
- [x] AC-2: All four `auto-option.md` copies state the bypass precedence `recommended` > `default_if_auto` > committee, so that a gate whose option carries both markers resolves through the `recommended` branch and a gate carrying neither reaches the committee.
- [x] AC-3: `recommended` is declared as a structured boolean field on `kiwi-pm`'s `NEEDS_USER` option schema in all four `kiwi-pm` renderings, beside the existing `key`, `label` and `consequence` fields; it is absent by default, and the schema declares no field describing why an option is recommended.
- [x] AC-4: All four `auto-option.md` copies state that a prose `(권장)` label carries no machine meaning and is never parsed as a recommendation; the three existing `(권장)` labels in `kiwi-pm`, two of which annotate a HALT option, acquire no `recommended: true` marker through this change, so the fast path starts with no member in the existing suite.
- [x] AC-5: `auto-option.md` §3's ladder-violation prohibition is reworded in all four copies so that a zero-vote fast-pathed adoption is permitted, while adopting any decision that is neither a committee majority nor a declared `recommended` / `default_if_auto` bypass remains an immediate HALT; the reworded line admits no lead-member tie-break.
- [x] AC-6: Verification evidence for this requirement carries an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed copy at `~/.claude/skills/_shared/kiwi/auto-option.md` and recording that landing the four bundled copies changes no `--auto` behaviour on the machine until a reinstall, since the charter forbids running `speckiwi init` with this repository as its target root.

### FR-FLOW-068 — confidence-spread cross-check folded into the confidence-threshold mechanism

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [--auto contract decisions](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/07.auto-contract-decisions.md) |

#### Requirement

The shared `--auto` contract SHALL fold `auto-option.md` §4.1.2's committee confidence cross-check into §4.1.1's confidence-threshold mechanism: a spread of 0.3 or more between the highest and the lowest member confidence SHALL adjust the adopted decision's confidence downward, and only a resulting confidence below the severity threshold in force SHALL escalate the gate to `critical`. Confidence spread alone SHALL never trigger a re-vote, an added committee member, or an escalation of committee size.

#### Rationale

`07` §4.1 records this as the largest item in the `--auto` change, larger than the voting change itself, with two silent failure modes if the rule is left as written. With no rung left anywhere, every committee is terminal, so `escalate one rung` degenerates into an immediate critical HALT on any spread of 0.3 or more — members self-reporting 0.9 and 0.6 is routine, making the mode the user asked to lighten strictly more halting than before. Left alongside a rung, spread correlates with vote splits and the rung becomes a general escalation-on-disagreement path, restoring the ladder the user deleted through the back door. `08` §2 carries this fold verbatim as one of the two conditions of the `leave-all-three` re-decision, which is why it may not be dropped as an optimisation (see the requirement recording that boundary). `05` §12 edit 12.1.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: In all four `auto-option.md` copies, §4.1.2 contains no instruction to escalate one rung, add committee members, or re-vote on confidence spread; no 3-to-5 or 5-to-7 escalation text survives in that section in any copy.
- [x] AC-2: In all four `auto-option.md` copies §4.1.2 supplies no confidence adjustment of its own and no re-vote: the spread is recorded in the decision audit row and drives nothing. The governing confidence is the minimum within the winning bloc after §4.1.1's per-member adjustments — the whole committee when the vote is unanimous — and escalation to `critical` fires only when that one value falls below the severity threshold in force, 0.5 for `clarification` and 0.7 for `business-decision`, each raised by §4.1.3's +0.1 lower-tier model adjustment when it applies.
- [x] AC-3: In all four copies, a unanimous committee whose members report a spread of 0.3 or more but whose governing confidence stays at or above the threshold adopts its decision and does not halt; the pre-change text escalated exactly this case to a critical HALT at terminal committee size.
- [x] AC-4: Verification evidence for this requirement carries an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed copy at `~/.claude/skills/_shared/kiwi/auto-option.md` and recording that landing the four bundled copies changes no `--auto` behaviour on the machine until a reinstall, since the charter forbids running `speckiwi init` with this repository as its target root.

### FR-FLOW-069 — governing confidence under a split vote is the minimum within the winning bloc

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [--auto contract decisions](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/07.auto-contract-decisions.md) |

#### Requirement

When an `--auto` decision committee adopts an option by a split (non-unanimous) vote, the confidence compared against the severity threshold SHALL be the minimum §4.1.1-adjusted confidence among the members who voted for the winning option. It SHALL NOT be the mean of the winning bloc, the minimum or mean across all members, or the lead member's confidence, and §4.1.1's adjustments SHALL be applied to each member's reported confidence before the minimum is taken.

#### Rationale

`auto-option.md` gates `business-decision` on confidence at or above 0.7. Under the unanimity rule `the confidence` was unambiguous; under a 2-1 it is undefined, and `07` §3a pins it to the conservative reading so that §4.1.1's x0.7 penalty for an empty `side_effects[]` on a mutation gate keeps biting on exactly the irreversible and externally visible gates that the `leave-all-three` decision leaves committee-decided, and so that a 2-1 carried by one confident and one hesitant member escalates to `critical` rather than shipping. `08` §2 carries this verbatim as one of the two conditions of that re-decision; one committee member stated their vote flips to promoting the gates without it. `05` §12 edit 12.7.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All four `auto-option.md` copies state that under a split vote the governing confidence is the minimum within the winning bloc, taken after §4.1.1's adjustments.
- [x] AC-2: A `business-decision` gate decided 2-1 whose two winning members carry adjusted confidences of 0.9 and 0.6 escalates to `critical` rather than adopting, because the governing value is 0.6 and the threshold is 0.7.
- [x] AC-3: The same gate decided 2-1 whose two winning members carry adjusted confidences of 0.9 and 0.75 adopts, and the dissenting member's confidence — whatever its value — does not enter the comparison.
- [x] AC-4: §4.1.1's x0.7 adjustment for a mutation gate with an empty `side_effects[]` is preserved verbatim in all four copies and is applied to each member's confidence before the minimum within the winning bloc is taken.
- [x] AC-5: Verification evidence for this requirement carries an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed copy at `~/.claude/skills/_shared/kiwi/auto-option.md` and recording that landing the four bundled copies changes no `--auto` behaviour on the machine until a reinstall, since the charter forbids running `speckiwi init` with this repository as its target root.

### FR-FLOW-070 — --auto decision audit log records zero-vote adoptions and the restated merge-method vocabulary

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [--auto contract decisions](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/07.auto-contract-decisions.md) |

#### Requirement

The `--auto` decision audit log at `docs/analysis/{run-id}/auto_decisions.json` SHALL record every adoption, including the two zero-vote bypasses, and its `merge_method.rule` vocabulary SHALL be exactly the three literals `majority`, `recommended-fastpath` and `default-if-auto`. A `recommended`-marked adoption SHALL be recorded as `{"rule": "recommended-fastpath", "committee_size": 0, "marked_by": "<gate declaration site>"}` and a `default_if_auto` adoption as `{"rule": "default-if-auto", "committee_size": 0, "marked_by": "<gate declaration site>"}`. `unanimous` SHALL be recorded as an outcome and never as a rule, `plurality` SHALL be renamed `majority`, and `tie-break-lead` SHALL be removed from the vocabulary.

#### Rationale

`07` §4.5 records that a fast-pathed decision has zero votes, no confidence and no committee size, so without an explicit shape it is not recorded at all — the decisions that received no deliberation would be precisely the ones leaving no evidence. `05` §12 edit 12.5 restates the vocabulary in full rather than extending it: `default-if-auto` needs its own literal because edit 12.3 ranks both bypasses above the committee and `adopt-default-if-auto` is already a member of §10.1's closed `AutoGateAction`, so with only the fast-path literal that branch would have no recordable outcome or would reuse `recommended-fastpath` and destroy the distinction; `plurality` becomes `majority` because that is what the simple-majority rule, `AutoGateAction`'s `adopt-majority` and `09` §9.2's two worked lock records all write; and `tie-break-lead` is deleted because the row that produces it is removed.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: In all four `auto-option.md` copies, §10's `merge_method.rule` vocabulary enumerates exactly the three literals `majority`, `recommended-fastpath` and `default-if-auto`, and neither `plurality` nor `tie-break-lead` appears as a `merge_method.rule` value in any copy.
- [x] AC-2: A `recommended`-marked adoption writes `{"rule": "recommended-fastpath", "committee_size": 0, "marked_by": "<gate declaration site>"}` and carries no committee vote entries.
- [x] AC-3: A `default_if_auto` adoption writes `{"rule": "default-if-auto", "committee_size": 0, "marked_by": "<gate declaration site>"}` and carries no committee vote entries, so the two bypasses ranked above the committee are distinguishable in the log.
- [x] AC-4: A committee-decided adoption writes `{"rule": "majority", "committee_size": 3}` under `--auto` and `{"rule": "majority", "committee_size": 5}` under `--auto --max`, with one `committee_votes[]` entry per participating member.
- [x] AC-5: The word `unanimous` appears in the §10 schema only as a description of a vote outcome and never as a `merge_method.rule` value, in all four copies.
- [x] AC-6: Verification evidence for this requirement carries an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed copy at `~/.claude/skills/_shared/kiwi/auto-option.md` and recording that landing the four bundled copies changes no `--auto` behaviour on the machine until a reinstall, since the charter forbids running `speckiwi init` with this repository as its target root.

### FR-FLOW-071 — etc local-LLM profile committee carve-out preserved through the --auto rewrite

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [--auto contract decisions](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/07.auto-contract-decisions.md) |

#### Requirement

The `--auto` contract change SHALL preserve, verbatim in substance, the `etc` local-LLM profile's carve-out of the `--auto` decision committee from the `etc` variant's `--max` default and from its single-worker policy. The carve-out lives in `skills/etc/_shared/kiwi/local-llm-profile.md`, which is the one rendering where that file exists, and this change SHALL NOT create the file in the `claude`, `codex` or `.agents` renderings.

#### Rationale

`local-llm-profile.md` makes the `etc` rendering behave as if `--max` were enabled by default, but explicitly carves the `--auto` committee out of that default so the committee stays at 3 members under `--auto` and 5 under `--auto --max`. `07` §4.7 records that if the carve-out is lost in the rewrite, every `etc` host silently jumps to a 5-member sequential committee on every gate — sequential because the same file disables multi-worker fanout. `05` §12 edit 12.8 attaches the path qualifier deliberately: the first revision cited the file unqualified while §12's preamble instructed `all four copies` for every row, which would have sent an implementer looking for three files that do not exist.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: After the change, `skills/etc/_shared/kiwi/local-llm-profile.md` still states that the `etc` `--max` default and the single-worker policy govern the verification/evaluation loop only, that the `--auto` committee is sized by `auto-option.md` at 3 members for `--auto` and 5 for `--auto --max`, and that the `etc` `--max` default does not force the committee to 5.
- [x] AC-2: The file still states that the `--auto` decision committee is a scoped exception to the single-worker policy: on hosts that cannot spawn members in parallel the members run sequentially while the committee size, voting and merge logic from `auto-option.md` are preserved.
- [x] AC-3: No `local-llm-profile.md` is created under `skills/claude/_shared/kiwi/`, `skills/codex/_shared/kiwi/` or `.agents/skills/_shared/kiwi/` by this change, so the file remains present in exactly one rendering.
- [x] AC-4: No sentence in `skills/etc/_shared/kiwi/local-llm-profile.md` states or implies a committee size other than 3 under `--auto` and 5 under `--auto --max`, and none of them describes a committee-size escalation rung.
- [x] AC-5: Verification evidence for this requirement carries an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed `etc`-rendering copy of `local-llm-profile.md` that still holds the pre-change text, and recording that landing the bundled copy changes no `--auto` behaviour on an `etc` host until a reinstall, since the charter forbids running `speckiwi init` with this repository as its target root.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/skills/auto-contract-etc-carveout.fr-flow-071.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4 | 4/4 pass (npx vitest run --no-file-parallelism, 2026-08-01). AC-1 asserts skills/etc/_shared/kiwi/local-llm-profile.md states the etc --max default and single-worker policy govern the verification/evaluation loop only, that a 320-char window around auto-option.md carries 3 members, 5 members and --auto together, and that it states the etc --max default does not force the committee to 5. AC-2 asserts the phrase scoped exception occurs and that a window around sequential carries the committee together with size, voting or merge and a preserv- stem. AC-3 asserts the file exists at the etc path and, over LOCAL_LLM_PROFILE_ABSENT_FROM, that no local-llm-profile.md exists under the claude, codex or .agents renderings. AC-4 splits the file into sentences, keeps those naming the committee, and asserts the set of member counts stated about the committee is exactly 3 and 5, that no such sentence matches RUNG, that the carve-out points at auto-option.md's simple-majority rule, and that it still states --max raises the committee and is not a no-op. |
| VE-2 | inspection | skills/etc/_shared/kiwi/local-llm-profile.md | AC-5 | Runtime-lag note, measured 2026-08-01 on this machine. AC-5 asks for the installed etc-rendering copy of local-llm-profile.md to be named. That copy does not exist: no installed etc rendering exists on this machine, so there is no installed copy of this file to name and naming one would be a fabrication. Measured: the installed skill roots under the home directory are .agent-kernel, .agents, .claude, .codex, .junie and .understand-anything-plugin; only .claude/skills/_shared/kiwi and .codex/skills/_shared/kiwi hold the shared kiwi modules (7 files each: auto-option.md, feasibility-policy-schema-v1.md, loop-option.md, pipeline-event.md, pipeline-v1.md, waves-event.md, workmode-policy.md), .agents/skills/_shared/kiwi holds only mini-option.md and pipeline-event.md, and the other three roots hold no _shared/kiwi at all. A find over all six skill roots to depth 4 returned no local-llm-profile.md anywhere, which is consistent with AC-3: the file exists in the etc rendering only, and the etc rendering is not installed here. Runtime lag therefore remains expected in the same sense as the rest of this family: landing the bundled etc copy changes no --auto behaviour on an etc host until that host reinstalls, and this machine is not such a host. docs/spec/00.charter.md:303-304 forbids running speckiwi init, skills install or upgrade --apply with this repository as the target root, so the lag is not closed by installing. |

### FR-FLOW-072 — no-majority and degraded-quorum committees escalate to critical and halt

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [scope and frame decisions](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/08.scope-and-frame-decisions.md) |

#### Requirement

When an `--auto` decision committee produces no majority, or when its quorum is degraded because a member failed after its single re-spawn, the shared `--auto` contract SHALL escalate the gate to `critical` and halt for the user. No members SHALL be added, no re-vote SHALL be held, and no lead member SHALL settle the outcome by ranking. `decideAutoGate` SHALL take a `tieRung: boolean` construction parameter whose shipped construction is `false`, so that a later reversal of this decision is additive within the function rather than structural.

#### Rationale

`08` §2 drops the tie rung 5-0 and rules that a gate with no majority escalates to `critical` and halts, honouring the charter's removal of the unanimity requirement and the 3-to-5 ladder literally. `07` §4.6 supplies the degraded-quorum half: with 3 members and one dropped, two remain and a 1-1 among them has no majority, so the old rule fell straight to the lead member and quietly became single-agent decision-making. `05` §12 edit 12.6 makes the degraded-quorum row escalate to `critical` unconditionally and deletes §3.1's lead-member row, because on its re-spawn-succeeds branch that row settles a tie by one member's ranking — the mechanism `08` §2 abolished — so closing only the quorum row would leave it live one row below in the same table. The `tieRung` parameter is retained per `08:76-77` and ships `false`; `08` §3's serial scope is what retires the rung's strongest counter-argument, since with no concurrent lanes a halt costs one prompt in a session the user is already attending.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: In all four `auto-option.md` copies, a committee that produces no majority — including a 1-1-1 among 3 members under `--auto` and any tie among 5 members under `--auto --max` — escalates the gate to `critical` and halts for the user, with no rung that adds members and no re-vote.
- [x] AC-2: In all four copies, §3.1's degraded-quorum row escalates to `critical` unconditionally: the branch that drops the failed member and proceeds while a majority quorum remains is removed, so a member that fails after its one re-spawn halts the gate.
- [x] AC-3: In all four copies, §3.1's lead-member row — the senior member's single re-spawn that settles a tie by one member's ranking on its success branch — is deleted, and no lead-member tie-break mechanism survives anywhere in the contract text.
- [x] AC-4: `decideAutoGate` accepts a `tieRung: boolean` construction parameter, and a test asserts that every shipped construction and call site passes `false`.
- [x] AC-5: With `tieRung` constructed `false`, `decideAutoGate` returns `escalate-critical` both for a vote with no majority and for a degraded quorum, and never returns `add-two-and-revote`.
- [x] AC-6: Verification evidence for this requirement carries an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed copy at `~/.claude/skills/_shared/kiwi/auto-option.md` and recording that landing the four bundled copies changes no `--auto` behaviour on the machine until a reinstall, since the charter forbids running `speckiwi init` with this repository as its target root.

### FR-FLOW-073 — committee-decided gate population bounded by the two carried --auto conditions

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [scope and frame decisions](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/08.scope-and-frame-decisions.md) |

#### Requirement

The `--auto` contract change SHALL promote no gate into any skill's `critical_gates[]`, leaving the irreversible and externally visible non-critical gates decided by a committee majority; and the boundary on that committee-decided population SHALL be the two conditions carried verbatim from the decision that left them there — that the governing confidence under a split vote is the minimum within the winning bloc after the §4.1.1 adjustments, and that §4.1.2's confidence-spread cross-check is folded into §4.1.1's threshold mechanism with no re-vote on spread alone. Neither condition SHALL be dropped while those gates remain committee-decided.

#### Rationale

`08` §2 re-decided rather than inherited the `leave-all-three` answer, because `07` §3b had made it conditional on a tie rung that `08` then dropped. It stays `leave-all-three` because halting for the user removes §3b's named unacceptable outcome — settlement by one member alone — more completely than the rung did, while promoting the three gates to `critical_gates[]` would halt on every invocation including a unanimous 3-0, a larger deviation from the charter than the rung was. `07:88-92` states that the decision named a sample and not a boundary: any gate with no declared severity is classified `business-decision`, so the affected population is open-ended by design, and the real boundary is `critical_gates[]` membership plus the two carried conditions. This requirement exists so the SRS records why those two conditions may not be dropped as independent optimisations.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: No gate identifier is added to any skill's `critical_gates[]` table by the `--auto` contract change.
- [x] AC-2: The three irreversible or externally visible non-critical gates named in the impact study remain `business-decision` and committee-decided after the change: `kiwi-srs-sync`'s `apply-selected`, `kiwi-commit-auto-push`'s `Closes` versus `Refs` trailer choice, and `kiwi-commit-auto-pr`'s existing-PR-body preserve versus `--update-pr-body` choice.
- [x] AC-3: All four `auto-option.md` copies carry both conditions in their contract text: the minimum-within-the-winning-bloc rule for a split vote, and the fold of the confidence-spread cross-check into the threshold mechanism with no re-vote on spread alone.
- [x] AC-4: All four `auto-option.md` copies preserve the rule that a gate with no declared severity and no `critical_gates[]` row is classified `business-decision`, so the committee-decided population remains open-ended and is bounded by the two conditions rather than by an enumerated gate list.
- [x] AC-5: The requirement's rationale and its SRS Change Notes state that the two conditions are conditions of leaving those gates committee-decided, so that removing either one requires re-deciding that question rather than being recorded as an optimisation.
- [x] AC-6: Verification evidence for this requirement carries an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed copy at `~/.claude/skills/_shared/kiwi/auto-option.md` and recording that landing the four bundled copies changes no `--auto` behaviour on the machine until a reinstall, since the charter forbids running `speckiwi init` with this repository as its target root.

### FR-FLOW-074 — kiwi-orchestrator skill in three variants, verb-indexed with a three-column critical gates table

| Field | Value |

| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [auto contract decisions](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/07.auto-contract-decisions.md) |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL ship a `kiwi-orchestrator` skill in all three bundled variants — `skills/claude/`, `skills/codex/` and `skills/etc/` — whose body is verb-indexed so that every member of the closed verb enum has exactly one `§V.<verb>` section and every `§V.<verb>` section names an enum member, and which declares `critical_gates[]` as a three-column `gate_id | reason | location` table; the `gate_id` set and the `§V.<verb>` section set SHALL be identical across the three variants, and the declared gate ids SHALL be the phase-1 gates only, because an undeclared `critical_gates[]` disables `--auto` while a declared gate whose predicate does not exist puts a live identifier into the parity assertions with nothing behind it.

#### Rationale

05 §4.4 makes the verb enum closed and gives each verb one skill section so a resumed agent reads one section rather than a 600-line document; §13 requires the three-column `gate_id | reason | location` shape that `auto-option.md:252` demands, records that `auto-option.md:237` disables `--auto` on an undeclared table, and splits the gate table by phase with the stated reason that declaring a gate whose machinery does not exist is worse than omitting it. §10.3 asserts cross-variant parity for the `critical_gates[]` identifiers per FR-FLOW-061 and for the verb enum, so a verb present in one variant and absent from another fails. §10.5 step 8 authors the SKILL.md last, so every sentence cites a verb that already resolves.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `skills/claude/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md`, `skills/codex/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md` and `skills/etc/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md` each exist and are non-empty when read through the ENOENT-to-empty-string reader, so a missing variant fails as an assertion rather than passing vacuously.
- [x] AC-2: In each variant, every member of the closed verb enum has exactly one `§V.<verb>` section and every `§V.<verb>` section names a member of that enum — no unindexed verb and no orphan section.
- [x] AC-3: Each variant declares `critical_gates[]` as a table with exactly the three columns `gate_id`, `reason` and `location`; a variant whose table carries two columns, or which declares no table at all, fails.
- [x] AC-4: The set of declared `gate_id` values and the set of `§V.<verb>` section names are set-equal across the three variants; an identifier present in one variant and absent from another fails.
- [x] AC-5: The declared `gate_id` set contains only phase-1 gates: no phase-2 gate identifier — for example `lane-lease-breach`, `lane-timeout`, `lane-workspace-dirty` or `isolation-profile-unavailable` — appears in any variant's `critical_gates[]`.
- [ ] AC-6: Every normative sentence this requirement adds to the skill body, counted per variant under the bilingual modal set, has a mutation-ledger row with at least one red mutation from a named class, and every modal carrier in those sentences is covered by a ledger row's carrier anchor.
- [x] AC-7: This requirement's verification evidence records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed copy under `~/.claude/skills/` that still holds the pre-change text, because the charter forbids running `speckiwi init`, `speckiwi skills install` or `speckiwi upgrade --apply` against this repository, so no reinstall is available here.
