### FR-FLOW-075 — Resumed session reads the run contract, preflights, resumes and performs only next_action.verb

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The `kiwi-orchestrator` skill body SHALL, in all three variants, open with the fixed numbered resume procedure as the only part of the skill a resumed session needs before it can act: read `docs/research/{work}/00.run-contract.md`, or with no `{work}` read the `work_root` out of the resume card; run `speckiwi orchestrate preflight` with the MCP workspace root and the git toplevel; run `speckiwi orchestrate resume`; then, when blocking, perform only `next_action.verb` and nothing else, otherwise read the `§V.<next_action.verb>` section only and the two or three artifacts that section names; and it SHALL state as a normative rule that a resumed session never reconstructs run state from conversation.

#### Rationale

05 §11.1 states the procedure as a fixed, numbered sequence and requires preflight to precede resume, because resolving the journal at a mismatched root pins the run to the wrong repository — the ordering rule at `kiwi-wave-master:129`. §11.3 records why the existing contract does not survive: `kiwi-wave-master`'s resume rules are 573 lines of prose and nothing in `src/` can read `waves.jsonl`, so 'resume correctly' means 'read a very large document correctly while low on context', which is exactly the operation compaction impairs. Reading one verb section rather than the whole body is what makes the contract survivable.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Each variant's body carries the numbered procedure as its first operative content, in the order run contract, preflight, resume, verb, with the order provable by section scoping rather than by proximity alone.
- [x] AC-2: Each variant states that `orchestrate preflight` runs before `orchestrate resume`, and states the reason that the run-root check must precede journal path resolution.
- [x] AC-3: Each variant states that with no `{work}` the resumed session takes `work_root` out of the resume card rather than guessing a path.
- [x] AC-4: Each variant states that on a blocking result the session performs only `next_action.verb` and nothing else, and otherwise reads only the `§V.<next_action.verb>` section plus the artifacts that section names.
- [x] AC-5: Each variant states as a normative sentence that a resumed session never reconstructs state from conversation, and that sentence carries at least one red mutation from a named class in the mutation ledger.
- [ ] AC-6: Every normative sentence this requirement adds to the skill body, counted per variant under the bilingual modal set, has a mutation-ledger row with at least one red mutation from a named class, and every modal carrier in those sentences is covered by a ledger row's carrier anchor.
- [x] AC-7: This requirement's verification evidence records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed copy under `~/.claude/skills/` that still holds the pre-change text, because the charter forbids running `speckiwi init`, `speckiwi skills install` or `speckiwi upgrade --apply` against this repository, so no reinstall is available here.

### FR-FLOW-076 — Write-ahead intent and write-behind result journal lines per verb

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The `kiwi-orchestrator` skill body SHALL, in all three variants, require every verb to perform three writes in a fixed order — a write-ahead `intent` journal line, then the verb, then a write-behind `result` journal line — followed by a full resume-card rewrite, with every journal write going through `speckiwi orchestrate journal append` rather than a hand-rolled JSONL append; it SHALL state the resume invariant that for the current run the last line for each `(verb, wave, lane)` key must be a `result`, so an `intent` with no matching `result` means that verb was interrupted; and it SHALL require that an interrupted `externally-visible` verb runs that verb's declared inspection before re-entering and raises `interrupted-external-action`, which halts even under `--auto`, only when that inspection leaves the external effect unresolved.

#### Rationale

05 §4.3 fixes the per-verb write discipline and the invariant a resumed session evaluates first, and records that both journal writes go through a tool so the agent never hand-rolls a JSONL append. §4.4 declares the three recovery classes `pure-reauthor`, `idempotent-by-key` and `externally-visible` as a closed enum and states that the first two are simply redone with no gate while the third runs its named inspection first. §13's `interrupted-external-action` row and §4.4 both scope the gate to the case where the inspection leaves the effect unresolved, because a committee cannot know what a half-finished merge did. The dispatch rule is deliberately weaker than revision 1's: this repository has a reproduced failure in which background teammate names are runtime-session-scoped, so after a resume every previously spawned agent is unreachable while still holding its worktree, and re-dispatching puts two agents on one branch.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Each variant states the four-step write discipline in order — append `intent`, perform the verb, append `result`, rewrite the card — and the ordered pair `intent` before the verb and `result` after it carries a swapped-ordering-pair red mutation in the ledger.
- [x] AC-2: Each variant states that journal lines are written through the `orchestrate journal append` tool and that the agent never hand-appends to `kiwi/waves.jsonl`.
- [x] AC-3: Each variant states the invariant that for the current run the last line for each `(verb, wave, lane)` key must be a `result`, and that an `intent` with no matching `result` means the verb was interrupted.
- [x] AC-4: Each variant declares all three recovery classes by name — `pure-reauthor`, `idempotent-by-key`, `externally-visible` — and states that the first two are redone with no gate.
- [x] AC-5: Each variant states that an interrupted `externally-visible` verb runs its own declared inspection before re-entering, that `interrupted-external-action` is raised only when the inspection leaves the external effect unresolved, and that this gate halts even under `--auto`.
- [ ] AC-6: Every normative sentence this requirement adds to the skill body, counted per variant under the bilingual modal set, has a mutation-ledger row with at least one red mutation from a named class, and every modal carrier in those sentences is covered by a ledger row's carrier anchor.
- [x] AC-7: This requirement's verification evidence records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed copy under `~/.claude/skills/` that still holds the pre-change text, because the charter forbids running `speckiwi init`, `speckiwi skills install` or `speckiwi upgrade --apply` against this repository, so no reinstall is available here.

### FR-FLOW-077 — Intake source classification, parallel investigators and user QnA for unclosed gaps

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The `kiwi-orchestrator` skill body SHALL, in all three variants, specify Phase 1 intake as three steps: classify the source into exactly one of a thin intent, a research or design document, or a GitHub issue; run three investigators in parallel over the existing code, with the stances intent, code-context and architecture-fit; and enumerate the gaps, putting to the user as QnA every gap the investigators cannot close, with the intake record written to `01.intake.md` where loop D's verifiers read it.

#### Rationale

05 §2.1 Phase 1 fixes 1.a source classification, 1.b's three parallel investigators and 1.c's gap enumeration into user QnA, and §4.4 gives each intake source its own verb — `intake-qna`, `intake-document`, `intake-issue` — plus `intake-investigate`. §3.1 records `01.intake.md` as Phase 1.b/1.c's artifact, read by the loop D verifiers, and §7.2 makes its unresolved items loop D's open-question denominator. `00.charter.md:54` names asking the user questions where the design is still weak as the one place the user is named explicitly.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Each variant names the three accepted intake sources as a closed classification — thin intent, a research or design document, and a GitHub issue — and maps each to its own verb.
- [x] AC-2: Each variant states that the investigation runs three investigators in parallel and names all three stances: intent, code-context and architecture-fit.
- [x] AC-3: Each variant states that gap enumeration puts to the user, as QnA, every gap the investigators cannot close.
- [x] AC-4: Each variant states that the intake record is written to `01.intake.md` and that its unresolved items are what loop D's open-question denominator is computed from.
- [ ] AC-5: Every normative sentence this requirement adds to the skill body, counted per variant under the bilingual modal set, has a mutation-ledger row with at least one red mutation from a named class — including a weakened-quantifier mutation on the three-investigator sentence — and every modal carrier in those sentences is covered by a ledger row's carrier anchor.
- [x] AC-6: This requirement's verification evidence records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed copy under `~/.claude/skills/` that still holds the pre-change text, because the charter forbids running `speckiwi init`, `speckiwi skills install` or `speckiwi upgrade --apply` against this repository, so no reinstall is available here.

| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The `kiwi-orchestrator` skill body SHALL, in all three variants, require the run's design document `design/00.design.md` to be authored in English at body scope — never routed through a step directory by `tdd` mode — with every normative statement marked as a top-level list row beginning `[D-nnn]` under a lowest-level heading and integration items marked `[I-nnn]`, ids unique and contiguous and never reused across a run, each item carrying exactly one normative token occurrence with `MUST NOT` counted once, and blockquote and fenced-code content excluded from both scans; it SHALL state that a paragraph under a lowest-level heading carrying a normative token outside a marked row raises `unmarked-normative-prose`, a critical gate naming the exact lines rather than a warning; and it SHALL forbid any implementation verb from running before `P-DESIGN-FROZEN` holds, raising `design-not-frozen` otherwise.

#### Rationale

05 §3.3 applies the boundary rule — the agent marks the items and the tool validates the marking — because classifying normative prose against illustration is a semantic judgment `freeze` must not be asked to make. Rule 4 makes unmarked normative prose a critical gate rather than a warning with the stated reason that the miss is silent and the count is load-bearing: an under-counted `design_items[]` shrinks every frozen denominator in loops D, W, P and F while `invariant_digest` reports no drift, so nothing else in the design can catch it. §4.1 defines `P-DESIGN-FROZEN` as `frozen.design_lock` naming a lock whose recomputed digest matches, §13 attaches `design-not-frozen` to any implementation verb attempted before it, and §14 registration 8 records the orchestrator's own wave flow as body scope so `tdd` mode's step-scoped routing never re-routes it.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Each variant states that `design/00.design.md` is written in English and is body-scope work, and that `tdd` mode's step-scoped routing does not re-route the orchestrator's own wave flow.
- [x] AC-2: Each variant states the marking rule: a design item is a top-level list row beginning `[D-nnn]` under a lowest-level heading, integration items use `[I-nnn]`, ids are unique and contiguous and are never reused across a run.
- [x] AC-3: Each variant states that every marked item contains exactly one normative token occurrence, with `MUST NOT` counted once and not twice, so an item with none is rejected and an item with two is split.
- [x] AC-4: Each variant states that blockquote and fenced-code content is excluded from both the item scan and the unmarked-prose scan.
- [x] AC-5: Each variant states that a paragraph under a lowest-level heading carrying a normative token outside a `[D-nnn]` or `[I-nnn]` row raises `unmarked-normative-prose` as a critical gate that names the exact lines, and that the only remedies are marking the line or rewriting it.
- [x] AC-6: Each variant states that no implementation verb runs before `P-DESIGN-FROZEN` holds and that `design-not-frozen` fires otherwise, and the ordered pair freeze-then-implement carries a swapped-ordering-pair red mutation in the ledger.
- [ ] AC-7: Every normative sentence this requirement adds to the skill body, counted per variant under the bilingual modal set, has a mutation-ledger row with at least one red mutation from a named class, and every modal carrier in those sentences is covered by a ledger row's carrier anchor.
- [x] AC-8: This requirement's verification evidence records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed copy under `~/.claude/skills/` that still holds the pre-change text, because the charter forbids running `speckiwi init`, `speckiwi skills install` or `speckiwi upgrade --apply` against this repository, so no reinstall is available here.

### FR-FLOW-079 — Loop D frozen denominator and needs-decision routing back to a user question

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The `kiwi-orchestrator` skill body SHALL, in all three variants, fix loop D's frozen denominator as exactly three sets computed externally before round 1 — the open-question set of every unresolved item in `01.intake.md`, being the QnA residuals plus every dissent item `/kiwi-srs-research` preserved; the implementability set of one row per `[D-nnn]` design item with a verdict of `implementable`, `needs-decision` or `contradicts-existing`; and the constraint set in the always-written `design/constraints.json` — SHALL make loop D pass only when every open question is resolved with an answer pointer or deferred with a named reason, there are zero `needs-decision` rows, zero `contradicts-existing` rows, zero constraint violations and no edit was applied in that round; and SHALL route every `needs-decision` row back to the `intake-qna` verb with that row as the question, re-entering the loop.

#### Rationale

05 §7.2 defines loop D's three externally computed sets and its pass condition, and states that a `needs-decision` row is where the charter's instruction to ask the user questions where the design is still weak actually happens — as a verb, not an aspiration. `contradicts-existing` requires a `file:line` pointer into the existing codebase, otherwise it is an opinion rather than a finding. §7.1's engine keeps denominators externally fixed and frozen at round entry, never computed by the verifier, and a row count differing from the frozen denominator invalidates the round for both verifiers. §13 attaches `design-intake-insufficient` to cap exhaustion with rows still open.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Each variant names all three loop D denominator sets — open questions, implementability, constraints — and states that they are computed externally before round 1, frozen at round entry, and never computed by a verifier.
- [x] AC-2: Each variant states that the open-question set is every unresolved item of `01.intake.md`, comprising the QnA residuals plus every dissent item `/kiwi-srs-research` preserved.
- [x] AC-3: Each variant states the implementability verdict vocabulary as the closed three-value set `implementable`, `needs-decision`, `contradicts-existing`, with one row per `[D-nnn]` item.
- [x] AC-4: Each variant states that a `contradicts-existing` verdict requires a `file:line` pointer into the existing codebase and is otherwise an opinion rather than a finding.
- [x] AC-5: Each variant states that `design/constraints.json` is always written, even when empty, and is the constraint-layer denominator.
- [x] AC-6: Each variant states loop D's five pass conjuncts: every open question resolved with an answer pointer or deferred with a named reason; zero `needs-decision`; zero `contradicts-existing`; zero constraint violations; and no edit applied in that round.
- [x] AC-7: Each variant states that a `verify-design` round emitting any `needs-decision` row routes back to the `intake-qna` verb with that row as the question and the loop re-enters, and that `design-intake-insufficient` fires when the cap is exhausted with `needs-decision` or `contradicts-existing` rows still open.
- [ ] AC-8: Every normative sentence this requirement adds to the skill body, counted per variant under the bilingual modal set, has a mutation-ledger row with at least one red mutation from a named class, and every modal carrier in those sentences is covered by a ledger row's carrier anchor.
- [x] AC-9: This requirement's verification evidence records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed copy under `~/.claude/skills/` that still holds the pre-change text, because the charter forbids running `speckiwi init`, `speckiwi skills install` or `speckiwi upgrade --apply` against this repository, so no reinstall is available here.

#### Requirement

The `kiwi-orchestrator` skill body SHALL, in all three variants, require every wave to have an authored English wave design document at `waves/wave-{n}/design.md`, written at Phase 3.a and verified by loop W with verifier 1 on coverage of that wave's `design_items` and verifier 2 on internal consistency against the frozen design lock, over that wave's `design_items` slice as the frozen denominator; loop W SHALL pass before the wave's `/kiwi-srs` registration at Phase 3.b, which consumes the document as its research document, and cap exhaustion SHALL raise `wave-design-insufficient` rather than counting as a pass.

#### Rationale

05 §2.1 Phase 3 orders 3.a before 3.b and gates 3.a with `wave-design-insufficient` and `unmarked-normative-prose`; §7.1's loop table fixes loop W's two stances and its frozen denominator as that wave's `design_items` slice, and reuses the engine's rule that cap exhaustion is not a pass. §3.1 marks `waves/wave-{n}/design.md` English, written at Phase 3.a, and read both by the loop W verifiers and by `/kiwi-srs` as its `--research-doc`. §3.3's marking rules are scoped to the run design document and the wave design document together, so the same unmarked-prose gate applies at 3.a.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-2: Each variant names loop W's two verifier stances — coverage of the wave's `design_items` and internal consistency against the frozen design lock — and states its frozen denominator as that wave's `design_items` slice.
- [x] AC-3: Each variant states that the wave design is authored and loop-W-verified at 3.a before the wave's `/kiwi-srs` registration at 3.b, which reads it as the research document; this ordered pair carries a swapped-ordering-pair red mutation in the ledger.
- [x] AC-4: Each variant states that loop W cap exhaustion raises `wave-design-insufficient` and that cap exhaustion is not a pass.
- [x] AC-5: Each variant states that the wave design document is under the same marked-item rules as the run design document, so a normative sentence outside a `[D-nnn]` or `[I-nnn]` row raises `unmarked-normative-prose` at 3.a.
- [ ] AC-6: Every normative sentence this requirement adds to the skill body, counted per variant under the bilingual modal set, has a mutation-ledger row with at least one red mutation from a named class, and every modal carrier in those sentences is covered by a ledger row's carrier anchor.

### FR-FLOW-081 — Per-lane English handoff document verified by loop H with a final-round executability probe

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [scope and frame decisions](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/08.scope-and-frame-decisions.md) |

#### Requirement

The `kiwi-orchestrator` skill body SHALL, in all three variants, require one handoff document per lane at `waves/wave-{n}/lanes/lane-{k}.md`, written in English, conforming to the handoff schema of YAML front matter plus ten required body headings in the order `## Setup`, `## Objective`, `## Context`, `## Interfaces`, `## Tasks`, `## Acceptance`, `## Constraints`, `## Out of scope`, `## Manifest`, `## Escalation`, and validated by `validateHandoff`'s five mechanical layers for its `handoff_kind`; each handoff SHALL be verified by loop H with verifier 1 on completeness and fidelity over a frozen denominator of thirteen fields per task plus the lane's assigned design items, and verifier 2 on cold-agent executability over a frozen denominator of the lane's `write_set` union the `acceptance[]` rows carrying `test_id: null`, including in the final round only an executability probe that gives a subagent the handoff document alone and compares the file-change plan it emits, with no edits applied, against `write_set`, a mismatch being a handoff defect rather than a coder defect.

#### Rationale

05 §6 ships the handoff document in full — schema, five-layer validator, verification loop, English-only gate and the untested-acceptance cap — because `08:30-33` singles the `test_id: null` hole out as the single critical defect a serial cut does not remove. §6.1 fixes ten body headings, stated as ten in the sentence and ten in the list after earlier revisions disagreed, because `validateHandoff` is authored from the bolded normative sentence. §6.4 fixes loop H's two denominators and records why the null `acceptance[]` rows are inside verifier 2's: with `write_set` alone, a handoff with a two-entry write set and one null row emits three rows against a denominator of two, invalidating every round until the cap is exhausted, so every wave containing one legitimately untested acceptance criterion would serialise entirely. §6.4 also states that in phase 1 cap exhaustion raises `handoff-verify-failed` directly, because every unit already runs serially and there is nowhere to demote to.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Each variant states that there is one handoff per lane at `waves/wave-{n}/lanes/lane-{k}.md`, written in English, and that non-Latin script in the body, in the `escalation` field or in any `acceptance[].untested_reason` — outside fenced code, inline code and blockquote content — raises `handoff-not-english`.
- [x] AC-2: Each variant names all ten required body headings in the stated order and states the count as ten, so a body naming nine or eleven headings fails.
- [x] AC-3: Each variant states that a handoff is validated by `validateHandoff`'s five mechanical layers and that which layers apply is decided by `handoff_kind`.
- [x] AC-4: Each variant states loop H's verifier 1 denominator as thirteen fields per task plus the lane's assigned design items, and that a verifier reporting a checked count differing from the expected count invalidates the round.
- [x] AC-5: Each variant states loop H's verifier 2 denominator as the lane's `write_set` union the `acceptance[]` rows carrying `test_id: null`; a body stating `write_set` alone fails.
- [x] AC-6: Each variant states that the executability probe runs in the final round only, that the probing subagent is given only the handoff document and applies no edits, that its emitted file set is compared against `write_set`, and that a mismatch is a handoff defect and not a coder defect.
- [x] AC-7: Each variant states loop H's four pass conjuncts: `validateHandoff` returns ok, both denominators are complete, merged CRITICAL and HIGH counts are zero, and no edit was applied in that round.
- [x] AC-8: Each variant states that in phase 1 cap exhaustion with the executability probe still mismatched raises `handoff-verify-failed` directly, with no demotion attempted, because every unit already runs serially and there is nowhere to demote to.
- [ ] AC-9: Every normative sentence this requirement adds to the skill body, counted per variant under the bilingual modal set, has a mutation-ledger row with at least one red mutation from a named class, and every modal carrier in those sentences is covered by a ledger row's carrier anchor.
- [x] AC-10: This requirement's verification evidence records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed copy under `~/.claude/skills/` that still holds the pre-change text, because the charter forbids running `speckiwi init`, `speckiwi skills install` or `speckiwi upgrade --apply` against this repository, so no reinstall is available here.

### FR-FLOW-082 — Wave-close validate then sync-index at 3.k with index-drift halt

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The `kiwi-orchestrator` skill SHALL run `speckiwi validate` and then `sync-index` exactly once per wave at Phase 3.k activity (3) — after the last unit of the last stage of that wave has executed, after the wave's `serial_epilogue` task set has run at 3.k activity (0), and before loop P at 3.l — and SHALL halt the run under the critical gate `post-merge-index-drift` when `validate --fail-on-warning` still reports drift after `sync_index` has run; because `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` performs no merge, charter C4's after-every-merge obligation attaches to the wave-close point at which SRS-affecting work has landed, and the per-merge attachment returns in `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`.

#### Rationale

05 §5.14 (`Charter C4 attaches to the wave, not to a merge`), §2.1 Phase 3.k activity (3), and §13's `post-merge-index-drift` row. Phase 1 executes every unit serially at the host root on the integration branch and performs no per-lane merge, so a per-merge attachment has no subject; C4's detector role is not relied on because its silent class needs two concurrent branches, but its repair role is, because §5.14 difference 2 puts `kiwi-coder`'s four mandatory MCP mutations back inside the wave at the host root.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All three bundled variants — `skills/claude/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md`, `skills/codex/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md`, `skills/etc/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md` — place `validate` then `sync-index` at Phase 3.k activity (3), ordered after 3.k activity (0)'s epilogue execution and before 3.l; a variant placing it before activity (0) or after 3.l fails.
- [x] AC-2: All three variants state the two commands in the order `validate` first and `sync-index` second; a variant naming only one of the two, or naming them in the opposite order, fails.
- [x] AC-3: `post-merge-index-drift` appears in each of the three variants' `critical_gates[]` table with Phase 3.k among its declared locations and with the predicate that `validate --fail-on-warning` still reports drift after `sync_index` has run.
- [x] AC-4: No variant attaches the obligation to a per-lane merge or to an `integrate-lane` step: the strings naming a per-merge attachment are absent from the phase-1 text, and the phase-2 return of the per-merge attachment is stated as deferred rather than silently dropped.
- [x] AC-5: Each of the three variants states that the run has no merge in phase 1 and that the obligation therefore attaches to the wave, so a reader cannot conclude the charter clause was dropped.
- [x] AC-6: The requirement's Verification Evidence carries an explicit runtime-lag note recording that the change lands in bundled text under `skills/{claude,codex,etc}/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md` while the running agent reads the installed copy at `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md`, which does not carry this text, because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids `speckiwi init`, `skills install` and `upgrade --apply` against this repository; a Verification Evidence table with no such note fails.

### FR-FLOW-083 — Convergence recipe enum and its lane-eligibility rule deciding execution order

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The orchestrator's convergence contract SHALL declare every convergence point with a `recipe.kind` drawn from the closed four-value enum `exclusive-lane`, `orchestrator-only`, `regenerate`, `replay`; SHALL refuse at Phase 2.c, through the critical gate `convergence-without-recipe`, a declared convergence point carrying no recipe from that enum; and SHALL decide lane eligibility from the matched point's `recipe.kind` under the most-restrictive-wins precedence order `orchestrator-only > replay > regenerate > exclusive-lane`, applied identically at every site that matches a path against the registry, so that `exclusive-lane` is lane-eligible under a whole-wave uniqueness constraint forcing every task touching that unit into one lane while `orchestrator-only`, `regenerate` and `replay` are not lane-eligible and place their tasks in `serial_epilogue`. In `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` that placement SHALL decide the unit's execution position — the `serial_epilogue` set is executed order-last within the wave at Phase 3.k activity (0) — and SHALL NOT decide its executor, which is the same host executor every unit uses.

#### Rationale

05 §5.2's `convergence-point` rule table and its glob/precedence paragraph, §5.3's `serial_epilogue` definition, §5.14 difference 6, §2.1 Phase 2.c and 3.k activity (0), and §9.1's reassignment of the lane-eligibility rule and the normative shipped default into `_shared/kiwi/run-ledger.md` for phase 1. Without the stated rule `exclusive-lane` is unreachable — every registry match went to the serial epilogue, so the enum member existed and was dead — and without the precedence order plan-time eligibility and merge-time restore could legitimately disagree about one file.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All three variants of the carrying text — the `kiwi-orchestrator` SKILL.md body and `_shared/kiwi/run-ledger.md` under `skills/claude`, `skills/codex` and `skills/etc` — name exactly the four `recipe.kind` values `exclusive-lane`, `orchestrator-only`, `regenerate`, `replay`; a variant naming three or five values fails.
- [x] AC-2: All three variants carry the precedence order `orchestrator-only > replay > regenerate > exclusive-lane` together with the statement that it is applied identically at every site matching a path against the registry.
- [x] AC-3: All three variants carry the four-row eligibility mapping: `exclusive-lane` is lane-eligible under a whole-wave uniqueness constraint, and each of `orchestrator-only`, `regenerate` and `replay` is not lane-eligible and routes its tasks to `serial_epilogue`.
- [x] AC-4: `convergence-without-recipe` appears in each of the three variants' `critical_gates[]` table with location Phase 2.c and the predicate that a declared convergence point has no recipe from the closed enum.
- [x] AC-5: All three variants state that in phase 1 a not-lane-eligible classification changes the unit's execution position — order-last within the wave, at 3.k activity (0) — and not its executor.
- [x] AC-6: No variant states as phase-1 behaviour that orchestrator-only paths are restored or removed per path at merge; the per-path restore is named as phase-2 content.
- [x] AC-7: No variant states as phase-1 behaviour that deferred mutations are replayed at the host root; the host replay is named as phase-2 content.
- [x] AC-8: The requirement's Verification Evidence carries an explicit runtime-lag note recording that the change lands in bundled text under `skills/{claude,codex,etc}/` while the running agent reads the installed copies at `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md` and `~/.claude/skills/_shared/kiwi/run-ledger.md`, which do not carry this text, because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids `speckiwi init`, `skills install` and `upgrade --apply` against this repository.

Covers the kiwi-orchestrator SKILL.md half of the carrying text: the four recipe.kind values, the literal precedence string "orchestrator-only &gt; replay &gt; regenerate &gt; exclusive-lane" with the applied-identically-everywhere clause, the four-row eligibility mapping asserted per row by proximity, convergence-without-recipe at Phase 2.c with the closed-enum predicate, the position-not-executor rule with order-last at 3.k activity (0), and the two phase-2 clauses named as deferred rather than dropped. |

### FR-FLOW-084 — Loop P post-execution wave verification over five denominators with a single-witness unit layer

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

Post-execution wave verification at Phase 3.l SHALL run the shared verify-loop engine over an evidence bundle comprising `kiwi-wave-master`'s existing rows plus `lanes.lock.json`, every handoff of the wave, the wave's commit range on `frozen.integration_branch` keyed by `Orch-Lane` and `Orch-Task` trailers, and each unit's `docs/analysis/kiwi-pm-…` bundle, against five externally frozen denominators — REQ/AC, the wave's design items, the constraints, the preservation layer and a unit layer — with verifier 1's design layer extended by an intent layer whose `expected` is loop D's open-question set and whose `checked` is the rows whose resolution the executed result still honours. The unit layer SHALL be a single sub-denominator whose `expected` is every task `lanes.lock.json` assigns to a lane, to `serial_epilogue` or to `unassigned`, and whose `checked` is every task carrying an `Orch-Task` trailer on a commit reachable from the integration head and whose `verification_cmd` passed, plus every task carrying an admissible `intentionally_empty` declaration, which stays in `expected` and enters `checked` rather than being removed from the denominator. One scheduled task that never landed SHALL forbid `ALL_MATCH`, and `unapproved-damage = 0` together with `failing_tests ⊆ baseline_failing_tests` SHALL remain pass preconditions.

#### Rationale

05 §7.6, including its phase-1 reading stated before the section, and §7.1's loop table row for P. The phase-1 narrowing is that the lane layer becomes a unit layer with one witness where phase 2 has two: the claim-audit conjunct has no phase-1 counterpart because §P2 7.4 is deferred, and the bundle's lane-manifest row is replaced by the wave's commit range plus each unit's analysis bundle. The two-sub-denominator split is not carried into phase 1 because the split between lanes, `serial_epilogue` and `unassigned` changes execution order rather than executor.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All three variants name five frozen denominators for loop P — REQ/AC, design items, constraints, preservation layer and unit layer; a variant naming four denominators fails.
- [x] AC-2: All three variants extend verifier 1's design layer with an intent layer whose `expected` is loop D's open-question set and whose `checked` is the rows whose resolution the executed result still honours, and include `00.charter.md` and `01.intake.md` in the evidence bundle; a variant omitting the intent layer fails.
- [x] AC-3: All three variants define the unit layer as one sub-denominator whose `expected` is the union of the tasks `lanes.lock.json` assigns to lanes, to `serial_epilogue` and to `unassigned`; a variant that splits it into a lane sub-denominator and an epilogue sub-denominator fails.
- [x] AC-4: All three variants define `checked` as the conjunction of an `Orch-Task` trailer on a commit reachable from the integration head and a passing `verification_cmd`.
- [x] AC-5: All three variants state that a task carrying an admissible `intentionally_empty` declaration stays in `expected` and enters `checked`, rather than being removed from `expected`.
- [x] AC-6: All three variants state explicitly that phase 1 has one witness where phase 2 has two and that the claim-audit conjunct has no phase-1 counterpart, so the reduction is recorded rather than silently absent.
- [x] AC-7: All three variants state that one task scheduled but never landed forbids `ALL_MATCH`.
- [x] AC-8: All three variants replace the lane-manifest bundle row with the wave's commit range on `frozen.integration_branch` keyed by `Orch-Lane` and `Orch-Task` trailers plus each unit's `docs/analysis/kiwi-pm-…` bundle, and retain `lanes.lock.json` and every handoff as bundle rows.
- [x] AC-9: All three variants retain `unapproved-damage = 0` and `failing_tests ⊆ baseline_failing_tests` as loop P's pass preconditions.
- [x] AC-10: No variant admits the executing unit's own worklog `TASK_DONE` as a conjunct of `checked`, because it is written by the same party whose work it attests and adds no independence.
- [x] AC-11: The requirement's Verification Evidence carries an explicit runtime-lag note recording that the change lands in bundled text under `skills/{claude,codex,etc}/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md` while the running agent reads the installed copy at `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md`, which does not carry this text, because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids `speckiwi init`, `skills install` and `upgrade --apply` against this repository.

### FR-FLOW-085 — Wave-boundary issue protocol with a closed classification list and a next-wave precondition

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

Wave-boundary issues SHALL be triaged at Phase 3.m by the `wave-issue-triage` verb, after loop P and before `promote-requirements`, into `waves/wave-{n}/issues.md` plus a generated `issues.lock.json`; every issue SHALL receive exactly one classification from the closed six-value list `local-defect`, `missing-task`, `design-gap`, `new-wave-required`, `design-contradiction`, `out-of-run`, each carrying the route stated for it; and the next wave's Phase 3.a SHALL be blocked by the precondition `P-WAVE-ISSUES-CLOSED`, evaluated by `orchestrate wave close --wave N`, until every issue carries a terminal classification, every `local-defect` and `missing-task` carries a resolution proof that is a resolvable `file:line`, a test id or a commit sha, every `design-gap` names its new design lock digest, and every `out-of-run` and `new-wave-required` carries a recorded user decision — an `out-of-run` deferral SHALL require user consent even under `--auto`.

#### Rationale

05 §8, discharging `00.charter.md:91-92` (new issues must not be carried silently into the next stage; plan how to resolve them, write the plan to a document, and resolve it before the next stage begins). §2.0 fixes the vocabulary so the charter's stage is this design's wave. The consent rule for `out-of-run` follows `kiwi-wave-master:205`'s existing rule for `out_of_scope` at the coverage gate, which is what stops deferral from becoming a free escape.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All three variants name exactly the six classification values `local-defect`, `missing-task`, `design-gap`, `new-wave-required`, `design-contradiction`, `out-of-run`, each with its stated route; a variant naming five or seven values fails.
- [x] AC-2: All three variants state that every issue receives exactly one classification and that the list is closed.
- [x] AC-3: All three variants place `wave-issue-triage` at Phase 3.m, after loop P and before `promote-requirements`, and name both written artifacts `waves/wave-{n}/issues.md` and `issues.lock.json`.
- [x] AC-4: All three variants state `P-WAVE-ISSUES-CLOSED` with exactly its four conjuncts — terminal classification on every issue; a resolution proof that is a resolvable `file:line`, a test id or a commit sha on every `local-defect` and `missing-task`; a named new design lock digest on every `design-gap`; a recorded user decision on every `out-of-run` and `new-wave-required` — and name `orchestrate wave close --wave N` as its evaluator.
- [x] AC-5: All three variants state that `out-of-run` requires user consent even under `--auto`; a variant that lets `--auto` or a committee discharge it fails.
- [x] AC-6: `wave-issues-open` and `design-contradiction-at-wave-boundary` each appear in all three variants' `critical_gates[]` table with location Phase 3.m.
- [x] AC-7: All three variants route `design-contradiction` to end the run through `abort-run` after naming the two contradicting `[D-nnn]` ids with evidence, and state that it is not committee-decidable.
- [x] AC-8: All three variants cap `new-wave-required` at three appended waves per run and name `wave-append-cap-exhausted` as the gate that fires on exhaustion.
- [x] AC-9: All three variants record the stated limitation that the tool checks the form of a classification and not its correctness, together with its compensation, that the next wave's loop P re-checks the previous wave's `out-of-run` items.
- [x] AC-10: The requirement's Verification Evidence carries an explicit runtime-lag note recording that the change lands in bundled text under `skills/{claude,codex,etc}/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md` while the running agent reads the installed copy at `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md`, which does not carry this text, because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids `speckiwi init`, `skills install` and `upgrade --apply` against this repository.

#### Verification Evidence

### FR-FLOW-086 — Immediate termination of an oscillating verification loop

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The shared verification engine carried in `_shared/kiwi/verify-loop.md` SHALL terminate a loop immediately, rather than consuming its remaining round cap, when the same `finding_id` closes and re-opens across two or more rounds or the same hunk is reverted and re-applied, and SHALL record `verdict = fail-residual` together with the `reason_class` value `oscillation` and raise the critical gate `verification-oscillation`.

#### Rationale

05 §7.8, which records that `kiwi-wave-master:341-360` has caps, streaks and unreachable-PASS arithmetic but no clause for a genuinely contested finding, so a loop whose two stances are in real conflict burns its whole cap buying nothing. The rule is added to the shared engine rather than to a caller so it applies to every loop the engine drives; the `oscillation` value is an additive v1.4.0 addition to `waves-event.md`'s closed `reason_class` vocabulary.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All three variants of `_shared/kiwi/verify-loop.md` — under `skills/claude`, `skills/codex` and `skills/etc` — state both triggers: the same `finding_id` closing and re-opening across two or more rounds, and the same hunk being reverted and re-applied.
- [x] AC-2: All three variants state that the loop stops immediately on either trigger rather than continuing to its cap; a variant that instead escalates a rung, extends the cap or continues rounds fails.
- [x] AC-3: All three variants record both outcome values on termination — `verdict = fail-residual` and the `reason_class` value `oscillation` — and a variant recording only one of the two fails.
- [x] AC-4: `verification-oscillation` appears in all three variants of the `kiwi-orchestrator` `critical_gates[]` table with a location covering any loop.
- [x] AC-5: The rule is stated denominator-agnostically in `_shared/kiwi/verify-loop.md` rather than inside a single caller, so it reaches every loop the engine drives — D, W, H, P and F in phase 1.
- [x] AC-6: The requirement's Verification Evidence carries an explicit runtime-lag note recording that the change lands in bundled text under `skills/{claude,codex,etc}/_shared/kiwi/verify-loop.md` while the running agent reads the installed copy at `~/.claude/skills/_shared/kiwi/verify-loop.md`, which still holds the pre-change text, because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids `speckiwi init`, `skills install` and `upgrade --apply` against this repository.

AC-4 is the gate row, asserted present in every variant's three-column critical_gates[] table with a location covering any loop. |

### FR-FLOW-087 — Requirement promotion at 3.n with landed defined by trailer and verification command

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

Requirement promotion SHALL happen once per wave, at the host root, at Phase 3.n after loop P's 3.l verdict is `pass`, over the wave's Phase 3.b allocation set restricted to requirements every one of whose sidecar tasks has landed, where landed means a commit on `frozen.integration_branch` carrying that task's `Orch-Task` trailer together with a passing `verification_cmd`, or an admissible `intentionally_empty` declaration; such requirements SHALL transition `planned` or `in_progress` to `implemented`, and SHALL transition `implemented` to `verified` only when loop P's verdict for that wave is `pass` with no residual naming the requirement and every one of the requirement's tasks carries a passing `verification_cmd`; a requirement carrying any `test_id: null` acceptance row MAY reach `implemented` and SHALL NOT reach `verified` in that wave; each promoted requirement SHALL carry a test-kind evidence row whose reference is the `verification_cmd` and whose detail is the unit's `docs/analysis/kiwi-pm-…` bundle and a commit-kind evidence row whose reference is the `Orch-Task`-trailered commit sha on `frozen.integration_branch`, except that a task landed by an `intentionally_empty` declaration carries test-kind evidence only; and a requirement with any unlanded task SHALL be left at its current status and recorded in that wave's `issues.md` as `missing-task`.

#### Rationale

05 §7.10, including its phase-1 reading of the set, the transition and the evidence, together with §5.14's `intentionally_empty` disposition and difference 5's `Orch-*` trailers. `00.charter.md:297-298` forbids marking a requirement verified without evidence, and §7.10 exists because the earlier one-clause step named neither which requirements, nor to what status, nor against what evidence. The loop-L per-acceptance-row closure is unavailable in phase 1 because loop L is deferred, and the `test_id: null` bar is what still stops an untested acceptance row from reaching `verified`.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All three variants state that promotion happens once per wave, at the host root, at Phase 3.n, and only after loop P's 3.l verdict is `pass`.
- [x] AC-2: All three variants define the promoted set as the wave's 3.b allocation set restricted to requirements every one of whose sidecar tasks landed.
- [x] AC-3: All three variants define landed for phase 1 as an `Orch-Task`-trailered commit on `frozen.integration_branch` plus a passing `verification_cmd`, or an admissible `intentionally_empty` declaration; a variant defining landed by a `git-ancestor` proof against a lane head or by a passing claim audit fails.
- [x] AC-4: All three variants carry the two-step transition with both rows: `planned` or `in_progress` to `implemented` when every task landed, and `implemented` to `verified` additionally on loop P `pass` with no residual naming the requirement and a passing `verification_cmd` for every one of the requirement's tasks.
- [x] AC-5: All three variants state that loop L's per-acceptance-row `realised-with-test` closure is unavailable in phase 1 and name it as deferred; a variant that silently omits it, or that claims it as a phase-1 conjunct, fails.
- [x] AC-6: All three variants state that any `test_id: null` acceptance row permits `implemented` and bars `verified` in that wave, and that the row's `untested_owner` names the wave that will close it.
- [x] AC-7: All three variants state both evidence rows with their phase-1 references — test-kind referencing the `verification_cmd` with the unit's `docs/analysis/kiwi-pm-…` bundle as detail, and commit-kind referencing the `Orch-Task`-trailered commit sha on `frozen.integration_branch`.
- [x] AC-8: No variant names a lane `audit.json` or an `integrate-lane` merge sha as a phase-1 evidence reference.
- [x] AC-9: All three variants state that a task landed by an admissible `intentionally_empty` declaration carries test-kind evidence only and no commit-kind reference, because it produced no commit.
- [x] AC-10: All three variants state that a requirement with any unlanded task is left at its current status and recorded in that wave's `issues.md` as `missing-task`, and that a task carrying an admissible `intentionally_empty` declaration is landed and therefore not a `missing-task`.
- [x] AC-11: The requirement's Verification Evidence carries an explicit runtime-lag note recording that the change lands in bundled text under `skills/{claude,codex,etc}/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md` while the running agent reads the installed copy at `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md`, which does not carry this text, because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids `speckiwi init`, `skills install` and `upgrade --apply` against this repository.

### FR-FLOW-088 — Orchestrator isolation stated in its own section zero and no --wt propagation

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The `kiwi-orchestrator` skill SHALL state in its own §0 why `kiwi-wave-master`'s wave-level rationale for `wt-delegation-refused` is not inherited, on the phase-1 ground that `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` creates no per-wave worktree and no lane workspace that could break wave accumulation, executes every unit of every wave at the host root on the run's integration branch, and freezes `isolation_profile` to the constant `none-serial` with the Preflight P.6 isolation probe deferred; and the orchestrator SHALL never pass `--wt` to a delegated `kiwi-pipeline`, refusing such a delegation through the inherited critical gate `wt-delegation-refused` at Preflight P.2, on the ground that a cycle-scoped worktree nested inside a lane-scoped one is a topology this design does not support.

#### Rationale

05 §1.3 (`wt-delegation-refused` is not inherited, and the skill must say why in its own §0), §2.1 Preflight P.2 and P.6, §4.1 (`isolation_profile` is the constant `none-serial` in phase 1, kept inside `frozen` so `invariant_digest` covers it), and §5.14. `kiwi-wave-master:144` refuses `--wt` because per-wave worktrees break wave accumulation; phase 1 has no per-wave worktree, so that rationale does not transfer, while the refusal to propagate `--wt` to a delegated pipeline does transfer and is inherited verbatim.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All three variants carry a §0 section stating the distinction, with all three phase-1 grounds present: no per-wave worktree or lane workspace, every unit executed at the host root on the run's integration branch, and `isolation_profile` the constant `none-serial`.
- [x] AC-2: Every place the phase-1 skill text names an isolation profile it writes the literal `none-serial`, and no variant names `worktree`, `branch-serial-lane` or `patch-lane` as a phase-1 profile.
- [x] AC-3: All three variants mark Preflight P.6's isolation probe as deferred to `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`, so the constant profile is not read as a probe result.
- [x] AC-4: `wt-delegation-refused` appears in all three variants' `critical_gates[]` table with location Preflight P.2 and with the predicate a delegated `kiwi-pipeline --wt`.
- [x] AC-5: All three variants give the orchestrator's own reason for the refusal — a cycle-scoped worktree nested inside a lane-scoped one — and do not restate `kiwi-wave-master`'s per-wave-accumulation reason as the orchestrator's own.
- [x] AC-6: All three variants name the task-granularity isolation half as re-entering in `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes` rather than claiming it for phase 1.
- [x] AC-7: The requirement's Verification Evidence carries an explicit runtime-lag note recording that the change lands in bundled text under `skills/{claude,codex,etc}/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md` while the running agent reads the installed copy at `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md`, which does not carry this text, because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids `speckiwi init`, `skills install` and `upgrade --apply` against this repository.

### FR-FLOW-089 — route-downgrade-available at the end of Phase 2 as the one legal downgrade

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [routing design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/09.routing-design.md) |

#### Requirement

At the end of Phase 2, alongside the refusal-floor evaluation at 2.e, the orchestrator SHALL raise the gate `route-downgrade-available` with severity `business-decision` and outside `critical_gates[]` when all three conditions hold — the decomposition returned exactly one wave, no disqualifier removed `R-STEP` at Phase 1.c′, and no SRS mutation on this run has moved a requirement's `status` or `stability` — offering exactly two options, `continue-orchestrated` carrying the structured marker `recommended: true` and `downgrade-to-step`; the wave's `design_items` count SHALL be carried in the gate's evidence and SHALL drive no predicate; the gate SHALL be legal at 2.e and nowhere later, with `route-deescalation-refused` applying after Phase 3.b; and a `downgrade-to-step` resolution SHALL re-raise `route-step-requires-mode-switch` when the persisted work-mode is not `tdd`, SHALL write a new `routing/route.lock.json` carrying rung `R-STEP` under the verb `downgrade-route` and the append-new-artifact rule so the new digest re-enters `invariant_digest`, and SHALL leave `design/00.design.md` and the decomposition on disk as sunk artifacts named in the run report and not passed to `kiwi-tdd`.

#### Rationale

05 §1.4 and `09` §7.4. The original row — degrade to `--serial` below the refusal floor and report that concurrency rather than isolation was disabled — is superseded by `08`'s scope cut: under a serial phase 1 there is no concurrency to disable and that degradation is the default path. What survives is the routing question the floor was really asking, and `09` §7.4 attaches it at 2.e as the one downgrade that is free, because on a run that reached `R-ORCH` at 1.c′ Phase 3.b is the run's first SRS mutation, so nothing has been registered and nothing must be retracted.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All three variants declare `route-downgrade-available` firing at the end of Phase 2 alongside the refusal-floor evaluation, with all three conditions present; a variant declaring two of the three fails, and a variant stating a fourth condition over a design-item magnitude threshold also fails.
- [x] AC-2: All three variants state the third condition as no SRS mutation having moved a requirement's `status` or `stability` — not as the absence of a commit on the integration branch — and record the reason, that the run lands `commit-run-artifacts` commits on the integration branch on every run so a commit-based condition would always be false.
- [x] AC-3: All three variants offer exactly two options, `continue-orchestrated` and `downgrade-to-step`, with the structured field `recommended: true` on `continue-orchestrated`, so `--auto` adopts it with no committee and never silently downgrades an unattended run; a variant marking `downgrade-to-step` recommended fails.
- [x] AC-4: All three variants declare `route-downgrade-available` in the skill body with severity `business-decision` and it is absent from all three variants' `critical_gates[]` table.
- [x] AC-5: All three variants state the legality window: the gate exists at 2.e and nowhere later, and after Phase 3.b `route-deescalation-refused` applies instead.
- [x] AC-6: All three variants state what `downgrade-to-step` does — re-raise `route-step-requires-mode-switch` when the persisted work-mode is not `tdd` so the mode precondition is not bypassed, write a new `routing/route.lock.json` with rung `R-STEP` under the verb `downgrade-route` and the append-new-artifact rule with the new digest re-entering `invariant_digest`, and leave the design document and the decomposition unread by `kiwi-tdd`.
- [x] AC-7: No variant offers a `--serial` degradation below the refusal floor or states that the orchestrator disables concurrency, because phase 1's default execution is serial; the only outright refusal stated at the floor is `decomposition-input-missing`.
- [x] AC-8: The requirement's Verification Evidence carries an explicit runtime-lag note recording that the change lands in bundled text under `skills/{claude,codex,etc}/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md` while the running agent reads the installed copy at `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md`, which does not carry this text, because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids `speckiwi init`, `skills install` and `upgrade --apply` against this repository.

### FR-FLOW-090 — No-duplication discharged by a commit-range audit, a coupling check and a recorded absence

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The charter's no-duplication requirement SHALL be discharged by one shipped detection mechanism, one shipped coupling mechanism and one recorded absence: a duplication audit SHALL run at Phase 3.k activity (2), after the wave's serial-epilogue set has executed at activity (0), over the wave's commit range on `frozen.integration_branch` restricted to the union of every unit's `write_set`, writing `waves/wave-{n}/duplication-audit.md` with one row per finding carrying `symbol_or_block`, `lanes[]`, `paths[]` and a `verdict` drawn from the closed enum `duplicate`, `parallel-evolution`, `acceptable`, and the critical gate `cross-lane-duplication-unresolved` SHALL fire on a `duplicate` row whose resolution is neither the id of an epilogue task that already ran at activity (0) and whose commit consolidated the block nor an `issue:{id}` reference naming a `local-defect` row opened in that wave's `issues.md`; separately, at Phase 3.f″ a path in one lane's `write_set` and another lane's `read_set` within one stage SHALL force the two lanes into one by re-freezing `lanes.lock.json` under the append-new-artifact rule, re-authoring the affected handoffs and re-running loop H, bounded at one pass per stage with a second hit in the same stage raising `stage-coupling-unresolved`; and the preventive half of no-duplication SHALL be recorded as absent rather than claimed, because at file level `write-set-overlap` already covers every declared surface and at symbol level no planner output carries a symbol, with the absence escalated as X-04.

#### Rationale

05 §7.9 halves (a) and (b), §2.1 Phase 3.k activity (2) and 3.f″, and §0.7 X-04. `00.charter.md:100` forbids introducing code duplication; the first revision discharged it only inside the weaknesses section, and the preventive predicate it named could never fire because it was circular, quantified over a handoff field authored two phases later, and needed symbols no surface carries. Phase 1's audit input is the wave's commit range rather than the union of lane diffs because there are no lane diffs, and the audit's value is unchanged: two units that each invent the same helper do so whether they run concurrently or one after another, since each unit is a fresh `/kiwi-pm` run over its own handoff.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All three variants state the phase-1 duplication-audit input as the wave's commit range on `frozen.integration_branch` restricted to the union of every unit's `write_set`; no variant states the union of lane diffs as the phase-1 input.
- [x] AC-2: All three variants place the audit at Phase 3.k activity (2), after activity (0)'s epilogue execution, and name the artifact `waves/wave-{n}/duplication-audit.md`.
- [x] AC-3: All three variants state the four-field finding row `symbol_or_block`, `lanes[]`, `paths[]`, `verdict` and name exactly the three verdict values `duplicate`, `parallel-evolution`, `acceptable`.
- [x] AC-4: All three variants admit exactly two resolution forms for a `duplicate` row — the id of an epilogue task that already ran at activity (0) and whose commit consolidated the block, or `issue:{id}` naming a `local-defect` row opened in that wave's `issues.md` — and state that a note is not a resolution.
- [x] AC-5: `cross-lane-duplication-unresolved` appears in all three variants' `critical_gates[]` table with location Phase 3.k.
- [x] AC-6: All three variants state that the verdict is a recorded subagent judgment and that the tool checks only that a verdict from the closed enum was recorded for each emitted candidate.
- [x] AC-7: All three variants state the 3.f″ coupling predicate — a path in one lane's `write_set` and another lane's `read_set` within one stage — together with its action: re-freeze `lanes.lock.json` under the append-new-artifact rule, re-author the affected handoffs, and re-run loop H on them.
- [x] AC-8: All three variants bound the coupling check at one pass per stage, name `stage-coupling-unresolved` as the gate on a second hit in the same stage, and state that the 3.e′ partition verdict is not re-sought.
- [x] AC-9: All three variants record the preventive half as absent with both stated reasons — file level already covered by `write-set-overlap`, symbol level carried by no planner output — and escalated as X-04.
- [x] AC-10: No variant declares a `shared-substrate` conflict reason or a `shared-substrate-unhoisted` gate, both of which are withdrawn as unsatisfiable.
- [x] AC-11: The requirement's Verification Evidence carries an explicit runtime-lag note recording that the change lands in bundled text under `skills/{claude,codex,etc}/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md` while the running agent reads the installed copy at `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md`, which does not carry this text, because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids `speckiwi init`, `skills install` and `upgrade --apply` against this repository.

### FR-FLOW-091 — Design refutation by an executing unit and the sanctioned mid-wave amendment

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

A unit that finds a frozen `[D-nnn]` design item unimplementable as written SHALL report the refutation with its `design_item_id` and its evidence and stop, raising the critical gate `lane-design-refuted` at Phase 3.g — or at Phase 3.k activity (0) when the reporting unit is the epilogue — leaving that unit's commits in place and unreverted on `frozen.integration_branch` and recording the terminal `lane_disposition` kind `refuted` on the `execute-unit` result line; and a sanctioned mid-wave design amendment SHALL be written through the `amend-design` verb as a new `design/00.design.{seq}.md` frozen into a new `00.design.lock.json`, never as an in-place edit, plus a journal line naming the old lock digest, the new lock digest, the refuted `[D-nnn]` and the reporting unit's evidence, after which the resume card's `frozen.design_lock` pointer moves to the new lock and `invariant_digest` is recomputed so that `run-invariant-drift` fires only when the digest disagrees with the lock the card currently names, every denominator frozen on the old design items in loops W, P and F is re-frozen from the new lock with the affected rounds restarted, each affected unit is re-planned and re-executed by one `execute-unit` over a newly authored handoff, and mid-wave amendments are bounded at two per wave with the third classified `design-contradiction-at-wave-boundary`.

#### Rationale

05 §8.1 and its phase-1 reading, §4.4's `amend-design` row, §4.2's `lane_disposition` row (in phase 1 the carrier is the `execute-unit` result line and the one kind phase 1 can produce is `refuted`), and §13's `lane-design-refuted` row. Before §8.1 the mid-wave case had no route at all: a unit that found a design item unimplementable could only breach its lease, die and discard the finding, or implement something known wrong, while `invariant_digest` over the frozen block made any mid-wave amendment a drift halt by construction. Both outcomes are unacceptable, so the amendment path must be legitimate and must not read as drift.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All three variants state that a unit finding a `[D-nnn]` unimplementable reports `design_item_id` and evidence and stops.
- [x] AC-2: `lane-design-refuted` appears in all three variants' `critical_gates[]` table with both phase-1 locations, Phase 3.g and Phase 3.k activity (0).
- [x] AC-3: All three variants name the phase-1 carrier as the executing unit's report and not a lane manifest `status: design-refuted`, and state that the unit's commits stay on `frozen.integration_branch` unreverted rather than that a lane branch is preserved un-merged.
- [x] AC-4: All three variants state that `amend-design` writes a new `design/00.design.{seq}.md` and a new `00.design.lock.json` and never edits either in place, and that a journal line names the old digest, the new digest, the refuted `[D-nnn]` and the evidence.
- [x] AC-5: All three variants state that `frozen.design_lock` moves to the new lock and `invariant_digest` is recomputed, so `run-invariant-drift` fires only against the lock the card currently names; a variant that leaves the pointer unmoved would make a legitimate amendment read as drift and fails.
- [x] AC-6: All three variants state that loops W, P and F re-freeze their denominators from the new lock and that the affected rounds restart.
- [x] AC-7: All three variants state that re-execution is one `execute-unit` over a newly authored handoff, and name the phase-2 re-dispatch cap of one as phase-2 content that does not apply here.
- [x] AC-8: All three variants bound mid-wave amendments at two per wave and classify the third as `design-contradiction-at-wave-boundary`.
- [x] AC-9: All three variants state that the phase-1 `lane_disposition` written on the `execute-unit` result line carries kind `refuted`, which is the one terminal kind phase 1 can produce.
- [x] AC-10: The requirement's Verification Evidence carries an explicit runtime-lag note recording that the change lands in bundled text under `skills/{claude,codex,etc}/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md` while the running agent reads the installed copy at `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md`, which does not carry this text, because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids `speckiwi init`, `skills install` and `upgrade --apply` against this repository.

### FR-FLOW-092 — Three-place record of committee-answered intake questions under --auto

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [auto contract decisions](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/07.auto-contract-decisions.md) |

#### Requirement

Where `--auto` causes intake design questions to be answered by the committee rather than by the user, the orchestrator SHALL record that fact in three places: a Phase 0 notice in the run header stating that `--auto` is in effect and that design questions will be answered by a committee rather than by the user, together with a separate Phase 1.c line naming the actual number of such questions once gap enumeration has produced it; an `intake_autonomy` block in `00.run-contract.md`, written at the end-of-Phase-1 amendment, carrying whether `--auto` answered design questions, how many, and where the per-decision audit records are; and one `kiwi/waves.jsonl` line per committee-decided intake row on the v1.4.0 `decision` field carrying `question`, `options`, `decision`, `rule`, `committee_size`, `confidence` and an `origin` of `intake`.

#### Rationale

05 §7.2 and §0.7 X-05, with the audit-record shape from `07.auto-contract-decisions.md:169-170` and the run-contract block from §11.2. `00.charter.md:54` names the user exactly once in the intake section, and routing those questions to a committee is a recorded divergence from it rather than a user-accepted degradation, so the design pays for it in evidence: a decision that received no user attention leaves the most evidence. The three records are mandatory whichever way X-05 is ruled, so nothing here is blocked on that ruling.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All three variants name all three records; a variant naming two of the three fails.
- [x] AC-2: All three variants keep the Phase 0 notice and the Phase 1.c line as two separate records and place the question count only on the 1.c line, because the count does not exist before gap enumeration; a variant that puts the count in the Phase 0 header fails.
- [x] AC-3: All three variants place the `intake_autonomy` block in `00.run-contract.md`, written at the end-of-Phase-1 amendment, and state its three contents: whether `--auto` answered design questions, how many, and where the per-decision audit records are.
- [x] AC-4: All three variants require one journal line per committee-decided intake row on the `decision` field, carrying all seven keys `question`, `options`, `decision`, `rule`, `committee_size`, `confidence` and `origin`, with `origin` set to `intake`.
- [x] AC-5: All three variants describe the routing as a recorded divergence from the charter's intake sentence and do not describe it as a user-accepted degradation.
- [x] AC-6: All three variants state that the three records stand regardless of how the open escalation over the charter's intake sentence is ruled, so the records are not conditional on that ruling.
- [x] AC-7: All three variants keep `design-intake-insufficient` firing on loop D cap exhaustion with `needs-decision` or `contradicts-existing` rows open, so the records supplement the existing gate rather than replacing it.
- [x] AC-8: The requirement's Verification Evidence carries an explicit runtime-lag note recording that the change lands in bundled text under `skills/{claude,codex,etc}/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md` while the running agent reads the installed copy at `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md`, which does not carry this text, because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids `speckiwi init`, `skills install` and `upgrade --apply` against this repository.

### FR-FLOW-093 — Integration branch ownership, committed run artifacts and the abort run report

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The run SHALL have a named integration branch `kiwi/orch/{run_id}/integration`, created or adopted at Phase 0.b off `--base-branch` and recorded in the resume card's `frozen` block; the orchestrator SHALL never merge that branch into the base branch and SHALL never open a pull request; every artifact the run authors under `docs/research/{work}/` SHALL be committed under the verb `commit-run-artifacts` on the declared schedule with an explicit pathspec and never through `git add -A` or `git commit -a`, so that `git clean -fd` cannot destroy the frozen design or the locks the resume card's `invariant_digest` names; and an aborted run SHALL leave `frozen.integration_branch` exactly where it is, write `docs/research/{work}/00.run-report.md` naming the integration branch and its sha, which waves are complete, the commits the run left on the integration branch, the gate that ended the run and the exact command to resume if the cause is fixable, and release the run lock.

#### Rationale

05 §5.12 with its phase-1 reading (abort's reap step is a no-op and the report's workspace rows do not exist, so the report names commits and branches only), §4.8 policy 5's commit schedule, §2.1 Phase 0.b, and §11.2's closed list of forbidden actions. The failure this prevents is loud and total: a hermeticity leak prompts `git clean -fd`, this repository's own documented recovery, which deletes the frozen design, every lock the card's `invariant_digest` points at and every verification round, leaving the run halting on `run-invariant-drift` with nothing to recompute from. `abort-run` is a first-class verb rather than a synonym for `halt` because a halt that does not name the repository state it leaves is an abandonment.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All three variants name the integration branch `kiwi/orch/{run_id}/integration`, create or adopt it at Phase 0.b off `--base-branch` defaulting to the current branch, and record it inside the resume card's `frozen` block so `invariant_digest` covers it.
- [x] AC-2: `integration-branch-unavailable` appears in all three variants' `critical_gates[]` table with location Phase 0.b.
- [x] AC-3: All three variants state that the orchestrator never merges the integration branch into the base branch and never opens a pull request, both in the skill body and in `00.run-contract.md`'s closed list of forbidden actions.
- [x] AC-4: All three variants state the one obligation the orchestrator cannot discharge: whoever later merges the integration branch into the base branch must run `validate` then `sync-index` on the base branch immediately afterwards.
- [x] AC-5: All three variants carry the `commit-run-artifacts` schedule with its phase-1 commit points and state that every one of them stages an explicit pathspec; `git add -A` and `git commit -a` appear in `00.run-contract.md`'s forbidden actions in all three variants.
- [x] AC-6: All three variants state that `abort-run` is a distinct verb from `halt`, leaves `frozen.integration_branch` exactly where it is because merges are never unwound, writes `docs/research/{work}/00.run-report.md`, and releases the Preflight P.5 run lock.
- [x] AC-7: All three variants list the phase-1 run-report contents — the integration branch and its sha, which waves are complete, the commits the run left on the integration branch, the gate that ended the run, and the exact resume command — and omit the workspace rows, naming them as phase-2 content rather than dropping them silently.
- [x] AC-8: All three variants name the phase-1 terminal halts that occur after work has landed as `wave-verify-fail-residual`, `post-merge-index-drift` and `design-contradiction-at-wave-boundary`, and mark `srs-mutation-replay-failed` as phase 2.
- [x] AC-9: The requirement's Verification Evidence carries an explicit runtime-lag note recording that the change lands in bundled text under `skills/{claude,codex,etc}/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md` while the running agent reads the installed copy at `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md`, which does not carry this text, because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids `speckiwi init`, `skills install` and `upgrade --apply` against this repository.
