### FR-FLOW-094 — Handoff carries no base sha and the ledger records the stage dispatch base

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

A handoff document SHALL NOT carry a `base_sha` front-matter field and SHALL be rejected when one is present, and its `## Setup` section SHALL state that the base commit is already checked out without naming the sha, because a commit's sha is a hash over its own tree and no value written into a file inside that commit can equal it; handoff validation's reference-resolution layer SHALL resolve against HEAD at Phase 3.f plus the set of paths Phase 3.f′ is about to stage, never against a sha that does not yet exist; and the run's ledger SHALL carry the stage's dispatch base sha — the sha of that stage's 3.f′ commit, per stage and not per wave — in the journal's `isolation.base_sha` and in the resume card's `open[].base_sha`.

#### Rationale

05 §2.1 ordering rule 2, §4.1's `open[]` and its worked-example consistency paragraph, §4.2's `isolation` field, and §6.2 layer 4. Making `base_sha` a required front-matter field defined as the sha of the commit that stages the handoff is unsatisfiable: no amend sequence converges, and the natural workaround of writing the last known sha puts the handoff outside its own `base_sha` and reopens the ENOENT-on-first-read failure recorded as RW-04. The value is therefore carried only where it can be, and the ledger is the phase-1 carrier.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All three variants state that a handoff carrying a `base_sha` front-matter field is rejected, and state the reason — a commit's sha is a hash over its own tree, so no value inside that commit can name it.
- [x] AC-2: All three variants state that `## Setup` says the base commit is already checked out and never names the sha, and that any `dod` clause comparing against it refers to the value the ledger supplied rather than to a handoff field.
- [x] AC-3: All three variants state that the dispatch base sha is the sha of that stage's 3.f′ commit and is per stage rather than per wave.
- [x] AC-4: All three variants record the dispatch base sha on the journal's `isolation.base_sha` and in the resume card's `open[].base_sha`.
- [x] AC-5: All three variants state the reference-resolution point as HEAD at Phase 3.f plus the set of paths Phase 3.f′ is about to stage; no variant states that references resolve at `base_sha`.
- [x] AC-6: The spawn-prompt carrier clause is absent from the phase-1 text and named as phase 2: all three variants state that a phase-1 unit runs at the host with the integration branch already checked out.
- [x] AC-7: The requirement's Verification Evidence carries an explicit runtime-lag note recording that the change lands in bundled text under `skills/{claude,codex,etc}/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md` while the running agent reads the installed copy at `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md`, which does not carry this text, because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids `speckiwi init`, `skills install` and `upgrade --apply` against this repository.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

AC-1 asserts the rejection together with the reason, because the rejection alone would read as an arbitrary restriction rather than a fixed point — no amend sequence converges when a file must name the sha of the commit containing it. AC-5 is asserted both ways: the HEAD-at-3.f resolution point must be present AND the body must state that references do not resolve against a sha that does not yet exist. |

### FR-FLOW-095 — Every pre-merge loop residual enters the wave-boundary issue ledger

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The wave-boundary issue triage at Phase 3.m SHALL take as its input the residual of every pre-merge verification loop the run produced — loops D, W and H — in addition to loop P's `verification.residual[]`, so that a finding closed as pass-with-residual cannot cross a wave boundary unrecorded; loop L's residual joins that union in `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes` together with loop L itself.

#### Rationale

05 §8's input union, discharging `00.charter.md:91-92` (new issues must not be carried silently into the next stage). The shared engine supports a pass-with-residual closing path and every loop uses it, so an input list naming loop P alone lets a loop that passed with residuals carry them past 3.m, past `P-WAVE-ISSUES-CLOSED` and into the next wave with nothing recording them. Adding the other loops to the union is the narrowest fix; leaving them out left the charter clause half-implemented.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All three variants name loops D, W, H and P as the phase-1 residual sources of the 3.m triage input union; a variant naming loop P alone fails.
- [x] AC-2: All three variants name loop L as re-joining the union in `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes` and do not list it as a phase-1 source, because phase 1 has no loop L.
- [x] AC-3: All three variants record that the shared engine's pass-with-residual closing path is what makes a passing loop still able to carry residuals, so the union is not vacuous on a clean run.
- [x] AC-4: All three variants state that a residual entering the union receives a classification from the closed six-value wave-boundary list and is subject to `P-WAVE-ISSUES-CLOSED`, so an unclassified residual leaves `wave-issues-open` firing at Phase 3.m.
- [x] AC-5: The requirement's Verification Evidence carries an explicit runtime-lag note recording that the change lands in bundled text under `skills/{claude,codex,etc}/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md` while the running agent reads the installed copy at `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md`, which does not carry this text, because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids `speckiwi init`, `skills install` and `upgrade --apply` against this repository.

AC-3 asserts the pass-with-residual mechanism and the explicit "not vacuous on a clean run" clause, because without it a reader could take the union to be dead code on a passing wave. |

### FR-FLOW-096 — kiwi-orchestrator route classification at Phase 1.c-prime from a frozen route probe

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [routing design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/09.routing-design.md), [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The `kiwi-orchestrator` skill body SHALL place route classification at Phase 1.c′ — after the three intake investigators at 1.b and after 1.c's gap-closing QnA, before design authoring at 1.d, and before any SRS mutation, any target registration and any plan authoring — and SHALL require the route probe to be persisted to `docs/research/{work}/routing/probe.json` before the `route-proposal` gate is raised, with each of the probe's thirteen fields recording `producer`, `call`, `value` and `read_at`, and with no predicate permitted to read a value absent from that file.

#### Rationale

Charter `00.charter.md:69-73` requires the orchestrator to judge scale and scope as its own judgment about the work rather than as a mode read from configuration, and the decision has exactly one legal position in the phase flow: 1.c′ is the first point at which the classifier's whole input set exists and is produced for every rung, it is before 1.d which is the first rung-specific expense (a body-scope design document is worthless to `R-STEP`, which authors its own SDS, and unnecessary for `R-PLAN`, whose requirements and plan already exist), and it is before Phase 3.b, which is the run's first SRS mutation — so nothing irreversible in `docs/spec/` has happened yet. Freezing the probe to disk before the gate is what makes the decision auditable: a reviewer can re-run the classifier over the recorded file and reproduce the rung. From `09` §2.1, §2.2, §2.3, §3.2 and §9.1, and `05` §2.1a.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All three bundled variants place Phase 1.c′ between 1.c and 1.d in the phase flow, naming the two artifacts it writes before the gate (`routing/probe.json` via verb `probe-route`, and the English prose file `routing/00.routing.md`) and the one it writes after the gate (`routing/route.lock.json` via verb `freeze-route`).
- [x] AC-2: The skill body enumerates the probe's thirteen fields — S1, S2, S3, S3c, S4, S5, S6, S7, S8, S9, S10, S11 and S12 — each with a named producer and a named tool call, and states that every field records `producer`, `call`, `value` and `read_at`.
- [x] AC-3: The body states that no predicate may read a value absent from `probe.json`.
- [x] AC-4: The body states what has not happened at 1.c′: no `add_requirement`, `update_status` or `update_stability` call, no target registered, no plan authored, and no routing artifact committed — the run's first SRS mutation is Phase 3.b.
- [x] AC-5: The body states what has happened at 1.c′: the run root is pinned (P.1), the regression baseline is captured and pinned (P.4), the run lock is held on the git common dir (P.5), the integration branch `kiwi/orch/{run_id}/integration` is created or adopted (0.b) with two `commit-run-artifacts` commits already on it, and the work-mode has been read and not written (P.3).
- [x] AC-6: The body states that the probe's magnitude fields drive no threshold: `S5.files` is recorded for the gate's evidence table and the user-facing prose only, `existing_srs_context.json.similarity_score` is recorded and drives no threshold, `S9.summary` counts requirements already in the target rather than incoming work, and the wave splitter is not called at 1.c′.
- [x] AC-7: The verification evidence for this requirement records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed path that does not yet hold this text — `~/.claude/skills/` carries no `kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md` — because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids running `speckiwi init`, `speckiwi skills install` or `speckiwi upgrade --apply` against this repository, so no reinstall is available here.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | Test | test/skills/kiwi-orchestrator-routing.fr-flow-096.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | 51 passed in this file. AC-1's position is proved by offset ordering inside the phase-flow section — 1.c, then 1.c-prime, then 1.d — rather than by all three merely being present. AC-2 counts the probe table's S-rows and requires exactly 13, then asserts each id individually, so a table that drops or duplicates a field fails on the count rather than passing on the presence of the rest. AC-6 is the negative half that keeps the amended magnitude ruling honest: S5.files, similarity_score and S9.summary must each be stated as driving no threshold, and the wave splitter must be stated as not called at 1.c-prime. |

### FR-FLOW-097 — Disqualifier-first routing with a fixed selection order and a traceable decision of record

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [routing design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/09.routing-design.md), [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The `kiwi-orchestrator` skill body SHALL specify routing as disqualifier-first — every predicate removes rungs and none selects one — with the first surviving rung in the fixed order `R-PLAN → R-STEP → R-ORCH` selected, `R-ORCH` removed by no predicate so the ladder always terminates at exactly one rung, and SHALL require that a wrong route be traceable either to one named predicate and one recorded observed value in `route.lock.json`'s `removed[]`, or, when the gate selected the alternative, to the recorded `proposed_rung`, `overridden_by` and ballot resolution in the same lock.

#### Rationale

The disqualifier-first shape is the property the whole routing design is built to buy: because no predicate selects a rung, a misroute cannot be attributed to an unnamed aggregate judgment and must instead name one predicate and one value, which is what makes the classifier's recall measurable at all through `routing/misroute-{n}.json`. The fixed order puts `R-PLAN` first because its surviving preconditions are the strongest (a contract-valid plan, on the active target, with open Tasks, no blocked stability, and whose requirement ids intersect what this work's files are already anchored to) and `R-STEP` before `R-ORCH` because D3 and D4 remove `R-STEP` for exactly the breadth and ordering conditions that make a run orchestration-shaped. From `09` §3.1, §3.3, §3.4, §8.3 and §9.2, and `05` §2.1a.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The body names the eight disqualifiers D1 through D8 with, for each, its predicate over named probe fields, the rung or rungs it removes, and its threshold in a stated unit; the body states that each removes rungs and none selects one.
- [x] AC-2: The selection order is stated as the fixed sequence `R-PLAN → R-STEP → R-ORCH` with the first survivor winning, and the body states that no predicate removes `R-ORCH`, so the ladder always terminates at exactly one rung.
- [x] AC-3: The body states that the classifier contains no tie-breaking rule because ties cannot arise inside it, and that the only place a tie can arise is the presentation gate's ballot.
- [x] AC-4: The body states that D8's map from an unreadable probe field to the rung it protects is total: `S3`, `S3c`, `S4`, `S5`, `S7`, `S8` and `S12` remove `R-STEP`; `S2`, `S9` and `S10` remove `R-PLAN`; `S1` and `S6` remove nothing; and `R-ORCH` is never removed.
- [x] AC-5: `route.lock.json` records `rung`, `proposed_rung`, `overridden_by`, `alternative`, `decisive` and a `removed[]` array whose rows carry `{rung, by, observed}`, plus the probe path and probe digest.
- [x] AC-6: The body states that on either override branch — user or committee — the lock records the alternative as `rung`, the classifier's selection as `proposed_rung`, `overridden_by` as `"user"` or `"committee"`, and the ballot's resolution row, and that `computeRoute` is not re-run after an override.
- [x] AC-7: The body states that the ballot is closed over the classifier's own outputs — the selected rung plus `decision.alternative`, or the selected rung plus `abort` when `alternative` is `null` — so an override changes which computed survivor executes and can never introduce a rung the classifier did not produce.
- [x] AC-8: The verification evidence for this requirement records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed path that does not yet hold this text — `~/.claude/skills/` carries no `kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md` — because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids a reinstall against this repository.

### FR-FLOW-098 — Work-mode never removes the step rung and both mode-related routing gates are user decisions

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [routing design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/09.routing-design.md), [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The `kiwi-orchestrator` skill body SHALL state that the persisted work-mode never removes the `R-STEP` rung; that a classifier/mode disagreement — the selected rung is `R-STEP` and `S1.mode` is not `tdd` — raises the `business-decision` gate `route-step-requires-mode-switch` with the three options `switch-and-step`, `stay-and-orchestrate` and `abort`, only `stay-and-orchestrate` carrying `recommended: true`; that the orchestrator never calls `set_work_mode` on its own authority; and that under `--auto` a surviving `R-STEP` selection raises the `business-decision` gate `tdd-route-unattended` with `orchestrate-instead` recommended rather than disqualifying the rung.

#### Rationale

`00.charter.md:71` requires the routing judgment to be the orchestrator's own judgment about the work and not a mode read from configuration; since the default work-mode is `wait` and `wait` is also the fail-open value, a design in which `mode ≠ tdd` hard-removes the step rung would make the user's own named route structurally unreachable on every repository that never ran `set_work_mode`. Symmetrically, `--auto` on a child that declares no `critical_gates[]` is a silent ignore rather than a failure, so converting "this child ignores a flag" into "this child may never be selected" would disable the cheapest rung in exactly the mode the user chose to reduce supervision. Both collisions are therefore put on a gate rather than resolved silently in either direction. From `09` §4.1 through §4.5 and §12 alternatives 5 and 6.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The body states that work-mode is not a disqualifier and records the ground: the routing judgment is the orchestrator's own, the default mode is `wait`, and `wait` is also the fail-open value.
- [x] AC-2: The body enumerates the four classifier/mode cases and their recorded outcomes: `R-STEP` with mode `tdd` dispatches `/kiwi-tdd`; a rung other than `R-STEP` because D1 through D4 removed the step rung with mode `tdd` routes on and records one non-fatal WARN naming the mode and the rung; `R-STEP` surviving but `R-PLAN` winning on the fixed order with mode `tdd` routes `R-PLAN` and records one non-fatal WARN naming the mode, the rung and the overridden routing clause; and `R-STEP` with a mode other than `tdd` raises `route-step-requires-mode-switch`.
- [x] AC-3: `route-step-requires-mode-switch` is declared `business-decision`, is absent from `critical_gates[]`, and carries three options each with a consequence line, with the `recommended` marker on `stay-and-orchestrate` only; the body states that `switch-and-step` writes the persisted project-wide work-mode to `docs/spec/steps/state.md` and that `abort` mutates no requirement, target, plan or work-mode.
- [x] AC-4: The body states that when `S1.source` is `default-wait` the `recommended` marker on `route-step-requires-mode-switch` is withheld entirely, so an unread mode setting can never buy the zero-deliberation path in either direction, and that with three options a 1-1-1 committee split has no majority and halts.
- [x] AC-5: `tdd-route-unattended` is declared `business-decision`, is evaluated after `route-step-requires-mode-switch`, and fires only when the rung is still `R-STEP` after that gate resolved and that gate did not resolve to `switch-and-step`; its two options are `proceed-step` and `orchestrate-instead`, with `recommended: true` on `orchestrate-instead`.
- [x] AC-6: The body states that the orchestrator never calls `set_work_mode` on its own authority and that the only path that writes the mode is a human or committee selection of `switch-and-step` at the gate.
- [x] AC-7: The body states that `tdd_policy` keeps flowing on every rung: mode `tdd` still derives `tdd_policy = strict` in every child Task even when the work routes to `R-ORCH`, so the mode is not neutralised and only its routing clause is.
- [x] AC-8: The verification evidence for this requirement records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed paths that still hold the old text — `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-pipeline/SKILL.md` §2.8 and `~/.claude/skills/_shared/kiwi/workmode-policy.md`, with no `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md` present at all — because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids a reinstall against this repository.

### FR-FLOW-099 — Per-rung route table with preconditions, invocation form, flag propagation and post-return obligation

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [routing design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/09.routing-design.md), [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The `kiwi-orchestrator` skill body SHALL carry a route table stating, for each of the three rungs, the artifacts that must exist before invocation, the exact invocation form, the flag propagation set, and the orchestrator's obligation after the child returns, including on `R-PLAN` a plan-coverage close-out that verifies every requirement in the probe's open-Task `req_ids` set reached `verified` or carries a recorded residual, rather than claiming a design-layer verdict for which the rung has no externally fixed denominator.

#### Rationale

Two of the three rungs are delegations that end the orchestrator's run, so the contract with each child has to be written where the orchestrator can be held to it: the preconditions are what the child would otherwise halt on, the flag set is where an unpropagated `--auto` stalls an unattended run and an over-propagated flag addresses a gate that does not exist on that path, and the post-return obligation is the only place a delegated run acquires closure. The `R-PLAN` close-out is denominator-honest by construction — the rung has no frozen design-item set, so it verifies the plan's requirement set, which is the denominator it does have. From `09` §5.1, §5.2, §5.3, §9.2, and `05` §13's `plan-coverage-unclosed` row and §14.1.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The `R-STEP` row states its preconditions: a frozen `routing/probe.json` and `routing/route.lock.json` with `rung = "R-STEP"`; a kebab `<task>` name of at most 40 characters derived deterministically from the intake summary; the work-overview paragraph already written at 1.c as `docs/research/{work}/01.intake.md`; `S1.mode == "tdd"`; and no design document, because 1.d has not run and must not since `kiwi-tdd` authors its own SDS.
- [x] AC-2: The `R-STEP` row states the invocation as `Skill({ skill: "kiwi-tdd", args: "<task> [--auto] [--mini | --loops N] [--model <name>]" })`, and states the flag set: `--mini`/`--loops N` propagate, `--model` propagates when the user specified it, `--auto` is passed for uniformity and is silently ignored by the child so the orchestrator must not read its lack of effect as an error, and `--max` and the four pass-throughs `--auto-integration`, `--auto-cost-warning`, `--force` and `--regression-baseline` are not propagated.
- [x] AC-3: The `R-STEP` row states three post-return outcomes: on promotion, record `outcome: "delegated-complete"` on the `dispatch-route` result line, release the P.5 run lock, leave the integration branch in place and name it in the run report, run `validate` then `sync-index` then `validate --fail-on-warning` with `post-merge-index-drift` (critical) firing on surviving drift, emit one `pipeline.jsonl` event with `next_hint: null` and a summary naming the rung and the step, then halt; on a halt at one of `kiwi-tdd`'s own gates, report verbatim and stop without re-routing; on a boundary redirect, escalate through E1.
- [x] AC-4: The `R-PLAN` row states its preconditions: a frozen lock with `rung = "R-PLAN"`; `PLAN_PATH` resolved explicitly by the orchestrator from the probe's plan field and never left to `kiwi-pm`'s newest-`generated_at` fallback; the active target confirmed non-empty and equal to the plan's target; and no design document and no `/kiwi-srs` run.
- [x] AC-5: The `R-PLAN` row states the `kiwi-pm` invocation form with `PLAN_PATH`, and states that `--resume` is passed exactly when `.kiwi/sessions/{plan run_id}/pm-state.json` exists, because that file and not the task catalogue statuses is what makes `kiwi-pm` skip `status="done"` Tasks and without it the run is a new session that re-executes completed Tasks.
- [x] AC-6: The `R-PLAN` row states the second hop `Skill({ skill: "kiwi-review-fix-loop", args: "--close-reqs …" })` on `TASK_DONE` as the orchestrator's own declared policy rather than an inherited obligation, with the ground that without `--close-reqs` no requirement reaches `verified` and the run has no closure.
- [x] AC-7: The `R-PLAN` flag set states that `--auto` is propagated explicitly, that `--max`, `--model` and `--mini`/`--loops N` propagate, and that `--auto-cost-warning`, `--auto-integration` and `--force` flow only when the user specified them while `--regression-baseline` always carries the orchestrator's own P.4 pin.
- [x] AC-8: The `R-PLAN` close-out states that `plan-coverage-unclosed` is critical and fires on any member of the plan's open-Task `req_ids` set that is neither `verified` nor named by a `coverage_residual[]` row, on any member that is `verified` while an acceptance criterion of it is uncovered by evidence and no residual row names it with a reason and an owner, and, regardless of every row's reason and owner, when the residual row count exceeds `max(--allow-plan-residual, ceil(|req_ids| / 4))`; residual rows are `{req_id, reason, owner}` on the `R-PLAN` `dispatch-route` result line in `kiwi/waves.jsonl` with `reason` at least 20 characters.
- [x] AC-9: The `R-PLAN` row states that `kiwi-review-fix-loop --close-reqs` skips and reports a `stability=draft` or non-`implemented` requirement without gating on it, so its `TASK_DONE` is not evidence that the requirement set closed, and that the close-out is followed by `validate`, `sync-index` and `validate --fail-on-warning` with `post-merge-index-drift` (critical) before the single closing event.
- [x] AC-10: The `R-ORCH` row states that nothing beyond the probe must exist before entering because the rung produces its own prerequisites, and states the per-wave delegation by name to `/kiwi-srs`, `/kiwi-planner` and `/kiwi-pm`, with the `/kiwi-review-fix-loop` remediation hop run over the wave's commit range on the integration branch and carrying neither `--commit-lane-work` nor `--close-reqs`.
- [x] AC-11: The body states two inherited safety rules on the delegated rungs: a child returning `NEEDS_USER` or `FAILED` halts the parent even under `--auto`, and a gate a child bubbles from its own `critical_gates[]` halts unconditionally even with no same-named row in the orchestrator's table.
- [x] AC-12: The verification evidence for this requirement records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed paths that still hold the old text — `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-tdd/SKILL.md`, `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-pm/SKILL.md` and `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-review-fix-loop/SKILL.md`, with no `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md` present at all — because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids a reinstall against this repository.

### FR-FLOW-100 — R-ORCH drives the shared wave engine directly and declares the wave-semantic critical gates

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [routing design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/09.routing-design.md), [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The `kiwi-orchestrator` skill body SHALL state that on `R-ORCH` the orchestrator drives the extracted `_shared/kiwi/` wave engine directly with the artifact root passed as a parameter, that `kiwi-orchestrator` never invokes `kiwi-wave-master`, that an explicit user request naming `kiwi-wave-master` is not intercepted — the orchestrator reports that the request names a sibling skill and stops without classifying and without starting a run — and that the wave-semantic critical gates are declared in the orchestrator's own `critical_gates[]` table rather than inherited, because `_shared` modules are not children, nothing bubbles a `gate_id`, and `_shared` modules carry no gate declarations.

#### Rationale

After the D1 extraction the engine lives in shared modules whose artifact root is a parameter, so invoking the sibling skill to reach modules the orchestrator already references is the duplicated path the charter forbids; the sibling's input contract also does not fit (it requires an input document it did not write and would re-derive design items from a baseline the orchestrator authored and froze), its run-scoped pins collide with the orchestrator's own regression baseline and journal root, and a nested run mints a second `run_id` under a second engine into one journal for one logical run, which defeats the very discriminator v1.4.0 adds. The gate half is the correction that matters: the general child-gate inheritance rule requires a running child bubbling a `gate_id` from its own table, and at `R-ORCH` there is no child — so an undeclared gate falls to `business-decision` and a committee approves it under `--auto`. From `09` §5.3, §5.4, §6 and §12 alternatives 11 and 12, and `05` §9.1 and §13.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The body states that `R-ORCH` is not a skill invocation: the orchestrator continues its own flow calling into `_shared/kiwi/wave-decomposition.md`, `_shared/kiwi/verify-loop.md` and `_shared/kiwi/run-ledger.md`, passing `docs/research/{work}/` as the artifact root where `kiwi-wave-master` passes `docs/analysis/kiwi-wave-master-{run_id}/`.
- [x] AC-2: The body carries the rule that `kiwi-orchestrator` never invokes `kiwi-wave-master`, together with its four grounds: the sibling is the duplicated path after the extraction, its input contract does not fit a design the orchestrator authored and froze, its run-scoped journal-root and regression-baseline pins collide with the orchestrator's own P.4 pin, and a nested run writes lines under a second `run_id` and a second engine into one journal for one logical run.
- [x] AC-3: The body states that an explicit user request naming `kiwi-wave-master` is not intercepted, is not classified, and starts no run — the orchestrator reports that the request names a sibling skill and stops.
- [x] AC-4: The orchestrator's own `critical_gates[]` table declares all thirteen wave-semantic gates in all three variants: `wave-verify-residual-critical`, `wave-verify-fail-residual`, `wave-verify-cross-wave-fix-required`, `final-verify-residual-critical`, `wave-decomposition-coverage-gap`, `out-of-scope-user-consent`, `wave-append-cap-exhausted`, `decomposition-input-missing`, `child-srs-needs-user-or-failed`, `child-pipeline-needs-user-or-failed`, `unsafe-option-refused`, `wt-delegation-refused` and `invalid-loop-option`.
- [x] AC-5: The body records why those gates are declared rather than inherited — `_shared` modules are not children, nothing bubbles a `gate_id`, gate declaration is per-skill by construction, and a gate with no declared severity falls to `business-decision` so a committee approves it under `--auto` — so a later session does not delete them as redundant.
- [x] AC-6: The body states that per-wave delegation calls `/kiwi-srs`, `/kiwi-planner`, `/kiwi-pm` and `/kiwi-review-fix-loop` individually rather than entering `/kiwi-pipeline --cycle`, and that there is no `/kiwi-srs-feasibility` hop on this rung.
- [x] AC-7: The verification evidence for this requirement records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed paths that still hold the old text — `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-wave-master/SKILL.md`, which still inlines the engine, and `~/.claude/skills/_shared/kiwi/`, which holds none of the new modules, with no `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md` present at all — because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids a reinstall against this repository.

### FR-FLOW-101 — One-way route escalation with sealed carry, landed-state gating and a read-never-recomputed rung on resume

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [routing design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/09.routing-design.md), [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The `kiwi-orchestrator` skill body SHALL state that route escalation is one-way — `R-STEP → R-ORCH` and `R-PLAN → R-ORCH` — with the named triggers and carry manifests for each, that work already completed is carried into the `R-ORCH` design baseline sealed `out_of_scope` with `exclusion_class = "already-implemented"` and is never re-planned as a Task, that `route-escalation-after-landed-state` is critical and fires when the escalation follows a commit on the integration branch or an SRS mutation that moved a requirement's `status` or its `stability` while an escalation detected before the first red test is free and ungated, that de-escalation is refused after Phase 3.b through the critical gate `route-deescalation-refused`, and that on resume the rung is read from `route.lock.json` and never recomputed.

#### Rationale

The journals do not permit de-escalation — `complete` is append-only with no reverse edge and resume skips completed waves forever — so the only honest exits are escalation upward and a terminal `abort-run` that names the repository state the user is left holding. Gating on what has landed rather than on the transition is what keeps unattended runs alive: the cheap escalation, detected before the first red test, does not halt, and the expensive one always does, whereas gating a free escalation would teach the agent to hide the trigger. The `stability` clause is load-bearing because a feasibility pass mutates `stability` target-wide and not `status`, so a condition testing only `status` would classify a post-feasibility escalation as free. From `09` §7.1 through §7.5 and §9.5.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The body names E1's two triggers with the moment each is observable and its cost — the SDS approaching the 200-line cap, observable at the child's Phase 2 before the first red test at near-zero cost, and an existing body-requirement edit detected or `promote_step_requirement` returning `MUTATION_DENIED`, observable at the child's Phase 6 after the implementation is already written — and names `EVIDENCE_REQUIRED` on promote and `COMPLETION_GATE_BLOCKED` on merge as explicitly not triggers, because those are defects in the run and escalating on a quality failure lets a stuck run climb until it runs out of rungs.
- [x] AC-2: The body names E2's three triggers: three accumulated `NEEDS_USER` on one Task whose resolution is that the Task needs requirements that do not exist; a `FAILED` return naming missing prior work; and the child's lifecycle gate stopping on an empty active target, which is a classifier defect because D7 should have caught it at intake; and it states that the `deprecated`/`frozen` branch is deliberately not a trigger because it is unreachable on the only sidecar shape the plan predicate admits.
- [x] AC-3: Every escalation writes `routing/misroute-{n}.json` naming the probe id, the trigger, the predicate that should have fired and the value it would have needed; on E1's second trigger and on E2's empty-target trigger it additionally names the file whose anchor should have hit and the observed anchor coverage.
- [x] AC-4: The carry manifests are stated per trigger: the step's `design.md` and `intent.md` become research inputs to 1.d and then per-wave `--research-doc` arguments; on the post-green trigger the merged tests and implementation stay on the integration branch and are sealed in the `R-ORCH` design baseline as `out_of_scope` with `exclusion_class = "already-implemented"`; the body states that green implementation is never re-planned as a Task because every code Task runs through a mandatory red confirmation that carried green code would fail.
- [x] AC-5: The body states that the `already-implemented` seal fires `out-of-scope-user-consent` even under `--auto`, so no escalation that discards work from a denominator is silent.
- [x] AC-6: The body states the lease hygiene on escalation from `R-STEP`: the orchestrator calls `update_step_state(<task>, "abandoned")` and does not call `update_step_state(<task>, "merged")`, because the step's requirement will be authored at body scope by `/kiwi-srs` instead.
- [x] AC-7: `route-escalation-after-landed-state` is declared in `critical_gates[]` and fires when the escalation follows either a commit on the integration branch or an SRS mutation that moved a requirement's `status` or its `stability`; an escalation detected before the first red test is stated as free and ungated.
- [x] AC-8: `route-deescalation-refused` is declared in `critical_gates[]` and fires on any attempt to move down a rung after Phase 3.b; the body states that the sole legal downgrade is `route-downgrade-available` at the end of Phase 2 and nowhere later, and that the terminal exit is `abort-run`, which leaves the integration branch and everything already written under `docs/research/{work}/` in place, releases the run lock, and names that state in the run report.
- [x] AC-9: The body states that on resume the rung is read from `frozen.route.rung` and never recomputed, that a `frozen.route.probe_digest` mismatch against `probe.json` raises the critical gate `run-invariant-drift`, and that `computeRoute` runs exactly once per run because a resumed session has no conversation and no investigators.
- [x] AC-10: The verification evidence for this requirement records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed path that does not yet hold this text — `~/.claude/skills/` carries no `kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md` — because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids a reinstall against this repository.

AC-1's two non-triggers are asserted alongside the reason, because naming EVIDENCE_REQUIRED and COMPLETION_GATE_BLOCKED as triggers is the failure mode this criterion exists to prevent — a stuck run climbing rungs on a quality failure. ### FR-FLOW-102 — Serial host-root execution of the frozen partition with the parallelisability analysis still produced

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [scope and frame decisions](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/08.scope-and-frame-decisions.md) |

#### Requirement

The `kiwi-orchestrator` skill body SHALL state that implementation in this target runs serially at the host root on the run's integration branch — one unit of the frozen partition at a time, in stage order and within a stage in lane-id order, each waited for before the next begins, through one `/kiwi-pm` run per handoff — that the run therefore delivers no wall-clock speedup, and that the per-task parallelisability analysis is nonetheless produced in full, frozen, published and reviewed per wave.

#### Rationale

The scope decision for this target keeps the partition analysis and drops concurrent execution, so the executor is one `/kiwi-pm` run per handoff at the host root; stating the absence of speedup plainly is what keeps the target honest, and keeping the analysis is what makes the deferred phase buildable from a reviewed artifact rather than re-derived. The five flags on the invocation are not optional decoration: without `--commit-lane-work` `kiwi-pm` commits nothing and the wave's output is left as an uncommitted working tree the next unit trips over, and without `--session-suffix` the units share one session directory. From `05` §5.14, §1.3, §2.1 and §13.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The body carries the executor invocation in all three variants — `/kiwi-pm --handoff docs/research/{work}/waves/wave-{n}/lanes/lane-{k}.md --session-suffix w{n}s{s}l{k} --no-final --no-pipeline-emit --commit-lane-work` with the optional `[--resume] [--auto] [--max] [--model <name>] [--mini|--loops N] [--regression-baseline <the P.4 pin>]` — run at the host root on `frozen.integration_branch`, one at a time.
- [x] AC-2: The body states the six differences from a concurrent dispatch with a reason for each: no worktree and no unit branch; no `--defer-srs-mutation`, so `kiwi-coder`'s four mandatory MCP mutations execute at the host root against the run's own target; no claim audit, because the commit range on the integration branch is the record and is derived rather than reported; `--commit-lane-work` is still required; commits keep the five `Orch-*` trailers `Orch-Run`, `Orch-Wave`, `Orch-Stage`, `Orch-Lane` and `Orch-Task` and carry no subject marker; and the non-code and SRS-write scheduling edges change a unit's position rather than its executor, landing such tasks in the serial epilogue executed order-last at 3.k.
- [x] AC-3: `serial-unit-failed` is declared in `critical_gates[]` with its three disjuncts: a unit whose `verification_cmd` exited non-zero after one retry against the same handoff, a unit that produced no commit and declared no `intentionally_empty` reason in its `/kiwi-pm` run's own `docs/analysis/` bundle, or a unit whose `/kiwi-pm` returned `NEEDS_USER` or `FAILED`.
- [x] AC-4: The intentionally-empty disposition is stated as per task in the unit's `docs/analysis/kiwi-pm-…` bundle, carrying the task id and a reason of at least 20 characters, admissible only when that task's `verification_cmd` exits zero and no path in the task's `write_set` differs between the unit's base and head on the integration branch; the task stays in `expected` and enters `checked` rather than being removed from the denominator, and for promotion it counts as landed with test-kind evidence only and no commit-kind reference.
- [x] AC-5: The body states that `validate` followed by `sync-index` runs at 3.k, after the last unit of the last stage and before the post-merge verification loop, with `post-merge-index-drift` (critical) unchanged, and records that this target performs no merge so charter C4's obligation attaches to the point at which SRS-affecting work has landed.
- [x] AC-6: The body states that the partition is frozen at 3.e′, published, and reviewed per wave, with `partition-review-unrecorded` (critical) refusing a wave that has no `review-partition` result line whose recorded lane-plan digest equals the digest frozen at 3.e′ and whose verdict is `pass`, and it states plainly that this target delivers no wall-clock speedup.
- [x] AC-7: The body states the resume contract for the executor: the verb is `execute-unit` with recovery class externally-visible, recovery reads the `Orch-Task` trailers on the integration branch together with `workflow_plan_status` and re-enters `/kiwi-pm` only for Tasks carrying neither a trailer nor a checked box, and `--resume` is passed when `.kiwi/sessions/{plan_run_id}/lanes/w{n}s{s}l{k}/pm-state.json` exists.
- [x] AC-8: The verification evidence for this requirement records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed paths that still hold the old text — `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-pm/SKILL.md`, which carries none of the five flags, with no `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md` present at all — because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids a reinstall against this repository.

AC-1 asserts each of the five mandatory flags and each optional flag individually plus the walk order, so dropping --commit-lane-work — which would leave the wave's output uncommitted for the next unit to trip over — fails on its own assertion rather than being absorbed by a looser match. AC-4's two admissibility conjuncts are asserted together with the sentence that the unchanged-write-set half is recomputed by the orchestrator rather than asserted by the unit, which is what stops the escape being a self-report. ### FR-FLOW-103 — Four business-decision routing gates declared in the skill body and kept out of critical_gates

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [routing design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/09.routing-design.md), [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [auto contract decisions](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/07.auto-contract-decisions.md) |

#### Requirement

The `kiwi-orchestrator` skill body SHALL declare the four `business-decision` routing gates — `route-proposal`, `route-step-requires-mode-switch`, `tdd-route-unattended` and `route-downgrade-available` — with their severities and firing conditions in all three variants, and SHALL keep all four out of `critical_gates[]`, recording that `route-proposal` fires on every run so promoting it would halt every unattended run at its first decision; the explicit declaration is what pins the confidence threshold of at least 0.7 rather than leaving the severity to the undeclared-gate default.

#### Rationale

`critical_gates[]` membership means halt regardless of `--auto`, so promoting a gate that fires on every run stalls one hundred percent of unattended runs at their first decision — the same objection already sustained against promoting the three `--auto` gates. The shared contract would classify an undeclared gate as `business-decision` anyway, so the declaration buys something different and specific: it pins the confidence threshold and puts the confidence de-rating in play, and it puts the four ids into the cross-variant identifier parity assertions. `route-downgrade-available` is the design's only mitigation for over-routing to the total default rung, and its recommended marker deliberately sits on the expensive option so an unattended run is never silently downgraded. From `09` §8.1, §8.2, §8.3 and §7.4, and `05` §13 and §1.4.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All four gate ids appear in the skill body of all three variants with severity `business-decision` and with the condition under which each fires: `route-proposal` on every run at 1.c′; `route-step-requires-mode-switch` when the rung is `R-STEP` and the mode is not `tdd`; `tdd-route-unattended` when the rung is still `R-STEP`, `--auto` is active, and `route-step-requires-mode-switch` did not resolve to `switch-and-step`; and `route-downgrade-available` at the end of Phase 2.
- [x] AC-2: None of the four appears in the `critical_gates[]` table in any variant, while the three critical routing gates `route-probe-unreadable`, `route-escalation-after-landed-state` and `route-deescalation-refused` do appear there with a reason and a location.
- [x] AC-3: The body records why `route-proposal` is not critical: it fires on every run, `critical_gates[]` membership means halt regardless of `--auto`, and the committee never overrides a critical halt — so promoting it would stall every unattended run at its first decision.
- [x] AC-4: The body records that declaring the severity is what pins the confidence threshold of at least 0.7 and puts the confidence de-rating in play, rather than relying on the shared contract's undeclared-gate default that would classify the gate `business-decision` anyway.
- [x] AC-5: `route-proposal`'s ballot is stated as the selected rung plus `decision.alternative`, or the selected rung plus `abort` when `alternative` is `null`; with two options and three members a tie is arithmetically impossible, and the residual halt is a degraded quorum, which escalates to critical and halts.
- [x] AC-6: The body states the five clauses under which `route-proposal` carries `recommended: true` — the unreadable field list is empty; `decisive` is non-`null` and either has margin at least 1 in its own unit or is a boolean corroborated by another predicate that also fired; the ambiguity count is zero after 1.c's QnA; when the rung is `R-STEP` the mode source is not `default-wait`; and when the rung is `R-STEP` cleared by measurement the anchor coverage is at least 0.2 — and states that `withheld_because[]` names the clause that failed.
- [x] AC-7: The body states that committee input carries the probe table and the removal table and never the main session's tentative proposal, that the user-facing prose file is what the user reads while the committee reads evidence, and that a fast-pathed decision writes an audit row carrying `{"rule": "recommended-fastpath", "committee_size": 0, "marked_by": …}` to the run's decision audit log.
- [x] AC-8: `route-downgrade-available` fires only at the end of Phase 2 and only when all three conditions hold: the decomposition returned exactly one wave; no disqualifier removed `R-STEP` at 1.c′; and no SRS mutation on this run has moved a requirement's `status` or `stability`. The wave's `design_items` count appears in the gate's evidence table and in no predicate.
- [x] AC-9: `route-downgrade-available` offers `continue-orchestrated`, carrying the `recommended` marker, and `downgrade-to-step`; the body states that selecting `downgrade-to-step` when the mode is not `tdd` re-raises `route-step-requires-mode-switch` and only `switch-and-step` proceeds, that `freeze-route` writes a new `route.lock.json` under the verb `downgrade-route` whose digest re-enters `invariant_digest`, and that the design document and the decomposition stay on disk as sunk artifacts named in the run report and are not passed to the child.
- [x] AC-10: The verification evidence for this requirement records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed paths that still hold the old text — `~/.claude/skills/_shared/kiwi/auto-option.md`, which still carries the pre-amendment contract, with no `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md` present at all — because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids a reinstall against this repository.

### FR-FLOW-104 — waves-event.md v1.4.0 additive field set across all four shipped copies

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [routing design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/09.routing-design.md) |

#### Requirement

`_shared/kiwi/waves-event.md` SHALL be raised to v1.4.0, adding additively — without changing the reading of any already-recorded event — the twenty fields `engine`, `writer`, `event`, `verb`, `inputs_digest`, `lane`, `stage`, `lane_plan`, `partition_review`, `isolation`, `lane_layer`, `wave_issues`, `convergence`, `allocation`, `decision`, `deadline_at`, `postmortem`, `coverage_residual`, `lane_disposition` and `card_digest`, with `allocation` carrying `pre_snapshot_digest` and `design_item_map`, together with the extended `phase` enum and the `reason_class` additions `oscillation` and `budget-exhausted`.

#### Rationale

The journal is the only ledger that survives compaction, and each added field carries a payload an earlier revision required in prose and gave no field — the allocation set and its design-item map, the per-decision audit record, the lane deadline, the postmortem pointer, the isolation selection record, a lane's terminal disposition, the 3.e′ partition verdict, and the `R-PLAN` coverage residual the `plan-coverage-unclosed` gate reads. The set is enumerated in a requirement rather than left implicit because the schema is asserted set-equal against it: a field a gate requires and this enumeration omits produces a schema that drops or rejects it. The `wave_issues` naming is fixed before the version ships because renaming a field after v1.4.0 ships is not an additive minor. From `05` §4.2 and §14 registration 5.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All twenty fields appear in the field table of all four shipped copies — the `claude`, `codex` and `etc` variants plus the `.agents` mirror — each with a type and a purpose, and the document version reads v1.4.0.
- [x] AC-2: The change is additive: the document states that nothing changes the reading of an already-recorded event, and that within a run containing any 1.4.0 line a later line with a lower `schema_version` is a `journal-version-downgrade` diagnostic.
- [x] AC-3: The field is named `wave_issues` in every copy and the string `stage_issues` does not occur, because the artifact is wave-scoped.
- [x] AC-4: `engine` is documented as `kiwi-wave-master | kiwi-orchestrator` with an absent value reading as `kiwi-wave-master`, and `writer` as `speckiwi-orchestrate/{pkgVersion}` stamped by the tool on every write, required only on lines whose `schema_version` is at least 1.4.0 while older lines report `unstamped` and never fail.
- [x] AC-5: The object-valued fields carry their declared members: `isolation` carries `profile`, `reason` and `rejected[]`; `allocation` carries `target`, `pre_snapshot_digest`, `requirement_ids[]` and `design_item_map`; `partition_review` carries `doc_path`, `digest`, `lane_plan_digest`, `reviewer` and `verdict`; `coverage_residual` rows are `{req_id, reason, owner}` on the `R-PLAN` `dispatch-route` result line; and `lane_disposition` is `{kind, reason, at}` with `kind` a closed four-value enum.
- [x] AC-6: The `phase` enum gains the eight values `intake`, `design`, `wave-design`, `schedule`, `handoff`, `lane`, `integrate` and `stage-close`, `reason_class` gains `oscillation` and `budget-exhausted`, and every other v1.3.0 field is reused verbatim.
- [x] AC-7: All four copies are edited in one change and the field set is identical across them.
- [x] AC-8: The verification evidence for this requirement records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed path that still holds the old text — `~/.claude/skills/_shared/kiwi/waves-event.md`, which is still at the pre-1.4.0 version — because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids a reinstall against this repository.

### FR-FLOW-105 — Engine-filtered wave resume predicate with absent engine reading as kiwi-wave-master

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The resume predicate documented in `_shared/kiwi/waves-event.md` and applied by both `kiwi-wave-master` and `kiwi-orchestrator` SHALL filter journal lines by `engine`, and SHALL read a line carrying no `engine` field as `engine = "kiwi-wave-master"`, so that a `kiwi-wave-master --resume` never selects an orchestrator run as its most recent incomplete run.

#### Rationale

The journal is shared by two producers and has no producer discriminator today, which is a live defect rather than a hypothetical one: `kiwi-wave-master --resume` selects the most recent incomplete run by run scope alone and would therefore adopt an orchestrator run's lines. Reading an absent `engine` as `kiwi-wave-master` is what makes the discriminator additive — every line written before v1.4.0 was written by that engine, so the default is a fact about the corpus rather than a guess. A single journal file was chosen over a second file precisely because a second file would fork the run-scoped resume predicate and duplicate the completion gate. From `05` §4.2 and §16 alternative R5.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The resume predicate text states the `engine` filter in all four shipped copies of `waves-event.md`.
- [x] AC-2: A line carrying no `engine` field reads as `engine = "kiwi-wave-master"`, and the document states the ground: every line written before v1.4.0 was written by that engine.
- [x] AC-3: The document records the defect this closes — today there is no producer discriminator, so `kiwi-wave-master --resume` can select an orchestrator run as the most recent incomplete run — and states that the closure applies to both skills.
- [x] AC-4: The engine filter composes with the existing run-scoped filter rather than replacing it: a resume reads only lines matching both the current `run_id` and the engine.
- [x] AC-5: The verification evidence for this requirement records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed path that still holds the old text — `~/.claude/skills/_shared/kiwi/waves-event.md`, whose resume predicate carries no engine filter — because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids a reinstall against this repository.

### FR-FLOW-106 — verify-loop.md carries the cross-verification engine denominator-agnostically

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

A shared module `_shared/kiwi/verify-loop.md` v1.0.0 SHALL carry the cross-verification engine — created by moving text rather than by writing text — denominator-agnostically, and `kiwi-wave-master` SHALL reference it from its section 0 table rather than inlining it.

#### Rationale

The verification engine is the whole anti-collusion and anti-skim apparatus, and it is the part of the wave machinery both callers need identically; leaving it inlined would mean the orchestrator either duplicates it or grows its own fixer, and a cheap round cap becomes unsafe the moment the closing paths and their streak cost are restated inexactly. Extracting it denominator-agnostically is what lets one implementation serve a wave denominator, a handoff denominator and a design-baseline denominator without any of them being wired into the loop. The oscillation detector is the one piece of new content, added because the existing termination table has no rule for a finding that opens and closes across rounds. From `05` §9.1 and §7.8.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `_shared/kiwi/verify-loop.md` exists at v1.0.0 in all three variants and in the `.agents` mirror.
- [x] AC-2: The module carries the evidence-bundle rules, the two stances, the externally fixed frozen denominator rule with the invalid-round-on-count-mismatch rule, the isolated then add-only cross-refutation then mechanical union sequence, verifier re-spawn from round 2, the three closing paths with their streak cost, the termination table with its caps and the unreachable-PASS rule, the distinction between `pass+residual` and `fail-residual`, the delegation-only remediation routing table with its explicit-scope requirement and the rule that a sub-loop's own PASS does not close a parent finding, and cross-wave carry-forward by file-set intersection.
- [x] AC-3: The module is denominator-agnostic: it takes the frozen denominator as an input and states no wave-specific, lane-specific or handoff-specific denominator of its own, so each caller supplies its own denominator table.
- [x] AC-4: The module carries the oscillation detector as new content: a verification loop that oscillates terminates immediately with `reason_class = "oscillation"` rather than consuming its cap.
- [x] AC-5: `kiwi-wave-master` references the module from its section 0 table and its own sections 5.5 and 5.6 are replaced by that reference plus its own denominator table; the moved sentences no longer appear in `kiwi-wave-master/SKILL.md`.
- [x] AC-6: The verification evidence for this requirement records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed paths that still hold the old text — `~/.claude/skills/_shared/kiwi/`, which holds no `verify-loop.md`, and `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-wave-master/SKILL.md`, which still inlines the engine — because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids a reinstall against this repository.

### FR-FLOW-107 — wave-decomposition.md carries wave splitting and the coverage gate with the artifact root as a parameter

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

A shared module `_shared/kiwi/wave-decomposition.md` v1.0.0 SHALL carry wave splitting, design-baseline materialisation and the coverage gate — created by moving text rather than by writing text — with the artifact root taken as an input parameter rather than hard-coded, and `kiwi-wave-master` SHALL reference it from its section 0 table rather than inlining it.

#### Rationale

Making the artifact root a parameter is what makes one baseline implementation serve two callers, and it is why there is one freeze implementation rather than two: `kiwi-wave-master` passes its own analysis directory and the orchestrator passes its work root. The contents move together because they are one contract — losing the splitter's existing-modules and dependency-direction inputs cuts one module across several waves, and losing the coverage gate's full design-item comparison unit makes an unmapped count of zero free and lets a sub-section design pass a top-level check. From `05` §9.1.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `_shared/kiwi/wave-decomposition.md` exists at v1.0.0 in all three variants and in the `.agents` mirror.
- [x] AC-2: The module carries the two-branch split heuristic with its 3 to 8 sub-goal bound, `existing_modules` plus dependency direction as splitter inputs, design-baseline materialisation with the one-normative-sentence granularity rule, `integration_items`, the closed `exclusion_class` vocabulary, the always-write-empty-constraints rule, append-new-over-edit-in-place for late inputs, carry-forward-wave denominator conversion, journal-only resolvability, and the coverage gate.
- [x] AC-3: The artifact root is a declared input parameter with both callers named — `kiwi-wave-master` passes `docs/analysis/kiwi-wave-master-{run_id}/` and `kiwi-orchestrator` passes `docs/research/{work}/` — and no hard-coded root remains in the module.
- [x] AC-4: The coverage gate's comparison unit is the full `design_items` set rather than a sub-section sample, and user consent is required on any non-empty `out_of_scope` set.
- [x] AC-5: `kiwi-wave-master` references the module from its section 0 table and its own sections 3, 3.1 and 3.2 are replaced by that reference; the moved sentences no longer appear in `kiwi-wave-master/SKILL.md`.
- [x] AC-6: The verification evidence for this requirement records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed paths that still hold the old text — `~/.claude/skills/_shared/kiwi/`, which holds no `wave-decomposition.md`, and `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-wave-master/SKILL.md`, which still inlines the splitter — because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids a reinstall against this repository.

### FR-FLOW-108 — wave-srs-registration.md carries the per-wave kiwi-srs registration contract for both callers

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

A shared module `_shared/kiwi/wave-srs-registration.md` v1.0.0 SHALL carry the per-wave `/kiwi-srs` registration contract, referenced by both `kiwi-wave-master` and `kiwi-orchestrator`, so that the rules are duplicated by reference rather than by text.

#### Rationale

Per-wave target registration otherwise stays in `kiwi-wave-master`, but the orchestrator's own registration step needs the identical rules, and one skill referencing another skill's body is not a sanctioned pattern. The module is small and exists only to avoid a copy, which is the correct reason for a module to exist; the `srs_authored` idempotency marker in particular cannot be restated approximately, because its companion clause is what stops a resumed run from re-authoring a wave whose registration had already completed. From `05` §9.2.

- [x] AC-3: Both `kiwi-wave-master` and `kiwi-orchestrator` reference the module from their section 0 tables and neither restates the registration contract in its own body.
- [x] AC-4: `kiwi-wave-master`'s own section 4 target registration otherwise stays in that skill; only the registration-contract lines move.
- [x] AC-5: The verification evidence for this requirement records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed paths that still hold the old text — `~/.claude/skills/_shared/kiwi/`, which holds no `wave-srs-registration.md`, and `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-wave-master/SKILL.md`, which still inlines the contract — because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids a reinstall against this repository.

### FR-FLOW-109 — run-ledger.md carries the resume ledger plus the three clauses reassigned from the deferred module

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

A shared module `_shared/kiwi/run-ledger.md` v1.0.0 SHALL carry the resume-card schema and its size cap, the closed verb enum, the three recovery classes, the write-ahead-intent and write-behind-result discipline, the proof-kind table, the reconciliation predicate, the four drift digests and the run-contract preamble convention, and SHALL additionally carry the three clauses reassigned from `isolation-and-integration.md`: the convergence-registry `recipe.kind` enum with its lane-eligibility rule, the normative shipped-default convergence registry, and the never-merge-to-the-base-branch rule together with the mandatory post-merge `validate` then `sync-index` sequence.

#### Rationale

The ledger is what makes an interrupted action distinguishable from a not-started one, which the existing three-stage wave resume cannot express; without it a resumed session either re-runs an externally visible action or skips one that never completed. The three reassigned clauses would otherwise ship with a module deferred to the next target even though all three are used in this one — the shipped-default registry is read when orchestrator-only paths are subtracted, the lane-eligibility rule decides execution order, and the never-merge rule plus the post-merge index sequence are how a run ends — so they are relocated rather than deferred with their original home. From `05` §4.1, §4.3, §4.4, §4.5, §4.6, §4.7 and §9.1.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `_shared/kiwi/run-ledger.md` exists at v1.0.0 in all three variants and in the `.agents` mirror.
- [x] AC-2: The module carries the resume-card schema and its cap, the closed verb enum, the three recovery classes, the write-ahead and write-behind discipline, the proof-kind table, the reconciliation predicate, the four drift digests and the run-contract preamble convention.
- [x] AC-3: The verb enum is closed, each verb declares exactly one of the three recovery classes, and the module states that an out-of-enum verb is a hard stop on resume.
- [x] AC-4: The write discipline is stated as one intent line before the verb and one result line after it, with the invariant that for the current run the last line for each `(verb, wave, lane)` key must be a `result` and an intent with no matching result means the verb was interrupted.
- [x] AC-5: The three reassigned clauses are present in `run-ledger.md` — the convergence-registry `recipe.kind` enum with its lane-eligibility rule, the normative shipped-default registry including `CP-01`, `CP-02` and `CP-07`, and the never-merge-to-the-base-branch rule with the mandatory post-merge `validate` then `sync-index` sequence — and the merge-time recipe meaning table is not among them.
- [x] AC-6: The verification evidence for this requirement records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed path that still holds the old text — `~/.claude/skills/_shared/kiwi/`, which holds no `run-ledger.md` — because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids a reinstall against this repository.

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

AC-2 is asserted per variant across four cases: the resume-card schema with its writer-enforced 8 KB hard cap, the rollup rule, six named card fields and the five P- precondition literals; the seven proof kinds with the rule that journal can never be a verdict line's only proof, both reconciliation outcomes, and a section-6 digest list asserted to hold exactly four enumerated digests naming both drift classes; the section-7 run-contract preamble asserted to name 00.run-contract.md, be a closed list, and carry intake_autonomy, the immutable wave order and the exact resume command; and the forbidden-action list asserted closed with its git-add-all ban and never-re-dispatch rule. ### FR-FLOW-110 — The shared-module extraction preserves behaviour with a red mutation per normative sentence

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The extraction of the shared modules SHALL preserve behaviour: every pre-existing `kiwi-wave-master` text assertion SHALL resolve after the move, and each normative sentence — counted per variant over the bilingual modal set, because the four extracted skill bodies carry zero `MUST` tokens in all three variants — SHALL still have a mutation that is authored, applied and observed red.

#### Rationale

The section-scoped text assertions over `kiwi-wave-master` are the only regression net the wave machinery has, and moving text out of the skill breaks them; a harness change that loosens an assertion is itself a regression and a mutation is the only way to detect it. Keying the obligation on the English `MUST` token alone would make it a no-op against the files that actually exist, since all four affected skills carry zero `MUST` tokens in every variant — which is why the modal set is bilingual and counted per variant. The four-step order exists so that the move produces a genuine, unfaked red whose failure list enumerates exactly which rules the move touched, which is the proof that the move lost nothing. From `05` §9.3 and §10.3.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The test harness reads through a resolved-skill reader that appends the bodies of every `_shared/kiwi/` module a skill's section 0 table references, in the order that table lists them; against unextracted sources the change is a no-op and all 247 `it()` blocks across the five wave test files stay green, which is how the harness change itself is verified.

### FR-FLOW-111 — pipeline-event.md registers kiwi-orchestrator in the closed skill enum and adds two T1 rows

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

`_shared/kiwi/pipeline-event.md` SHALL add `kiwi-orchestrator` to its closed `skill` enum and SHALL add T1 routing-hint rows for both `kiwi-orchestrator` and `kiwi-wave-master`, the latter being a member of the enum today with no T1 row.

#### Rationale

An out-of-enum `skill` value is warned about and skipped, so without the enum entry every event the orchestrator emits is silently discarded and the run leaves no journal record at the pipeline layer; and `kiwi-wave-master` has sat in the enum with no routing hint since it shipped, so even the existing skill has no declared next step. Both are fixed in one edit because they are the same table. The orchestrator's own hint is `null` because the run terminates on its own integration branch: commit and push to the base branch and pull-request creation are deliberately not auto-chained. From `05` §14 registrations 1, 2 and 7, and §9.2.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `kiwi-orchestrator` is a member of the closed `skill` enum in all four shipped copies of `pipeline-event.md`, so its events are no longer warned about and skipped as out-of-enum.
- [x] AC-2: The T1 table gains a `kiwi-orchestrator` row whose terminal status maps to a `null` next hint, with the recorded reason that the run terminates on its own integration branch and that commit, push and pull-request creation are deliberately not auto-chained.
- [x] AC-3: The T1 table gains the missing `kiwi-wave-master` row with a `null` next hint.
- [x] AC-4: The orchestrator emits through the MCP pipeline-emit tool rather than the hand-rolled shell append block the document prescribes to every other skill, with the documented shell fallback retained because emit is best-effort.
- [x] AC-5: The run-level event uses the bare run id as its emit key, with a resume suffix form for a resumed run, and the document states that no other emit key collides with it.
- [x] AC-6: The verification evidence for this requirement records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed path that still holds the old text — `~/.claude/skills/_shared/kiwi/pipeline-event.md`, whose skill enum carries neither entry — because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids a reinstall against this repository.

### FR-FLOW-112 — loop-option.md records the orchestrator's loop-flag propagation targets

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [routing design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/09.routing-design.md) |

#### Requirement

`_shared/kiwi/loop-option.md` section 6 SHALL gain a `kiwi-orchestrator` row recording its propagation targets — the routed child on a delegated rung, `kiwi-tdd` or `kiwi-pm`, or on the orchestrated rung the per-wave set `kiwi-srs`, `kiwi-planner`, `kiwi-pm` and `kiwi-review-fix-loop` — and recording that `--mini` and `--loops N` propagate to the routed child, per wave, and additionally bound the orchestrator's own D, W, H, P and F loop caps.

#### Rationale

The propagation table is what a parent consults to decide which flags cross a spawn boundary, and an orchestrator absent from it silently propagates nothing, so a user who asked for a reduced round cap gets the default in every child. The routed children belong in the row because on two of three rungs the whole run is a delegation, and the orchestrator's own cap list names five loops rather than six because the per-lane loop is deferred to the next target and listing a cap for a loop that does not run would pin a contract with nothing behind it. From `05` §14 registration 3.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Section 6's table carries a `kiwi-orchestrator` row in all four shipped copies of `loop-option.md`.
- [x] AC-2: The row names the routed children as propagation targets on the delegated rungs — `kiwi-tdd` on the step rung and `kiwi-pm` on the plan rung — in addition to the per-wave set on the orchestrated rung.
- [x] AC-3: The row names the per-wave set as `kiwi-srs`, `kiwi-planner`, `kiwi-pm` and `kiwi-review-fix-loop`.
- [x] AC-4: The row names `--mini` and `--loops N` as the propagated options and names the orchestrator's own cap list as the D, W, H, P and F loops — five loops — with the per-lane loop absent because it is deferred.
- [x] AC-5: The verification evidence for this requirement records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed path that still holds the old text — `~/.claude/skills/_shared/kiwi/loop-option.md`, whose section 6 table carries no orchestrator row — because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids a reinstall against this repository.
