### FR-FLOW-113 — kiwi-pm handoff, session-suffix and no-final flags with handoff as the execution set and the lease

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

`kiwi-pm` SHALL accept `--session-suffix <lane>`, relocating its whole session directory to `.kiwi/sessions/{plan_run_id}/lanes/{lane}/`; `--no-final`, skipping T-final requirement promotion; and `--handoff <path>`, which supplies the execution set and the lease and nothing else — `kiwi-pm` executing exactly the front matter's `task_ids[]`, in sidecar declaration order, taking each Task's body from the sidecar path, using `write_set` as the commit pathspec, treating `req_ids[]` and `acceptance[]` as read-only context passed into the coder spawn prompt, treating a `depends_on_task` predecessor outside `task_ids[]` as satisfied, and refusing `--from-task` together with `--handoff`.

#### Rationale

Defining `--handoff` as the execution set and the lease is what makes it buildable: the handoff is not a plan and carries no Task bodies, no TDD block, no acceptance tests, no definition of done and no rollback, so a definition that called it the input source of truth left an implementer nothing to build from and left `kiwi-pm` executing every task of the plan in declaration order with only a start-point selector available — which would put two units over one plan each writing every file in it and each breaching its lease. `task_ids[]` is generally non-contiguous because it is a connected component of the conflict graph, which is precisely why a start-point selector cannot express it. `--session-suffix` restores per-unit ownership of `pm-state.json`, `pm.lock`, `worklog.jsonl`, `state.json` and the reports directory, discharging rather than weakening the sequential-spawn precondition that pm's own race-freedom rests on. From `05` §5.4.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `--handoff <path>` supplies the execution set and the lease and never the Task bodies: `kiwi-pm` executes exactly the front matter's `task_ids[]` and no other task of the plan, in sidecar declaration order, taking each Task's body from the sidecar path as it does today.
- [x] AC-2: `write_set` is the commit pathspec used by the commit flag, and `req_ids[]` and `acceptance[]` are read-only context passed into the coder spawn prompt that drive no execution decision.
- [x] AC-3: A `depends_on_task` predecessor outside `task_ids[]` is treated as satisfied and the depends-on-violation gate does not fire on it, while a dependency violation inside the execution set is still a gate.
- [x] AC-4: `--from-task` is refused together with `--handoff`, and the skill body records the reason: a start-point selector cannot express a generally non-contiguous `task_ids[]`.
- [x] AC-5: `--session-suffix <lane>` moves the whole session directory — `pm-state.json`, `pm.lock`, `worklog.jsonl`, `state.json` and the reports directory — to `.kiwi/sessions/{plan_run_id}/lanes/{lane}/`, and the run-id line in the coder spawn prompt from which `kiwi-coder` derives its own state paths follows the relocation.
- [x] AC-6: `--no-final` skips T-final requirement promotion, with the recorded reason that a requirement spans units and its all-done denominator would otherwise be one unit's task subset.
- [x] AC-7: The skill body does not define `--handoff` as `kiwi-pm`'s input source of truth: the execution-set-and-lease semantics above are what is written, so two units over one plan cannot each execute every task.
- [x] AC-8: The verification evidence for this requirement records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed path that still holds the old text — `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-pm/SKILL.md`, which carries none of the three flags — because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids a reinstall against this repository.

### FR-FLOW-114 — kiwi-pm and kiwi-review-fix-loop accept no-pipeline-emit so a delegated unit writes no false pipeline record

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [scope and frame decisions](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/08.scope-and-frame-decisions.md) |

#### Requirement

`kiwi-pm` and `kiwi-review-fix-loop` — and not `kiwi-pipeline`, which no orchestrated unit invokes — SHALL accept `--no-pipeline-emit`, taking no argument and suppressing the append to `kiwi/pipeline.jsonl`, so that a unit executed under the orchestrator writes no pipeline record of its own.

#### Rationale

A child skill that appends its own pipeline event while running as one unit of a larger run writes a record that misdescribes the run: the pipeline journal would show a `kiwi-pm` run completing where in fact one unit of one stage of one wave completed, and a parent may not record on a child's behalf to correct it. Suppressing the child's append is therefore the only clean mechanism. The flag is registered on exactly the two skills a unit runs and deliberately not on `kiwi-pipeline`, which no unit invokes — an earlier revision named `kiwi-pipeline` here while the sections it cites named `kiwi-pm`, and a registration list whose purpose is to catch silent breakage is the worst place for that disagreement to sit. From `05` §14 registration 11 and §5.14.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `--no-pipeline-emit` is documented in both `kiwi-pm` and `kiwi-review-fix-loop` in all three variants and takes no argument.
- [x] AC-2: With the flag present neither skill appends to `kiwi/pipeline.jsonl`; without it, each skill's existing emit behaviour is unchanged.
- [x] AC-3: `kiwi-pipeline` does not gain the flag, and the recorded reason is that no orchestrated unit invokes it.
- [x] AC-4: The orchestrator's executor invocation passes `--no-pipeline-emit` on every unit run, and the body states the consequence of omitting it: the unit writes a false pipeline record.
- [x] AC-5: The verification evidence for this requirement records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed paths that still hold the old text — `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-pm/SKILL.md` and `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-review-fix-loop/SKILL.md`, neither of which documents the flag — because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids a reinstall against this repository.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

AC-1 runs it.each over the four kiwi-pm renderings and the four kiwi-review-fix-loop renderings and asserts each documents --no-pipeline-emit, that no occurrence is followed by an argument placeholder, and that a line states the no-argument property explicitly rather than leaving it implied. AC-2 asserts for each of those eight copies that a line states what the flag suppresses, that kiwi/pipeline.jsonl is named as the journal it would otherwise write, and that a separate line states the emit is unchanged without the flag. AC-3 runs it.each over the four kiwi-pipeline renderings and asserts none of them contains the string --no-pipeline-emit, and a companion case asserts each of the eight unit copies records the exclusion with the reason that no orchestrated unit invokes kiwi-pipeline. AC-4 asserts each of the eight states the false-pipeline-record consequence and carries an every-unit line naming the flag. |
| VE-2 | test | test/skills/orchestrator-followup-consistency.fr-flow-116.test.ts | AC-2 | 20/20 pass (npx vitest run --no-file-parallelism, 2026-08-01). A further case asserts all three renderings carry the pointer, so a partial edit of one translation fails. |

### FR-FLOW-115 — kiwi-pm accepts commit-lane-work so an orchestrated unit's work is committed with only its write set staged

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [scope and frame decisions](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/08.scope-and-frame-decisions.md) |

#### Requirement

`kiwi-pm` SHALL accept `--commit-lane-work`, taking no argument, committing each completed Task with only the `write_set` of the handoff already passed via `--handoff` staged and with the run's commit-policy trailers attached.

#### Rationale

Without the flag `kiwi-pm` commits nothing, so the rule that the work is written where it will live would leave a unit's output as an uncommitted working tree that the next unit's run then trips over; and because the commit range on the integration branch is what the orchestrator derives its record from in this target, an uncommitted unit is an unrecorded one. The flag takes no argument deliberately: an earlier revision typed it as taking a pathspec file and pointed that file at a path under the orchestrator's own state directory, which is git-ignored and therefore absent from an isolated workspace, so the pathspec has to come from the handoff the same invocation already names. From `05` §14 registration 11, §5.14 difference 4 and §10.5 step 9.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `--commit-lane-work` is documented on `kiwi-pm` in all three variants, takes no argument, and stages exactly the `write_set` of the handoff already passed via `--handoff`.
- [x] AC-2: One commit is produced per completed Task, staging an explicit pathspec drawn from `write_set` and never staging the whole working tree.
- [x] AC-3: Each commit carries the run coordinates in git trailers and no run coordinate in the commit subject.
- [x] AC-4: The skill body records that a pathspec file is deliberately not used, because any path under the orchestrator's own state directory is git-ignored and therefore absent from an isolated workspace.
- [x] AC-5: The body states the consequence of omitting the flag: `kiwi-pm` commits nothing and the unit's output is left as an uncommitted working tree the next unit trips over.
- [x] AC-6: `--commit-lane-work` is documented on `kiwi-pm` and is not documented on `kiwi-review-fix-loop` in this target.
- [x] AC-7: The verification evidence for this requirement records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed path that still holds the old text — `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-pm/SKILL.md`, which does not document the flag — because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids a reinstall against this repository.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/skills/orchestrator-delegated-flags.fr-flow-113.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | 102/102 pass for the file (npx vitest run --no-file-parallelism, 2026-08-01). AC-1 runs it.each over the four kiwi-pm renderings and asserts each documents --commit-lane-work, that no occurrence is followed by an argument placeholder, and that a line inside the orchestration-handoff-flags section ties the flag to write_set with --handoff named as its source. AC-2 asserts a line states the per-Task commit granularity and is about the commit, and that staging the whole working tree is named and forbidden rather than left unmentioned. AC-3 asserts the section carries trailer, that a line states the commit subject carries no run coordinate, and that the prohibition carries no hedge. AC-4 asserts a line records that a pathspec file is deliberately not used and that the git-ignored kiwi/orchestrator/ directory is named as the reason. AC-5 asserts a line states that omitting the flag leaves kiwi-pm committing nothing and that the leftover uncommitted tree the next unit trips over is named. AC-6 runs it.each over the four kiwi-review-fix-loop renderings and asserts none contains --commit-lane-work; a corollary case asserts each kiwi-pm rendering's no-automatic-commit rule now names the flag as its one exception. |
| VE-2 | test | test/skills/orchestrator-followup-consistency.fr-flow-116.test.ts | AC-5 | 20/20 pass (npx vitest run --no-file-parallelism, 2026-08-01). A further case asserts all three renderings carry the pointer. |

### FR-FLOW-116 — kiwi-tdd declares a critical gates table and joins the closed pipeline skill enum

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [routing design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/09.routing-design.md), [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

`kiwi-tdd` SHALL declare a `critical_gates[]` table in the three-column `gate_id | reason | location` form, and `_shared/kiwi/pipeline-event.md` SHALL add `kiwi-tdd` to its closed `skill` enum with a T1 routing-hint row.

#### Rationale

`kiwi-tdd` declares no `critical_gates[]` today, and by the shared safe default that makes `--auto` inactive for it — a silent ignore rather than a failure, so the skill runs but still stops at its own user gates, which under an unattended run is a stall no automatic mechanism can clear. It is also absent from the closed pipeline `skill` enum, so its events are warned about and skipped, which means a delegated step run leaves the orchestrator's own event as the only journal record and a parent may not record on the child's behalf to fill the hole. This is authored as a separate additive requirement rather than folded into the routing requirements, because folding it in would smuggle a second behavioural change into them. From `09` §4.4, §5.1, §13 A4 and A7, and `05` §14 registration 3-prime.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `kiwi-tdd/SKILL.md` carries a `critical_gates[]` table in the three-column `gate_id | reason | location` form in all three variants; the string does not occur in the skill today.
- [x] AC-2: Each of the four user-interaction points that survive a correctly dispatched step run — the Phase 1 claim write-skew rejection, the Architecture-Decisions approval, `EVIDENCE_REQUIRED` on promote, and the Phase 7 completion gate — appears in the skill body with a stated severity, so none of them falls to the undeclared-gate default.
- [x] AC-3: With the table declared, `--auto` is no longer inactive for `kiwi-tdd` by the shared safe default, and the skill body states which of its gates `--auto` can and cannot resolve.
- [x] AC-4: `kiwi-tdd` is a member of `pipeline-event.md`'s closed `skill` enum in all four shipped copies, so its events are no longer warned about and skipped as out-of-enum.
- [x] AC-5: `pipeline-event.md`'s T1 table gains a `kiwi-tdd` row.
- [x] AC-6: This requirement is authored separately from the routing requirements and none of them depends on it: until it lands, a delegated step run leaves the orchestrator's own event as the only journal record, which the routing design states rather than working around by having the parent record on the child's behalf.
- [x] AC-7: The verification evidence for this requirement records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed paths that still hold the old text — `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-tdd/SKILL.md`, which declares no critical gates table, and `~/.claude/skills/_shared/kiwi/pipeline-event.md`, whose skill enum lacks the entry — because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids a reinstall against this repository.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/skills/orchestrator-tdd-routing.fr-flow-116.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | 54/54 pass (npx vitest run --no-file-parallelism, 2026-08-01). AC-1 runs it.each over the four shipped kiwi-tdd renderings, asserts a critical_gates section exists, that its header splits into exactly three content columns, and that they are gate_id, a reason and a location. AC-2 asserts each of the three critical gate ids is its own table row with a non-empty reason and a non-empty location, that the parsed gate-id set equals the canonical three and is identical across the four copies, that no gate is declared twice, that each row names the phase it fires in, and that the fourth surviving interaction point sds-architecture-decision-approval carries the explicit severity business-decision while being absent from the critical table. AC-3 asserts the gate section mentions --auto, carries a line naming what --auto cannot resolve and a line naming what it can, and names the superseded safe default so the change is legible. AC-4 parses the section-3 fenced skill enum of each of the four pipeline-event.md copies and asserts kiwi-tdd is a member. AC-5 asserts each copy's section-4 T1 table carries a kiwi-tdd row keyed on TASK_DONE with a non-empty hint, pinned to kiwi-review-fix-loop so the chosen value cannot drift silently between renderings. AC-6 asserts no kiwi-tdd rendering instructs a parent to record the child's pipeline event on its behalf. |

### FR-FLOW-117 — kiwi-pipeline section 2.8 accepts a frozen route lock as its step-scoped conjunct

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [routing design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/09.routing-design.md), [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

`kiwi-pipeline` section 2.8 SHALL state that when a `kiwi-orchestrator` run has frozen a route, section 2.8's step-scoped conjunct is satisfied by `route.lock.json` and is not re-judged.

#### Rationale

Section 2.8 routes on a conjunction of work-mode and step-scope, but only the first conjunct is operational: step-scope is given no test, no threshold and no named input anywhere in that section or in the shared work-mode policy. The routing classifier's disqualifier set is that missing definition, so the amendment records where the conjunct is now answered rather than leaving two places to answer it. It is needed even though section 2.8 never runs inside an orchestrator run today — cycle and from-stage entries are excluded from its routing and the orchestrator calls the stages individually — precisely so that a later change to that exclusion does not silently create two live routers disagreeing mid-run. From `09` §4.1 and §11, and `05` §14 registration 8.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Section 2.8 carries the sentence in all three variants and names `route.lock.json` as what satisfies the step-scoped conjunct, stating that the conjunct is not re-judged.
- [x] AC-2: The amendment changes nothing about section 2.8 outside an orchestrator run: the work-mode conjunct and the fallback chain for work that is not step-scoped are unchanged.
- [x] AC-3: The body records why the amendment exists although section 2.8 never runs inside an orchestrator run today — cycle and from-stage entries are excluded from its routing and the orchestrator calls the stages individually — so that a later change to that exclusion does not silently create two live routers.
- [x] AC-4: The body records that the step-scoped conjunct section 2.8 declares and never defines is supplied by the classifier's disqualifier set, and that the definition only ever narrows the step rung and never widens it.
- [x] AC-5: The verification evidence for this requirement records an explicit runtime-lag note naming the installed path that still holds the old text — `~/.claude/skills/kiwi-pipeline/SKILL.md`, whose section 2.8 carries no such sentence — because `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids a reinstall against this repository.

AC-1 asserts the section exists, that a line within it names route.lock.json, that the clause is scoped to a kiwi-orchestrator run, that the lock satisfies rather than merely informs the conjunct, and that the section states the conjunct is not re-judged. ### FR-FLOW-118 — The run contract's closed list of forbidden actions has a fixed membership

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The closed list of forbidden actions the kiwi-orchestrator skill body states SHALL have a fixed membership of exactly eight prohibitions — no re-decomposition, no Requirement ID allocation outside Phase 3.b, no editing a completed unit, no weakening or deleting tests, no writing outside the lease, no hand-appending to the run journal, no git add -A or git commit -a, and no merging the integration branch into the base branch or opening a pull request — stated identically in all three shipped variants, and the deletion of any one of them SHALL be a test failure.

#### Rationale

FR-FLOW-093 names the list and pins two of its eight entries: AC-3 the merge and pull-request pair, AC-5 the bulk-staging pair. Nothing fixed the other four, and a grep over test/ for the list or its heading returned nothing, so five of the eight could have been deleted from a shipped skill with every gate staying green. A list that calls itself closed while its membership is unasserted is closed in name only. The mutation ledger landed on 2026-08-02 already pins all eight with one rule each and a red mutation apiece; this requirement records the obligation the ledger discharges, so the membership does not depend on a test nobody's requirement asks for. Measured: the list is one line in each variant, byte-identical across the three.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All eight prohibitions appear in the closed list in each of the three shipped variants, and the list is the same in all three.
- [x] AC-2: Deleting any one of the eight from a variant fails a named test, so the closed list cannot shrink silently.
- [x] AC-3: The assertion enumerates the eight from a declared set rather than from a count literal, so adding a ninth prohibition without declaring it also fails.
- [x] AC-4: The requirement's Verification Evidence records the runtime-lag note naming the installed copy that still holds the pre-change text, because the charter forbids running speckiwi init, speckiwi skills install or speckiwi upgrade --apply against this repository.

AC-1 asserts the list line exists in all three variants and that the three are one identical string, then asserts each of the eight prohibitions per variant, 24 cases. AC-3 parses the line on its own semicolon separator, asserts the parse is non-empty so the membership cases are not vacuous, asserts no parsed entry is undeclared, and asserts the parsed count equals the declared set size so a ninth prohibition arriving without a declaration fails. AC-2 does not assert that String.replace removes a substring — it runs the same predicate the per-entry cases use against a line with one entry deleted and requires it to throw naming that entry, and it refuses a probe whose replacement changed nothing. Real-file probe: deleting the test-weakening entry from skills/claude/kiwi-orchestrator/SKILL.md turns 4 cases red including AC-1's identical-across-variants case; bytes restored and re-run 27/27 green. The mutation ledger pins the same eight independently, one rule each. |

Measured: ~/.claude/skills/kiwi-orchestrator/ does not exist, so no installed copy of this skill holds either the old text or the new one, and no installed rendering reads this list today. The charter forbids running speckiwi init, speckiwi skills install or speckiwi upgrade --apply with this repository as the target root, so the lag is not closed by installing. Naming a stale installed path that is not there would be a fabrication; the honest note is that no installed copy exists. ### FR-FLOW-119 — --drive autonomous wave mode: bounded self-repair behind a closed refusal list

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.4 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-master, auto, drive, unattended, gates |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

When the user passes `--drive`, kiwi-wave-master SHALL carry the whole wave sequence to the last wave without user interaction: it SHALL imply `--auto`, `--auto-integration` and `--auto-cost-warning`; it SHALL authorise self-repair of a blocking defect only when the repair is reversible, confined to the run root, leaves existing public contracts and existing tests unchanged, and rests on a diagnosis reproduced by an independent check; it SHALL NOT open the gates named in its refusal list, whatever confidence a committee reports; and it SHALL name in the wave journal every gate it resolves automatically and every gate it halts on.

#### Rationale

Measured on a real run (DocuOrbit, 2026-08-02, kiwi/waves.jsonl). The installed kiwi-wave-master declared no critical_gates[], which auto-option.md defines as `--auto` inactive with silent ignore, so `--auto` had been a no-op and every wave boundary asked the user to continue. The Wave 5 halt is the second half of the evidence: the agent reproduced the cause independently (a JDK 8 tools.jar absent from the test compile classpath), proposed a reversible one-line build fix, judged it the recommended path, and halted anyway; that journal line records the stop in prose and names no gate, so the halt is traceable to no declared entry. FR-FLOW-059 already names that class of undeclared stop. The refusal list is the load-bearing half of the flag rather than a caveat on it: opening existing-test-weakened-or-deleted, existing-public-contract-change or out-of-scope-user-consent would let an unattended run reach green by weakening a test or by dropping a design item out of every denominator, so the run would finish while the goal shrank. That failure looks like success and happens where nobody is watching.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All three shipped kiwi-wave-master variants give `--drive` its own section and state, in a sentence a test pins verbatim, that it turns on `--auto`, `--auto-integration` and `--auto-cost-warning` together.
- [x] AC-2: All three variants state the four self-repair conditions — reversible, confined to the run root, existing public contracts and existing tests unchanged, and a diagnosis reproduced by an independent check — as a conjunction whose failure halts, each condition in its own numbered item, with no hedge downgrading one to best-effort.
- [x] AC-3: All three variants declare a closed refusal list naming at least external-module-impact, existing-test-weakened-or-deleted, existing-public-contract-change, existing-file-deleted-or-moved, mock-detection, tdd-bypass-attempt, out-of-scope-user-consent, unsafe-option-refused and wave-append-cap-exhausted, state in a pinned sentence that `--drive` does not open them whatever confidence a committee reports, and carry no exception clause admitting one.
- [x] AC-4: All three variants state in a pinned sentence that wave-verify-residual-critical, final-verify-residual-critical and wave-verify-fail-residual stay closed under `--drive`, because reaching them means the automatic remediation loop already failed.
- [x] AC-5: `--drive` reaches its destination: the kiwi-wave-master pass-through table carries the row to kiwi-coder, kiwi-pipeline and kiwi-pm each state that they forward it, and kiwi-coder itself states that `--drive` opens its integration-test and cost-warning gates — without that last hop the route ends at a skill that has no rule for the token and the run halts there.
- [x] AC-6: All three variants state that a `--drive` halt names its gate id in the journal `abort_gate` field when that gate belongs to the orchestrator gate vocabulary, that a gate owned by a child skill arrives instead as child-pipeline-needs-user-or-failed, and that an automatically resolved gate records a `decision` row naming it.
- [x] AC-7: All four shipped copies of the shared `--auto` SSOT `_shared/kiwi/auto-option.md` record that one explicitly named flag may imply the child gate options, so the skill text does not contradict that module's rule that a parent never synthesises a child's gate-bypassing options.
- [x] AC-8: The critical-gate rows for integration-test-user-consent and cost-warning-large-task name `--drive` as an opener in all three variants, so the machine-read gate interface agrees with the prose rather than still halting the mode it declares.
- [x] AC-9: Every enumeration touching the forwarded option set names `--drive`: the kiwi-wave-master frontmatter, the kiwi-pm CLI argument summary and its pass-through paragraph, and the `decision` field occasions in the four waves-event copies. The kiwi-orchestrator inherited-option list names it as deliberately NOT inherited, because that skill runs its own gate table and its own `--auto` contract, so inheriting an implication set would claim it opens gates that table never declares.
- [ ] AC-10: An automated test pins each normative sentence above so that inverting it, hedging it, or adding an exception clause fails the suite — not only deleting its vocabulary — and scopes every section-bound assertion to a heading it cannot silently re-anchor away from.

#### Verification Evidence

Shaped by three independent mutation passes: round 1 left 10 of 19 survivors (vocabulary-presence assertions let every inversion through), round 2 left 5 (perimeter files still token-searched, so the kiwi-coder endpoint could deny opening its gates with the suite green), round 3 closed those and attacked the pins themselves. Fences are masked for kiwi-wave-master only, a scope measured rather than assumed. |

### FR-FLOW-120 — The prickly review checks comment claims a machine can check

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.7.0-verification-integrity |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The prickly-review axis table in each bundled `kiwi-coder` variant SHALL declare an axis whose subject is the truth of a claim a code comment makes about the code, SHALL bound that axis to a closed set of claim kinds a machine can check without reading intent, SHALL state that a comment claim outside that set is not a finding, and SHALL leave the existing `@req` tag prohibition in force.

#### Rationale

The seven axes are intent preservation, security, edge cases, concurrency, refactoring scope, error handling and test quality. None asks whether a comment says something true, and one comment class — the `@req` tag — is explicitly exempted from being checked at all. So a comment asserting a fact about the code is never re-read against the code, which is the failure mode this target exists to close: measured in this repository's own SRS, prose asserted a fact, reality moved, and nothing re-read the assertion until 44 verified rows carried it. Code comments have the identical shape. The axis must be bounded because checking an arbitrary comment against code costs the same reasoning as judging an acceptance criterion — measured at roughly 27 seconds per requirement — which does not scale to every comment in a diff. Claims naming a path, a symbol, a count or an absence are decidable by existsSync and grep, so bounding the axis to those makes it nearly free while covering the claims that actually went false here.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Each of the three bundled `kiwi-coder` variants declares an axis in its prickly-review axis table whose stated subject is whether a comment's claim about the code holds, distinct from the refactoring axis whose subject is duplication, over-abstraction, naming and function size.
- [x] AC-2: That axis names a closed set of checkable claim kinds — a file or directory path, a code symbol name, a count, and an absence claim such as `no caller` or `not wired` — the set is stated as closed rather than as examples, and it is identical in all three variants.
- [x] AC-3: That axis states that a comment claim outside the closed set is not a finding, so the axis cannot expand into judging intent.
- [ ] AC-4: The existing prohibition on verifying `@req` tags is present and unweakened in all three variants after the change, and the closed set's symbol kind is qualified so that a REQ-ID cannot be read into it.
- [x] AC-5: The axis table's row count and the set of axis subjects are equal across the three variants, so no variant carries an axis another lacks.
- [ ] AC-6: Every count claim the skill makes about its own axis table agrees with the table: no rendering, and no other bundled skill that borrows the table by name, states a number that the table contradicts.
- [ ] AC-7: A comment claim in the closed set that is measurably false is routed to a severity the Phase 2 gate blocks on, so an axis finding can stop the pipeline rather than only being advised; comment style and wording remain at the lower severity.
- [x] AC-8: The `.agents` mirror of the changed skill carries the same axis table as its source variant, asserted by a test rather than by a manual sync step.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/skills/kiwi-coder-comment-claim-axis.fr-flow-120.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7, AC-8 | 91 passed / 0 failed, 2026-08-04. The denylist cases could not be finished — the Implementation Note records why — so four cells are pinned by canonical equality across all four copies instead: the axis row, the bounding sentence, and both severity lines. Mutation-measured: 16 single-copy rewordings all die; a COORDINATED reword of all four copies, and an additive clause that re-opens the bound while leaving every asserted phrase in place, pass every pre-existing assertion and die only on the pin. An independent audit then measured three further mutants surviving — a count claim written inside section 5.2 on a line naming neither scan token, a Phase 2 gate that stops blocking on HIGH, and a sentence exempting axis 8 from the `@req` prohibition — and all three now die. NOT closed and not claimed: `skills/etc` runs a different loop and its Phase 2 gate names no severity at all, so AC-7's stated mechanism is absent there; its gate line is pinned separately rather than rewritten. |

It cannot catch unbounded rewording: Korean expresses "except" indefinitely many ways, so a clause re-opening the closed set survives any finite denylist.

### FR-FLOW-121 — kiwi-coder --defer-srs-mutation and its kiwi-pm pass-through

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | orchestrator, lane, skills, mutation, traceability |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md |

#### Requirement

`kiwi-coder` SHALL accept `--defer-srs-mutation <path>` and, under it, SHALL record each of its four mandatory MCP mutations to the queue at that path instead of calling them, keeping the queue entry shape and `args_hash` dedupe it already declares. `kiwi-pm` SHALL accept the same flag and pass it through into its `kiwi-coder` spawn prompt. The two halves SHALL be documented together in every shipped rendering — the three skill variants and the `.agents` mirror — because either half alone is inert: `kiwi-pm` owns the spawn, so a flag only `kiwi-coder` knows is unreachable, and a flag only `kiwi-pm` forwards is discarded by a `kiwi-coder` that does not read it.

#### Rationale

Charter C1 forbids SRS mutation inside a parallel task, but `kiwi-coder` §0.12 makes four MCP mutations mandatory per Task, and a lane's MCP writes land at the host root rather than in its worktree. Recording them to the existing `mcp_call_log[]` queue with the existing `args_hash` dedupe is the design's answer (05 §15.3 S12, §14 items 11-12, §5.4): the mutations are deferred, not skipped, because skipping them would break traceability while calling them would break C1. The design also states the two halves are inert apart, which is why one requirement owns both. No replay consumer exists yet — the queue producer lands first and the host-root replayer is separate work in this target.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Each of the four shipped `kiwi-coder` renderings — `skills/{claude,codex,etc}` plus the `.agents/skills` mirror — documents `--defer-srs-mutation <path>` in the §1.2 optional-input table and defines it in the §6.2 section its variant makes SSOT.
- [x] AC-2: Each of the four `kiwi-coder` renderings states — in whichever file that variant makes SSOT for §6.2, inline for the claude variant and `references/extended-workflow.md` for the others — that under the flag the four mandatory mutations are recorded to the queue rather than called, keeping the existing entry shape and `args_hash` dedupe; the rule sentence is asserted on its own, not on the sub-block heading that repeats its words, and the resolved SSOT section is asserted non-empty so a relocated section cannot pass by absence.
- [x] AC-3: Each of the four `kiwi-coder` renderings states, in sentences of their own, that the four mutations remain accounted for, that the orchestrator replays them at the host root, and that the flag is reached only through the `kiwi-pm` spawn rather than by direct invocation.
- [x] AC-4: Each of the four shipped `kiwi-pm` renderings documents `--defer-srs-mutation <path>` in all three places its sibling delegation flags occupy — the §1.2 optional-input table, the CLI argument summary, and the §1.5 delegation section — the §1.5 entry defines it as a pass-through into the `kiwi-coder` spawn prompt, and the §1.5 prose flag count agrees with the number its heading lists, so a member cannot be added to one and not the other.
- [x] AC-5: The flag carries a required path placeholder in every rendering of both skills, and no rendering names a default queue path — unlike `--commit-lane-work`, which takes no argument — so two lanes cannot be documented into sharing one queue.
- [x] AC-6: Each of the four `kiwi-coder` renderings states that no consumer APPLIES the queue yet, so the queue accumulates and the four mutations are lost unless an orchestrator run harvests and applies it; the caveat is absolute rather than hedged, and it lives in the shipped text a reader actually reaches, not only in this requirement. It stays precise as the consumer arrives in pieces: once IR-CLI-091 landed the planner `orchestrate replay plan`, the renderings name the planner as present and the applier as still absent, rather than continuing to claim no consumer exists at all.
- [x] AC-7: Each of the four `kiwi-coder` renderings states that `--dry-run` and `--defer-srs-mutation` are mutually exclusive and that the combination is refused with a recorded reason, rather than leaving a queue of `dry_run: true` entries whose replay semantics are undefined.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/skills/coder-defer-srs-mutation.fr-flow-121.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7 | Per-rendering text contracts over the four shipped copies of each skill. AC-2 resolves each kiwi-coder variant's §6.2 SSOT rather than assuming one — `references/extended-workflow.md` when that file exists beside the SKILL.md, inline otherwise — and asserts the resolved section non-empty, so a relocated section cannot pass by absence. Every rule assertion runs against the sub-block with its heading stripped, because the heading repeats the rule's words and an unscoped search would leave the sentence below it unchecked; the sibling suite names that same trap. Two red-green rounds: 24 failures across all eight renderings before the first edits, then 12 more after independent verification added AC-4's count agreement, AC-6 and AC-7 — the §1.5 heading had gained a sixth flag while the prose two lines below still said five, the text asserted a replay whose consumer does not exist, and the `--dry-run` interaction was undefined. Green at 40 files / 2108 tests in `test/skills` with no sibling regression. Mutation-checked in one variant at a time: removing the not-skipping sentence from the etc variant reddens AC-3 there alone, and removing the §1.5 sub-block from the codex kiwi-pm reddens AC-4 there alone, so the per-copy assertions do not collapse into one. The `.agents` rendering is regenerated by `speckiwi skills mirror --write`, never hand-edited, and the run left the two hermeticity-sentinel directories uncreated. Runtime lag: these read the BUNDLED copies, since the charter forbids reinstalling `~/.claude/skills` from this repository. Full suite at the certified state: 5050 passed, 11 failed in two files belonging to a concurrent uncommitted stream — `workflow_record_reclassification` exists at HEAD in neither `mutation.ts`, `read.ts` nor the appendix, and `exclusive-lock.ts` is mid-edit at +189/-11 — so no failure is attributable to this change set. |

### FR-FLOW-122 — Shared worktree-lane contract that both orchestrating skills declare

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | orchestrator, wave-master, worktree, lane, skills |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/16.wave-master-per-wave-worktree-subagent-research.md |

#### Requirement

A shared contract document `_shared/kiwi/worktree-lane.md` SHALL be the single source of truth for how a run creates a git worktree and works inside it, and both `kiwi-orchestrator` and `kiwi-wave-master` SHALL declare it in their §0 rather than each restating the procedure. The contract SHALL separate the run root, which stays bound to the MCP workspace and owns every SRS mutation, from a lane workspace, which is a worktree that holds only code and test edits; SHALL require an explicit checkout of the intended base commit because a freshly created worktree does not open at the current branch; SHALL forbid a lane from calling an SRS mutation, from committing under `docs/spec/`, and from passing `--root`; SHALL state that deferred mutations are replayed at the host root through the admitted set only; SHALL state that a lane's own verification result is not the verdict, which the host obtains by running the verification command against the lane workspace itself; and SHALL state that the bootstrap install passes `--include=dev` so a `NODE_ENV=production` environment cannot silently omit devDependencies.

#### Rationale

Both skills need the same procedure and neither has it. Writing it twice would put one responsibility in two places, which is the failure this repository has already paid for elsewhere: one copy gets corrected and the other silently diverges. The specific facts the contract fixes are each measured rather than assumed — a runtime-created worktree opened at `origin/<default-branch>`, 114 commits behind the branch under work, so an implicit baseline is wrong; `npm ci` under `NODE_ENV=production` omits devDependencies and still exits 0, so a lane can report green from a toolchain that never ran the tests; and the MCP workspace root cannot follow a session into a worktree, so a lane that calls an SRS mutation writes to the host root and splits the run's own accounting.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `_shared/kiwi/worktree-lane.md` exists in every shipped skill bundle and carries a version marker.
- [x] AC-2: It distinguishes the run root from a lane workspace and states that every SRS mutation belongs to the run root.
- [x] AC-3: It requires an explicit checkout of the base commit and says why a created worktree's default head cannot be relied on.
- [x] AC-4: It forbids a lane from calling an SRS mutation, from committing anything under `docs/spec/`, and from passing `--root`.
- [x] AC-5: It states that deferred mutations are replayed at the host root and only for the admitted mutation set.
- [x] AC-6: It states that the host, not the lane, obtains the verification verdict by running the command against the lane workspace.
- [x] AC-7: It requires `--include=dev` on the bootstrap install and names the `NODE_ENV=production` failure it prevents.
- [x] AC-8: `kiwi-orchestrator` and `kiwi-wave-master` each declare the contract in their §0 table in every shipped rendering, and neither restates the procedure in place of referencing it.
- [x] AC-9: Adding this contract introduces neither `branch-serial-lane` nor `patch-lane` into either skill body, so the phase-1 profile assertions of FR-FLOW-088 stay green.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/skills/worktree-lane-contract.fr-flow-122.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7, AC-8, AC-9 | red 27 failed / 33, then green 33 passed. Three mutations were authored and observed red: removing SRS mutation from the run-root row, removing --include=dev, and removing the §0 declaration from kiwi-wave-master. A first version of AC-2 was vacuous — a proximity window around "SRS mutation" was satisfied by an unrelated §3 sentence — and now reads the run-root row itself. Landing this also turned two pre-existing suites red and both were repaired rather than relaxed: the 05 §10.3 mutation ledger (the pointer row carried the modal carrier 금지 with no ledger anchor; the word was removed because the normative force belongs to the contract) and FR-FLOW-110 AC-1 (the expected shared-module list gained worktree-lane). |
| VE-2 | test | test/skills/worktree-lane-contract.fr-flow-122.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7, AC-8, AC-9 | Full-regression evidence, taken with the per-test timeout raised to 30s to control for host load (430 competing node processes drove CLI startup to 1.4s against a normal 300ms). No assertion was changed and the repository's committed timeout is unchanged; only the measurement confound was removed. Result: 386 files, 5209 passed, 4 skipped, 1 failed, zero timeouts. The single failure is record-reclassification-cross-process.fr-node-177, a cross-process lock serialisation case owned by another stream, which passes 3 of 3 runs in isolation. typecheck:test and lint exit 0. |

### FR-FLOW-123 — Both orchestrating skills carry an executable worktree procedure

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | orchestrator, wave-master, worktree, lane, skills |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/16.wave-master-per-wave-worktree-subagent-research.md |

#### Requirement

`kiwi-orchestrator` and `kiwi-wave-master` SHALL each carry a numbered section that an agent can execute to create a worktree and work inside it, naming the actual commands in order: create with the base commit as an argument of `git worktree add`, admit the placement with `speckiwi orchestrate preflight --role lane --lane-id --lane-plan`, do the code work there, harvest the deferred-mutation queue, replay it at the host root with `speckiwi orchestrate replay apply`, and release the workspace only after the harvest. Each section SHALL state the target under which it applies, so it does not contradict a phase-1 statement that the skill creates no lane workspace. Neither section SHALL restate the shared contract's reasoning; it SHALL point at `_shared/kiwi/worktree-lane.md` for the why and carry only the ordered steps.

#### Rationale

Declaring the contract in §0 gave the skills a pointer and no procedure: an agent reading either skill top to bottom finds nothing it can run, and the one section that mentions worktrees at all — wave-master §2.1 — refuses a delegation and hands the operator two manual recovery paths. A contract with no caller in the body is the documentation form of the kernel with no caller in `src/`, and this repository has now met that shape twice in one target. The steps must name real commands because the failure the shared contract was written to prevent was a command sequence that looked right and detached HEAD; prose that stops short of the command cannot be checked against the tool at all.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Each skill carries a numbered worktree section, in every shipped rendering.
- [x] AC-2: The section names `git worktree add` with the base commit as an argument on the same command, and does not instruct a separate checkout of that commit.
- [x] AC-3: The section names `orchestrate preflight` together with `--role lane`, so the placement is admitted by the gate rather than assumed.
- [x] AC-4: The section names `orchestrate replay apply` as the step that lands the lane's deferred mutations at the host root.
- [x] AC-5: The section states that release happens only after the harvest, and names both in that order.
- [x] AC-6: The section names the target under which the procedure applies, so it cannot be read as a claim about a phase that creates no lane workspace.
- [x] AC-7: The section points at the shared contract rather than restating its reasoning.

Three mutations on the claude orchestrator each turn a different criterion red: --role lane to --role host, replay apply to replay plan, and removing the target name. Landing the section turned two pre-existing suites red and both were satisfied by correcting the section rather than the checker: the 05 §10.3 ledger flagged an unanchored modal carrier, and FR-NODE-128 AC-3 rejected a line-continuation backslash, a bare enum value where the command has no positional, and a placeholder outside the declared thirteen. Full suite 388 files / 5269 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed / 0 timeouts; typecheck:test and lint exit 0. |
